- **Identity is the hash of the weights (decision 5).** `local/<name>@<12 hex of the sha256>`; the person types `local/<name>`. A name alone would let a replaced file keep its evidence. The file is hashed once per event and again on `--resume` (about 2.2 GiB/s measured on a 2 GiB file that had just been written; a cold disk is slower); its (device, inode, size, modification time) is checked before every server start and at the end, and a change fails the run as infrastructure and makes the event a trial. The GGUF header gives the file type (named by the closed table of llama.cpp's `llama_ftype`, tag b11277) and the trained context, which the declared context may not exceed.
- **The configuration is the person's, outside the repository (decision 7).** `~/.config/openhora/local-models.json`, strict. `weights`, `context` and `memory_gb` are required and never guessed (principle 5). `parallel` caps `--jobs` for the event (principle 7: one machine's CPU and memory is the shared resource), and `timeout_seconds` is the configured limit that `scratch_reason` compares against.
- **Usage is the model's own report (decision 10).** No key proxy and no meter, so `usage` comes from the stream. Nothing is billed, so a forged count moves no money, and a host-side meter would cost a second container per run for a figure with no price (principle 13). `runtime/prices.json` is not touched, so `auto` never picks a local model, and the lab facade refuses a `local/` id with one sentence.
- **Readiness uses the image's health check, with a shorter interval and a start period (found in review).** The image's check is `curl -f /health`, which fails with 503 while the weights load, and runs every 30 s by default. The server is started with `--health-interval 2s` so that a ready server is seen within seconds, and with `--health-start-period` equal to the load limit (`load_timeout_seconds`): without it docker's defaults (no start period, 3 retries) make the container `unhealthy` after about 6 s, before any real model has loaded (shown with a stand-in that answers 503 for 14 s). Failures in the start period do not count and the status stays `starting`; `hold_local` waits for `healthy` and fails on `unhealthy` or at the limit. These two flags are the ones beyond the spec's list. The server's threads are capped at 128 so that they stay inside its 256-process limit. Everything else in the server's arguments is the spec's: read-only root, a small temporary folder, no capability, no new privilege, memory equal to its swap limit, the weights as the only mount (read-only), no published port, no added host, no environment variable, one network. All flags were checked against the pinned build's `--help`.
- **An id that was free is not any more.** `local/<anything>` meant "a model the gate file does not list" in the runner's own tests (`local/x`); they use `own/x` now. A local model is tested alone, through `agents-dir`, with no web case (backlog N14a).
- **OpenCode needed no download.** A run on an internal network with no route out, in a throwaway home with no package cache, called the server and answered; the SDK is in the binary. `evals/container/` is unchanged.
- **Not done here.** No real local model's result was produced (only a 1.2 MB test model and a stand-in were run); the results on the skills are the person's tests.

## 2026-10-16: measurement change, infrastructure

N14: a local model runs in a model-server container beside its run, on the run's internal network; hosted runs are byte-identical (golden argv tests)

- Kind: infrastructure: locks, resumption, retries, pacing or reports changed in a file the fingerprint covers. No version is raised; the new fingerprint is committed with the reason.
- Fingerprint: `c5b4ea6a11807931a93428835e3b8276793b5ef5519e74320980972ad800f23f`.
- Written by `python3 evals/eval_status.py measurement --kind infrastructure`.
- Proof that it is infrastructure: the fingerprinted files changed are `evals/executor.py` (a new section for a local model's server, a `local` argument of `command()`, `run_names` gaining a key) and `adapters/agents-dir/run-prompt.sh` (a new branch for `local/*`, and its help text); the `ollama/*` and `openrouter/*` branches are untouched, and `evals/container/` is untouched. `evals/tests/test_executor_local_golden.py` runs the base commit's `executor.py` and `run-prompt.sh` (read from the repository's history, commit `aa31cfca`) beside the new ones: the docker arguments of `command()` and the call sequence and environments of `hold()` and `release()` are equal for every model of the gate file on the networks none, proxy and open, held and not held, and `run-prompt.sh` leaves the same record (arguments, variables, written `opencode.json`, reply, timing) for `openrouter/*` with and without the key proxy's base URL and for `ollama/*`. The only runs that are new are a local model's, which have no evidence to age.

## 2026-10-16: measurement change, execution (measurement version 9 to 10)

Pre-battery: the claude runner 2.1.284 knows the reference model, PDF tools in the image, the adapters report every tool that reads a skill's SKILL.md (skills_loaded) or touches its folder (skills_used) (R12), a loop of raw tool-call syntax is an early end even when a file was written (T27), and staging leaves a dangling link out of a skill folder it copies (it raised on Python 3.9)

- Kind: execution side: the image, an adapter, the runner's prompt or tools, the executor, the staging, or a hosted model under its id changed. The measurement version is raised and an epoch is entered: for the skills and models it reaches, earlier lab lines are inherited evidence and the baselines expire.
- Epoch 2026-10-16: skills all; models all.
- Fingerprint when the version was opened: `c5b4ea6a11807931a93428835e3b8276793b5ef5519e74320980972ad800f23f`, which is measurement version 9's own (N14 left it unchanged); the pre-battery package's `--close` writes the final one.
- Written by `python3 evals/eval_status.py measurement --kind execution`.
- The version stays open (no `measurement_sha256`) until the package's last commit closes it with `--close`, because the pre-commit hook compares the fingerprint of every commit with the committed one and the package changes fingerprinted files in several commits; nothing is measured in between. The order is the one of the P2b precedent (entry of 2026-10-15 above).
- Cost: none beyond the epoch of 2026-10-15. Every committed lab line is at measurement version 5, 6 or 8 and already inherited by that epoch, and every baseline already expired; the battery was already planned as a full test of every skill with its baselines on every lab model. The published image (`evals/image-lock.json`, P2d) must be published for the new definition before the battery.

## 2026-10-16: what the pre-battery measurement change holds and leaves out (T23, T27, T39, R12), and the thin-baseline tie

Package MEASURE-PREBATTERY, decided by the maintainer on 2026-10-15 (done before the battery, in one execution-side epoch) and on 2026-10-16 (the open questions, by recommendation): the epoch is measurement version 10, entered above.

- **Classification (T23).** Execution side, one epoch for all of it: the claude runner, the image's tools, the adapters' reports (R12), the early-end rule (T27: it decides which attempts are made again, like a retry; what the grader is shown and the scoring are unchanged) and the staging copy of a skill folder (`scripts/stage_skills.py`). The claude runner upgrade also changes the CLI the grader runs in (`--no-tools`); the grader model, the grading template and the rules are unchanged, so it is execution side and not grader side (grader side would have raised the floor and dropped every inherited line). The check is the regrade sample after the battery (decision N4), which measures the grader's agreement anyway.
- **The claude runner.** The release is the oldest one published at least 14 days before the change that carries `claude-sonnet-5-5` in its model table: 2.1.284 (published 2026-09-28T17:11:59Z, https://registry.npmjs.org/@anthropic-ai/claude-code/-/claude-code-2.1.284.tgz). 2.1.283 has no such string in its binary (0 occurrences) and 2.1.284 has 24; 2.1.285 to 2.1.287 are also old enough and were not needed. The package and the lock were written by `npm install --save-exact --package-lock-only`; opencode stays 1.18.32. The flags and variables the two scripts use, the account-limit and refusal texts and the side-call model names (`claude-haiku-4-5`, `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`) are all present in the new binary by a static search; `upstream_models` and `runtime/prices.json` need no change.
- **Left out, with the reason.** One image per runner (execution side): it would change the image identity an evidence line records (one `image_digest` per event), the image lock and the publication job (`.github/workflows/eval-image.yml`), for the size of the image only: dropped. Workaround 2, the floor adapter reading the prompt on standard input: not proven (it needs the stand-in recording in the container: the user message the upstream receives equal to the prompt file byte for byte, for a short prompt and one of 300 KiB), so the argument stays and the item is dropped with that reason; the runtime's bounded chat memory stays, being the router's measured form. Grading-rule improvements (grader side): none is decided. Convention sweeps (T19), workarounds 4 to 8 and the rename stages: changes of skills, cases, runtime or installers, none in the fingerprint, each its own package. The first attested battery on GitHub: the battery itself (P2d). Workaround 1 (dependencies outside the image): the install step is the design. Workaround 3 and the `modelUsage` recomputation: the key proxy meters every request and the runtime prices from `meter.json`; the recomputation stays by design.
- **T39 corrected.** `evals/execution.py` is not in the measurement fingerprint (`FINGERPRINT_FILES` of `evals/eval_status.py` lists the grading template, `measure.py`, `measurement.json`, `executor.py`, `stage_skills.py`, `evals/container/` and each eval adapter's `run-prompt.sh` and `eval.json`); the backlog's premise that the fix needed a measurement change was wrong. It landed in this package for convenience, with no measurement kind of its own.
- **R12 stays open.** The adapters report both lists, which is right under either outcome of the finding (the follow-up run ran the skill's script without reading its text, or touched nothing of the folder). No paid diagnostic run (about 0.2 to 0.6 USD) was made; the entry closes with the first recorded follow-up run that shows the lists, or with the stage-1 streams fed through the new script if they turn up.
- **A band rule changed: the thin-baseline tie.** `evals/eval_status.py` compared `with - baseline < margin` with no slack, so Python 3.11 and 3.12 could give different bands for the same evidence (a baseline of six case means at 0.8 against 1.0 with a margin of 0.2: 0.20000000000000007 on 3.11, 0.19999999999999984 on 3.12). It now compares against `margin - FLOAT_SLACK`, the slack the gate uses, so a distance of exactly the margin is not thin, in decimals or in floats. It is the rule that turns scores into bands (principle 12), not a measurement file: no fingerprint, no version, no epoch; recomputed from the existing evidence (`status` before and after the change: no band or cause of any skill on any model differs, because no skill has a thin baseline today). Decided by the maintainer's supervisor while the maintainer was away, by consistency with the gate's slack; revertible. Tests on Python 3.11 and 3.12 (`evals/tests/test_baseline_runs.py`).
- **Python 3.9 staging.** `scripts/stage_skills.py` is fingerprinted. `shutil.copytree(..., ignore_dangling_symlinks=True)` still raised `shutil.Error` for a dangling link on Python 3.9, so the test `test_a_link_inside_a_skill_is_copied_as_its_content_and_never_points_back` failed there (on the base too); a dangling link is now left out by the staging's own ignore function, which every version honours (same result: the link is not copied). The test joined CI's python39 job. It joined this package's epoch for being fingerprinted.

## 2026-10-16: the language artifacts are written in is one setting of the project, and every artifact records its own (LANGUAGE, skills part)

Maintainer's decisions of 2026-10-16 (specification `SPEC-LANGUAGE`, two rounds of answers). This entry is the skills part, landed before the battery so that each skill's full test covers it (class Y for 33 skills, class X for `eng-impact-analysis`, whose Stop rule 2 holds the plan header the line was added to; every skill is `needs a test` anyway, so no extra run); the runtime part follows in its own package.

- **One source.** The key `language` of the project's `docs/workbench/runtime.json` says in which language artifacts are written (the files under `docs/<area>/` and `docs/workbench/`, a state entry included; its keys stay English). Conversation and reading follow the context: a reply is in the language the person writes in, a document is read as it is. Absent: English. Rejected: `artifact_language` (the maintainer's word is `language`), `locale` (it implies formats; dates stay `date +%F`), a second key for posts (the strategy's rotation owns them), and any reading of `AGENTS.md` or of a skill's default as a source.
- **The tag.** `^[a-z]{2}(-[A-Z]{2})?$`: `pt-BR`, `en`, `en-US`. No script subtag and no numeric region (`es-419`): a tag with digits could meet the number rules of the lint scripts. A tag in the wrong case is refused with the right form, never rewritten.
- **`AGENTS.md` holds no value.** Line 8 of the workbench section is a pointer to the key (with the task's language line and the keep rule); an existing project gets it when `core-project-init` next updates the section. Six skills that read the language from `AGENTS.md` or from the strategy (`biz-icp-positioning`, `biz-market-analysis`, `brand-profile`, `brand-strategy`, `brand-voice`, `mkt-content-plan`) now read the project language (the task's `Project language:` or `Language of this deliverable:` line, else the key, else English); `brand-profile` and `brand-strategy` no longer ask the artifact language, only the post languages.
- **`AGENTS.md` leaves those six skills' inputs, and so their runtime run copies.** Limit L5 lets `AGENTS.md` into a run copy only for a skill that lists it in `inputs`; those six read it for nothing but the language, so a run of theirs no longer holds it. In an interactive session they read the key from `docs/workbench/runtime.json`; in a runtime run the task's language line (a later package) gives the value, and English when it is absent.
- **An artifact keeps its language.** Each of 30 output templates gains one header line, `Language: <tag>`, whose placeholder says: on an update, the tag on its `Language:` line (with none, the language the file is written in) unless the request sets another; for a new file, the language the request sets for this deliverable, else the task's language line, else the key, else English (an explicit request for one deliverable applies to that deliverable, new or updated). An artifact written before this change has no line, so it is never switched to the project language by an update. The two plan headers that other skills create first (`eng-impact-analysis`, `eng-refactor`) carry the line too. The model never switches an artifact's language from the conversation alone. Not given the line: a post file and a reply (their language is the calendar slot's or the comment's, already on the slot line), the state file and `AGENTS.md`, JSON and log outputs, and files that are the work itself.
- **What stays English:** the word `Language:`, file names and paths, frontmatter, state keys, the headings and labels a script reads unless the skill says to translate them, ids, ISO dates, commands, post language codes and evidence. `brand-guidelines` passes the translated heading of its examples table to `check_guide.py` (`--examples-heading`), because the check passed vacuously on a guide that is not in English.
- **The router.** `core-orchestrator`'s block gains one optional line, `Language: <tag>`, written only when the request's words ask for the deliverable in a named language other than the project language, never from the language the request is written in. Code reads it in the runtime package; the person sees it on the plan before approving.
- **Parsers.** No script changed: each header-reading lint or check has a test proving that the line leaves its verdict and findings as they were (`test_language_header_*.py` beside the script).
- **Eval impact.** The measurement fingerprint is untouched; no eval case changed. Lab runs never get the runtime's language line, so an artifact written in a lab run says `Language: en` by its template, which no case asserts against. A language case is not added (the authoring guide: a language assertion measures nothing). Field evidence does not record the language (backlog item L1).
- **The key exists now, and is only checked.** Without it, writing `language` in `runtime.json` would make the loader refuse the file (closed keys). `runtime/project_config.py` accepts it (`TASK_RUNTIME_KEYS`) and checks the tag; a wrong case or an underscore is refused with the right form, and accept-config takes it like every key. Nothing reads the value yet.
- **Not here:** the runtime's code (`runtime/language.py`, the per-request option, the plan card line, the router's reading of the line, the migration) is a later package of the same specification.

## 2026-10-16: the entity is called "flow" everywhere, the group `workflows` is renamed with no alias, routine content version 2 (FLOW-WORD)

Pull request: https://github.com/chrissgon/openhora/pull/433.

- **The word.** The maintainer decided on 2026-10-16 that a flow is a "flow" in code, files, operations and every text a person or a model reads. The "workflow" wording that the packages P6a, P6b and the documentation refresh of 2026-10-15 had put into pages, help, the glossary, the contract of the runtime and the interface is replaced; the entries of this log, the plan and architecture documents of 2026-10-14 and the recorded run endings stay as written (history). The word stays only for GitHub Actions' workflows (`.github/workflows`, a reusable workflow, `workflow_dispatch`, the evidence manifest key `workflow` and `--workflow-ref`). `runtime/tests/test_flow_word.py` scans the surfaces a person reads and fails naming the line; its list of allowed lines carries a reason for each.
- **The group of the channel policy `workflows` is now `flows`, with no alias.** It existed only on the integration branch since P6a, `main` has no such group, so no released project can hold it; `project_config` already refuses an unknown group with a sentence that lists the known ones, so a stale `"workflows"` in a project's `runtime.json` fails loudly and never widens or narrows a channel silently. An alias would cost a code path, a test and a removal later (principle 13: the saving is clear, the safety lost is none).
- **The routine content key `workflow` is now `flow`, and `routines.CONTENT_VERSION` is 2.** A routine approved under version 1 (a trial on the integration branch) no longer matches its hash, so at its next slot it stops and asks, naming "the format of the approved content (version 1 to 2)" first; it never fires on an unverified hash (D35). The store column was already `flow`: no migration.
- **Skills:** none edited. The few skill lines that used the word for the entity were already handled by SKILLFIX (#424) and FLOWS1 (#426); the skill lines that remain use it for GitHub Actions or for a user's words.
- **What changed no band:** no skill, no evidence and no fingerprinted file changed.

## 2026-10-16: macro-enabled Office packages are refused for every source (ATT-5)

Pull request: https://github.com/chrissgon/openhora/pull/436.

- **Decision.** The converter refuses a Word document, a spreadsheet or a presentation that carries macros, whoever sent it (a file the person adds, one that arrives from a messaging app, a Drive file). Open question 4 of the attachments specification, answered with its recommendation: every source, not only attachments. The converter accepts only Office Open XML packages (`sources.SUBTYPES` has no old binary Word, Excel or PowerPoint format; a binary file named as a package fails `check_package`), so the VBA streams of an OLE `.doc` or `.xls` are not reachable and need no check.
- **By content, not by name.** The input is mounted as `/in/input` and a sender chooses a file's name, so the extension (`.docm`, `.xlsm`, `.pptm`, `.dotm`, `.xltm`, `.potm`) cannot decide. `refuse_macros` in `providers/converter/_inner.py` looks at the package's part names and content types: a `vbaProject.bin`, `vbaProjectSignature.bin` or `vbaData.xml` part, a part under `xl/macrosheets/`, or a content type (any `ContentType` attribute) or a relationship type (any `Type` attribute of a `.rels` part) containing `macroEnabled`, `vbaProject`, `vbaData` or `macrosheet`. It runs first in `convert` for every Office type, after the zip-bomb guard and before any tool reads the file. Hardened after an independent review: the XML is read by expat so that character references are decoded and a document type declaration or entity is refused unexpanded; part names are compared as a reader would read them (backslash, percent escapes, leading slash, case) and a package with two parts of one name after that, or with no `[Content_Types].xml`, is refused; the XML read is capped (2 MiB a part, 32 MiB in all) and a damaged stream is a closed failure. `xl/dialogs/` was dropped: dialog sheets live in `xl/dialogsheets/` and hold no code. Left out on purpose: ActiveX controls, OLE objects, `customUI` callbacks and a remote `attachedTemplate` (the container has no network and runs nothing).
- **The refusal.** One fixed sentence, `MACRO_REFUSAL`, as the converter's failure (standard error, exit 3, nothing on standard output); the host and `runtime/sources.py` record it as they record the converter's other refusals. No new host code, no new reason word.
- **Rejected.** Refusing by extension (the name is the sender's); opening the VBA project to look for dangerous calls (a parser of a binary format for no gain: no tool of the image runs VBA, but a file that holds code is not a source); opening an embedded macro-enabled file inside a plain package (only the outer package is judged, stated in the README); a new `runtime.json` key (nothing to choose).
- **Needs the maintainer's step.** `_inner.py` is in the image's definition hash, so the image must be rebuilt: `python3 providers/converter/container.py build --confirmed`. `evals/container/` is the only fingerprinted container folder; `providers/converter/` is not, so no measurement change, no skill change, no bump, no lab run.
- **What changed no band:** no skill, no evidence and no fingerprinted file changed.
## 2026-10-16: e-mail: sending is a step effect, approved by the hash of the whole message (EMAIL-SEND)

Pull request: https://github.com/chrissgon/openhora/pull/438.

Decided by recommendation while the maintainer was away; each default below may be revised.

- **Sending an e-mail is a step effect, the second user of the STEP path.** The effect word `send` (declared in the vocabulary since the first environment contract, with no skill that used it) has a kind, `runtime/effect_send.py`, a step kind `send-email` that prepares and a gate that opens the decision, and the flow `send-email`. Nothing in the engine or in the step kind sends: the only function that does is `effect_send.execute`, reached from the operation `approve`. The hash the person approves covers the sender, every recipient (the Bcc too), the subject, the text, each attachment by name and hash, the key and the hash of what the provider's dry run printed, so an approval of one e-mail covers no other recipient (limit L42).
- **The approval names no platform.** `runtime/ops_step_effects.py` had the documents platform written into it (the configuration, the provider, the bounds, the call, the key). Those lines moved into each STEP kind as three names, `preflight(cfg, doc, root)`, `caller(cfg, root)` and `idempotency_key(doc, default)`, plus the optional `is_long`, `FORGETS_KEYS`, `NOTHING_DONE` and, on an error, the words `answer` and `next`. The write-back and the outputs behave as before and their tests are unchanged (`OUTPUT_VERBS` and `OUTPUT_TIMEOUT` moved to `effect_write.py` and are still readable from `ops_step_effects`). `preflight` takes the document, which the spec's `preflight(cfg)` could not: the check that the outbox names the document's sender needs it. A test holds that the file imports none of `documents`, `project_config` or an effect module.
- **IMAP and SMTP with an app password, through the standard library.** Any mailbox with a password works; the Gmail API (OAuth, `gmail.send` on a token of its own, so the reader stays read-only) is a later second implementation of the same class, needed by a host that forbids password logins. The provider is `providers/mailer/smtp.py` with the shared `providers/_email.py`.
- **A dedicated outbox account, one per machine, no recipient allowlist, a hard cap of 20 recipients in all, the Bcc shown on the card.** The person approves the exact recipients of each e-mail by its hash; the cap is code in the provider and in the runtime, equal by a test. The `outbox` key of `runtime.json` is `{"provider", "from"}`, accepted with `accept-config`, and holds no secret.
- **No standing approval for `send`.** `POLICY` is false and the kind is a STEP kind, so no bounds file can name it. Until the policy group `approve-effect-send` (POL-1) is granted, an e-mail is approved in the terminal or on the page only (`operations.EFFECT_CHANNELS`); the seam POL-1 grants is the name `effect_send.APPROVAL_GROUP`. An e-mail with an attachment is never whole on a messaging card (`effect_send.on_cards`), because the card cannot carry the file the hash covers.
- **A new exit code, 4, for an outcome that is not known.** The provider's exit 1 guarantees that nothing was sent, so the runtime can tell a refusal (the decision stays open and approving again tries again) from a lost answer (`unknown`: the decision stays open and approving again meets the provider's ledger, which looks in the Sent folder first). A key the person settles by hand is `resolve`.
- **Review fixes (2026-10-16).** A mail library decodes an encoded word in a subject or an address, and on some Python versions a decoded line break went onto the wire: `=?` is refused in the subject, the addresses and the reply id, and the provider reads the built message back and requires it to be exactly the approved one; a lone `\r` is written `\n`, a non-ASCII domain needs its `xn--` form, every send is `is_long` (the decision's lock, not the write lock), the limit is L42 (L41 is the cancel's, #427), and the wording of the unknown outcome says that `--not-sent` can lead to a second copy on a host that files nothing in the Sent folder.
- **Flow parameters cannot be blank, so the word `none` is no one.** The flow's `cc` defaults to `none`.
- **Not in this package:** the e-mail channel (reading the mailbox), publishing a post on the same path, and starting an e-mail from a phone (the router's change). They are in `docs/backlog.md`.
- **What changed no band:** no skill, no evidence and no fingerprinted file changed. Nothing was run against a real mail service; the live checks are in `providers/mailer/README.md`.

## 2026-10-16: tests never touch the real secret store (KEYCHAIN-ISOLATION)

Pull request: https://github.com/chrissgon/openhora/pull/440.

- **The problem.** On the maintainer's Mac, executors and reviewers running the suite kept being asked "python3.12 wants to access key 'openhora' in your keychain". The resolver reads the OS secret store through the `keyring` package when it is installed (`uv run --with keyring`), and only two places forced the null backend: `runtime/tests/conftest.py` and the loopback guard of `providers/loopback_only.py`.
- **What reached the store (found with a test-only keyring backend that records and refuses every access, on all test folders).** `scripts/tests/test_adapter_secrets.py` started `scripts/doctor.py --json` with a minimal env (`PATH`, `HOME`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`), so the doctor looked up every registered secret under the services `openhora` and `ai-workbench` and ran `uv run <provider> --check` for each class; `providers/secrets/tests/test_resolver.py` ran `resolver.py --list`, `--list --json`, `--registry ... --list` and `--check <name>` the same way. 345 of 723 `uv run` calls of the suite carried no backend variable at all (mostly the vote and publisher tests of `scripts/tests/` and `runtime/tests/`, which held fake tokens in their env and so asked nothing). The in-process conftests of the provider folders could not help: a child with its own env does not inherit the parent's variable.
- **The rule and its home.** One `conftest.py` at the repository root, made the rootdir by a `pytest.ini` (without an ini-file pytest loads no conftest above the folders named on the command line, so `pytest providers/secrets/tests` would have run without it). It sets `PYTHON_KEYRING_BACKEND=keyring.backends.null.Keyring` for the process when imported and again before each test, gives a keyring library already loaded its null backend, and adds the variable to the `env` of any child started through `subprocess.Popen` that does not name one (a copy, never the caller's dict). `scripts/tests/test_test_isolation.py` fails when a test folder starts without the root conftest (it starts pytest on each folder and reads the header), when a settings file below the root moves the rootdir, and when a child with a minimal env does not get the variable.
- **Production code is not changed.** The variable is not added to `contained_env`, the isolated runner or the password-manager call: a net at `Popen` in the test run covers every env a test builds, including the ones production helpers build, and nothing outside a test sets it. Two tests that assert the exact env of such a child (`runtime/tests/test_isolated.py`, `providers/secrets/tests/test_password_manager.py`) name the variable as the one extra name.
- **Result.** A run of every test folder with the recording backend: before, 192 recorded accesses to the store (each name asked under both services), all from the children of two test files; after, none, with every `uv run` of the suite carrying the null backend.
- **Rejected.** A per-folder conftest in each of the 60 folders (60 files to keep, and a new folder has none until someone remembers); editing only the helpers that were found (the next helper with a minimal env would reopen it); a guard that scans test sources for `env=` (it cannot tell a minimal env from a full one).
- **What changed no band:** no skill, no evidence and no fingerprinted file changed.

## 2026-10-16: publishing a post now is a step effect, on the word that already had the reply (PUBLISH-EFFECT)

Pull request: https://github.com/chrissgon/openhora/pull/439.

Decided by recommendation while the maintainer was away; each default below may be revised.

- **A post is published through the approval path, on the existing word `publish`.** The word had one module, `runtime/effect_reply.py` (the reply to a comment under a standing approval), and the registry maps a word to one module, so the row is now `runtime/effect_publish.py`, a facade: the names of `effect_reply.py` are re-exported as they are (`effect_reply.py` is not edited), the step path's names are added, and `describe` answers for both documents (a post's has the key `operation`). `GATE` stays false, so a skill that declares `publish` still opens a review at its gate. A step kind `publish-post` prepares, a gate `publish` opens the decision, the flow `publish-post` starts it, and the only function that publishes is `effect_publish.execute`, reached from the operation `approve` (limit L43).
- **A consequence found while building.** `ops._bounds_words` dropped every STEP kind from the words a standing approval's bounds may name, which would have removed `publish` from the bounds of the reply. The rule is now "STEP and not POLICY" (`effects.step_only_words`): `send` and `write` stay out, `publish` stays in, and no bound can name a post because a post is approved by its own hash. It is one line of `ops.py`, net zero. The registry assertion of `test_effects.py` and of `test_effect_reply.py` changed with the registry row; the rest of the reply's tests did not.
- **The post is what the publishing skill's own script builds, and the skill is not edited.** `runtime/publish_payload.py` runs `skills/mkt-publish/scripts/payload.py build` isolated (as the reply runs `mkt-engage`'s gate), with no `--publisher`, in a private folder, and reads its answer; the text, the first comment and the image are the files the skill's scheduled job would hand the publisher. A test pins the fields of the script the runtime reads, so a later change to the script that breaks the runtime fails a test, not a publication. An image must be a plain relative path of the project with no link on the way; a path outside the project is refused (a content file is written by a run, and its image path is not the person's to verify).
- **The key is the skill's, so a post cannot go out twice by either path.** The key is the content file's name without `.md`. The publisher's ledger holds a key for one content and publishes it once, so a post the skill's job already published is refused at the preview (`already_published`, from the dry run's `existing_status`) and replayed at the approval, a job that is scheduled and has not run has written nothing to the ledger (so it is not seen, and is replayed when it runs if this path published first), and a post published here is replayed by the skill's job. A key left pending by an unknown outcome blocks both. After a failed publication the ledger is read by a dry run: nothing recorded is a clean refusal, `pending` or unreadable is `unknown`, `published` is "the post is out, the first comment failed" (approving again retries only the comment).
- **A published key's dry run is not the dry run before it (review of #439).** The real publisher's dry run of a key it holds as published puts the post's id into the first comment's request, raw and percent-encoded, so an exact comparison of the dry run at the approval answered `deviation` for ever: the comment never retried, and a post the skill's job published between the preview and the approval was a `deviation` and not `replayed`. `stable_dry_run` now replaces the id the dry run itself names (`existing_post_urn`, in every form) with a mark, and `same_dry_run` accepts the document's placeholder where the mark is and nothing else; the stand-in publisher carries the id the way the real one does. The effect decision's title says it is published now.
- **The document names the ledger.** Its path is in the hash and on the card; the approval refuses a dry run that prints another, the person's own `PUBLISHER_<PLATFORM>_LEDGER` variables that name another file, and a scheduled job for the key pinned to another file by `--ledger` (the vote job does this from the tick's environment). Only a scheduler that is not configured is read as having no jobs; a failing, slow or odd scheduler is a refusal, and its list is read with the `SCHEDULER_HOME` of the person's environment. The mark of a published key's post id goes only inside string values and only for ids of eight characters or more, and a dry run that is not plain JSON is a usage error, not a crash after the approval's row.
- **One ledger needs one data folder.** The provider's ledger is in `$XDG_DATA_HOME/ai-workbench/` where the variable is set, and the resolver's minimum environment does not pass it. The runtime passes it to the publisher (`publish_payload.PASSES`) and does not add it to the minimum of every provider. A scheduler that runs the skill's job with another data folder has another ledger; that is a live check, not code.
- **Immediate only.** The call carries no `--at` and no `--ledger`; the slot the file names is shown and is part of the hash ("published now, not at <slot>"). Scheduling stays the skill's (PB1). One post per decision, the skill's `action` scope (PB2 for a batch).
- **A blocked post opens no effect; the check line is shown, not enforced.** The skill leaves out a post whose `- Status:` says blocked or whose Checks section does not read `check_post.py: ok`. The runtime refuses the first (an explicit marker) and shows the second on the card, because the post files that other skills write (the vote round's) are not promised to carry that line, and the person reads the exact text anyway.
- **The long-content rule and the seam.** A post with an image is not whole on a messaging card (`effect_publish.on_cards`), and the group `approve-effect-publish` is the name `effect_publish.APPROVAL_GROUP` until POL-1 merges and grants it; the invariant test of POL-1 fails until its group is added. Approval stays the terminal and the page (`operations.EFFECT_CHANNELS`).
- **Not in this package:** scheduling, a batch, and starting a post from a phone (`docs/backlog.md`, PB1 to PB3).
- **What changed no band:** no skill, no evidence and no fingerprinted file changed. Nothing was run against a real platform; the live checks are in `providers/publisher/README.md`.

## 2026-10-16: cancel or stop at any moment, and the board's native status (CANCEL-BOARD: R18, R13)

Package CANCEL-BOARD of the delegation platform plan. The maintainer's decisions of 2026-10-16: R13 as written below, R18 as designed below.

- **Two verbs, both brakes.** `cancel` (now working while a run is in progress) ends a request and everything under it; the new `stop-task` ends one running task. Both are on the terminal, the conversation, the page and messaging, never on the MCP mode (an agent on the MCP is not the person; the refusal of a typed `/stop-task` says so as it does for `/cancel`). Both are in `operations.BRAKES` and in the new group `brakes`. **The default of messaging stays closed** (the maintainer's rule of 2026-10-16, every channel restrictive by default and the person widens it; decided by the supervisor while he was away): `DEFAULT_POLICY` keeps `routine-brakes` and gains nothing, so that cancelling or stopping a task from a messaging app is turned on by listing `brakes` (or `reject-cancel`, for `cancel` alone) in `channels.messaging`, accepted in the terminal; the first version of this package put the group in the default and was changed. `cancel` stays in `reject-cancel` too, the one operation in two groups, so that every policy written before stays valid; the test that walks the table allows it for that operation alone.
- **A stopped task ends `blocked`, not `cancelled`.** `retry` then resumes it (a step task at its step) and its dependents stay `planned`. A `cancelled` task is final: `_refresh` makes a dependent ready only when every dependency is `done`, so a cancelled one would leave the request stuck for ever, with the person unable to say "go on without it". The note says who stopped it.
- **The stop request is written first, and read by every finish.** Migration 15 adds `tasks.stop_requested_at` and `_by` and rebuilds `task_runs` (as migration 14 rebuilt `sources`) so that its status admits `cancelled`. The request is written in one transaction under the project's write lock; a run that returns files into the project holds the same lock from its check of the request until its finish, so the two exclude each other and a stopped run returns nothing, whatever the timing (limit L41). A finish that committed before the request keeps its result and the cancel then cancels its pending decisions, so a review is never applied. The alternative of a check without the lock was refused: it leaves a window in which files come back and the run is recorded cancelled.
- **No effect is interrupted.** The whole call holds the request's effect lock; one that meets an executing effect is refused with nothing written and nothing signalled (L41). The stop sequence: TERM to the holder of the task lock, 30 s (`run_stop.STOP_GRACE`: the lab's handler removes the containers and releases the key proxy, up to about 20 s), KILL to the same holder, then the containers, key proxy and network the process tree named before TERM are removed through the container tool. Descendants are never signalled by pid (a pid read from a listing can be another process by then).
- **The router's watcher.** The router runs inside the calling process, which is never signalled: a thread beside its lab call reads the request's stop request every second and ends the lab's runs of that process, safe because the lab guard makes the router the only lab session there. The canceller waits 30 s (`ROUTER_WAIT`) and, when the route lock is free with the row still `running`, finishes the row itself.
- **Spend.** A `cancelled` run keeps its reservation and counts as a run that may have spent; its tokens and cost are read from its folder as for a failed run. Readers of `task_runs.status` changed: `progress` (a `cancelled` count), the page's run words, and the dispatcher's report (a cancelled run has no failure). `autonomy.never_reached_the_model`, `spend_split`, the caps and `upkeep` needed no change.
- **The page** asks before it stops: the cancel dialog says, while something runs or the router answers, that what the run used so far is spent and nothing it made is kept, and a running task has `Stop task` with a second click that says the same. `Cancel request` is no longer hidden while the router works.
- **R13, the native status.** Opt-in `task_board.native_status` (absent: off) names a status property of the base other than the runtime's own state property, which stays and is the only one read. Mapping, as the maintainer decided: to-do is requested, planned and ready; in progress is running, waiting, blocked and failed; complete is done and cancelled. The option is the configured one for the group, else the first of the group; the groups are read from the base and named To-do, In progress and Complete on the reference (read on 2026-10-16). It is written on creation and when a row's state changes, never read back, so a person's move of it stays until the state changes.
- **After the independent review (2026-10-16).** A signal is sent only to a process shown to be the lock's holder: the lock text now carries the start time of its holder, and before TERM and before KILL the process of that pid must have it and a command line that is a `run-task`, `run-step` or `run-next` of the project; the recovery holds a lock without writing its text, so a dead holder's text, with a pid that now belongs to another process, would otherwise have got a TERM. A board sync pulls with the write lock held, so the cancels a person makes on the board are made after the sync let it go (`ops_cancel.after_sync`), and the board is written again. A step task's own atomic write asks `ctx.alive()` first. R13: the groups of a base in another language are mapped in the configuration (`native_status.groups`), and a native status that does not fit the base never fails the write of the runtime's own state (one warning line, `--check` exits 3). Cancel and stop-task live in `ops_cancel.py`; `ops.py` is back to the size it had.
- **Not verified.** No live Notion was called: the native status was exercised against the stand-in service only, whose groups and options are the reference's. No container was touched: the container tests use a fake tool. **What changed no band:** no skill was edited or bumped, no fingerprinted file changed.

## 2026-10-16: the runtime reads the project's language, and a request may carry its own (LANGUAGE, runtime part)

The runtime part of the specification `SPEC-LANGUAGE` and of the maintainer's decisions of 2026-10-16 (the skills part is the entry above). No skill, no fingerprinted file and no evidence changed; two eval cases of `core-orchestrator` were added (a case-only change: no bump, `pending` until they run in the battery).

- **One order, computed by code.** `runtime/language.py` `effective`: the request's recorded language, else the key `language`, else `en`. Nothing else is read: not the language a message or an answer is written in, not a skill's default. The rule is the one the key already had (`^[a-z]{2}(-[A-Z]{2})?$`, the maintainer's answer to question 3), kept in `language.py` and read by `project_config`, the request option, the router's line, the page and (written again, with a test that holds the two equal) the store's column.
- **The line is always in a task run and in the router's run**, key absent included (then `en`), right after the "For this task:" paragraph and before the standing instructions; a run with no task text gets it after the request. Always, because the rule that an artifact keeps the language its `Language:` line names and the rule that only this line sets the language must reach every run. Never in a contained run, a step task, the first runtime or the lab: `evals/`, `runtime/lab.py`, the steps and the contained run do not name the modules, which a test reads. The prompt-shape tests changed once (five tests).
- **The person's choice is a request option recorded by code.** `request --language <tag>` (terminal, MCP mode), the page's `Language` select beside `Model` and `POST /projects/{p}/requests`. A bad tag is a usage error (code 2) before anything is recorded, with the right form offered ("did you mean pt-BR?"). Migration **16** adds `tasks.language` (NULL: not requested; a `CHECK` with the tag shape); 15 is CANCEL-BOARD's.
- **The plan field, absent when none was requested.** The requested language is the field `language` of each plan task that runs a skill, so the plan hash covers it and another language is another plan; when none was requested the key is absent, so a plan keeps the hash it had before (the same convention as `sources`). A step task has none. The task row takes it from the approved plan, as the model is carried; a task added later (a backlog sub-task, a delivery routed again after its brief) takes the request's, else the approved plan's.
- **The line every plan shows.** `Language: pt-BR` (the project's), `Language: en (requested)`, or the groups when the tasks differ (`Language: pt-BR for tasks 1, 3; en (requested) for task 2`): the third line of the plan's text, so the terminal's preview, the messaging card and the page's "Plan as text" carry it, a line of the conversation's plan reply, and a note on the page's plan card. The payload gains `project_language` (the key, or `en`); the hash still covers the tasks only, so the project's own language is shown and not frozen: a run reads the key when it starts.
- **The router proposes, the person sees it.** An optional `Language: <tag>` line of the router's reply (written only when the request's words name a language, never from the language they are written in), read like `Sources:`: one line that matches the rule, else the reply is unclassified; a request with an explicit choice ignores it. A proposal becomes the language of the plan's tasks and is not written on the request row (the person approves the plan, with its hash, which holds it). Rejected: a typed marker in the message (`lang:en`), which a person on a phone does not know; a silent model choice.
- **The router has a paragraph of its own (review of the pull request).** A task run is told that a request or an answer asking for another language changes nothing, which would contradict step 8 of `core-orchestrator` (write the `Language:` line when the words ask for a named language) and make a weak model drop the proposal. The router's paragraph (`language.router_line`) gives the project's tag, says when to write the line, that the person approves it on the plan and that nothing else sets the language; when the request carries the person's own choice it says so and tells the router to write none, and the reply's `Language:` line is then not read at all (two lines or a bad tag cannot make the reply unclassified). Sub-tasks of a backlog task take that task's own language first, then the request's, then the approved plan's, so two deliveries of different languages keep theirs; the acceptance cards of sub-tasks show the language line like the plan card.
- **An answer never changes it.** An answer, a message or the language a person writes in changes neither a task's language nor the next prompt; a run asked in an answer for another language keeps its line's and says in its reply that the language is set on the request.
- **The page.** The first option of the select is the project's language (the `skills` read carries `language`); a typed tag wins over the list and is checked with the same rule before it is sent (and again by the service). The field is named `wb-new-lang` (a markup test forbids the substring that a code fence's class uses).
- **Eval impact.** Two cases for `core-orchestrator`'s battery (fixture `runtime.json` with `language: pt-BR`): a request that says "in English" yields `Language: en`; one written in Portuguese with no such words yields no `Language:` line. They are content assertions on the routing block. The measurement fingerprint is untouched. Case 14's prompt is Portuguese on purpose (the language a request is written in is what it tests), so it is declared as the exception the validator offers, a path entry of `.security-scan-allow` for `skills/core-orchestrator/evals/evals.json` with its reason, and the Portuguese is written literally, not hidden behind `\u` escapes (review of the pull request).
- **Not here:** a routine's language (backlog L2), the language on a request's line in `status` and on the page (L4), the language in field evidence (L1), translated headings (L3).

## 2026-10-16: measurement version 10 reopened for the review of the pre-battery package

The independent review of the pull request found the early-end rule of T27 too narrow and too wide in places, and R12's detection too wide in others; version 10 was reopened (`measurement_sha256` removed) for the fixes and is closed again by the last commit.

- **The branch was merged with the base** (`feature/delegation-platform` at 27b6b4bb: the language header, the stripper tests, and the rename of workflow to flow): both sides of the conflicts in the limits tables (L33 follows the rename, L39 keeps this package's text) and in this file are kept.
- **T27, the rule widened and narrowed.** Missed, with synthetic replies: write calls whose `content` parameter has ten or more lines (the content lines are not markup, so the share stayed under half); any loop after a fence left open (the fence parity flipped and hid it); markup that starts after prose on the same line. Flagged by mistake on a run that wrote a file: a four-space indented code block, XML of a project's own (`<parameters>`, `<parameterDefinition>`, `<tool_calls>`: the tag prefixes ended anywhere), and a fence of four backticks that holds one of three. Now: a line is markup when it holds a tag of `early_end.markup` or `<parameter` anywhere in it (a name ends where the tag does), or lies inside a call block (from an opening call tag to its closing one: `function_calls`, `invoke`, `tool_call`, `skill_tool`, `system-reminder`); a fence closes only with as many or more of the same character, a fence still open at the end is not one (the lines after it are counted; the rescans are bounded), a fence inside a call block does not open one, indented code and inline code spans are left out; the share is of lines or of characters. The constants (20 and 0.5) are unchanged.
- **T27 is not verified on the recorded collapse.** The reply of `design-ux-flows` case 1 (the floor model, 2026-10-04) is not in the maintainer's local `evals-workspace/` (nor in the other places searched under the home folder). The rule was read, read-only, over the 1,903 replies that folder holds: it flags two, each a real loop of `<system-reminder>` blocks by the floor model (`mkt-publish` and `brand-profile`, without the skill), and no other. Tests hold fictional replies of the same shape. The backlog says "built; not verified on the recorded collapse".
- **The rule reaches the runtime's runs.** `evals/run_attempts.py` classifies an attempt through `lab.early_end`, so a task run of the runtime whose reply is such a loop is now an early end and is made again (up to the retries), a paid retry, where before it was returned if it had written a file. `docs/architecture/platform/runtime.md` says so.
- **R12, the detection narrowed and widened.** The folder ends at `;`, `|`, `)`, `&`, `<`, `>`, `:` and a backtick too. `skills_used` reads only the inputs that name files (a command, a path, a pattern, a glob), never a write's content, an edit's text or a subagent's prompt. `skills_loaded` counts a `Read`, `Grep` or `Bash` input only when it names the `SKILL.md` of one skill: a command or a grep over several skills' `SKILL.md` loads none of them (they are used), so the routing mode does not mark every skill such a grep names as loaded.
- **T39, the gate file.** `model_entry_problems` of `evals/eval_status.py` reads `token_var` from `evals/execution.py` and reports a `pass_env` or `web_pass_env` (the grader's included: it is a model entry) that names a git-host, npm or `OP_` variable, as an error of the gate file and so of the validator, where the runner would have dropped it without a word.
- **Wording.** The opening entry's fingerprint is measurement version 9's own (N14 left it unchanged). The epoch's cause says staging leaves a dangling link out of the folder it copies. `AGENTS.md` says a thin baseline is one closer than `baseline_margin`, the tie rule of the reliability model.

## 2026-10-16: measurement version 10 reopened a second time (the re-check of the pre-battery package)

The re-check found two faults in the collapse rule of T27 (`evals/measure.py`); version 10 was reopened (`measurement_sha256` removed) and is closed again by the last commit.

- **A sentence that mentions a call tag turned every later line into markup.** The block depth opened for any unclosed call tag outside inline code and never closed: "The harness wraps its notes in a <system-reminder> tag before the model sees them.", "Each tool call is printed as <invoke name=...> by the old format.", "Wrap the agent's instructions in <function_calls> only when the API needs it.", each followed by 30 prose lines and a written file, read as 31 of 31 lines and an early end. Now a block opens only when its tag starts the line or follows only other tags, and a block whose closing tag never comes is not one: the scan is repeated without its opener, as it is for a fence left open at the end (bounded; past the bound blocks are off from the last opener on). The lines of a loop that stays open keep counting by their own tags. The three sentences and a fourth (a tag that starts the line and is never closed) are tests that must not be early ends; every loop test is still caught, and the 1,903 local replies give the same two hits.
- **Linear time on a long line.** Each tag match copied the rest of the line, so a 200,000-character single line took about 30 seconds. A tag is now read to its `>` within 200 characters and the gaps between tags are read once; a test reads a 200,000-character line, 25,000 unclosed tags and 20,000 lines of unclosed tags in under a second. A loop written on one line, with no newline, is one line, under the minimum of 20, by design.
- **`<parameter` is matched in lower case only**: the syntax of a call has it so, and another format's `<Parameter name=... value=.../>` (twenty-five unfenced lines of a Tomcat file were flagged) is not a call. The other tags keep matching in any case.
- **R12.** The two-skill under-report (one command or grep that names the `SKILL.md` of two skills loads neither; both are used) is left as it is and written in both adapters' README and header, with the delimiter list of the folder.
- **Backlog T27 is an open box again** (principle 10): built, not verified on the recorded collapse; it closes when the rule is shown on it or on the next collapse the battery records.

## 2026-10-16: follow-ups of the cancel and the stop (CANCEL-FOLLOWUPS: R18)

- **Decision.** The two `ps` calls of a stop (`ops_core._process_start`, `run_stop.processes`) run with `LC_ALL=C`: in a locale such as ja_JP `lstart` has four words, so no holder was ever verified (it failed safe, and a stop could not signal it). The L41 row now names its one exception: a `cancel` whose request runs nothing, while the effect lock is held only by the write of an output, a send or a publication, cancels the rest and keeps that decision open. A cancel during a send and during a publication is tested as it is during an output write. The duplicate import of `ops_cancel` in `ops.py` is removed (the file is one line shorter), and the `is_long` documentation of the send and the publication says a cancel keeps the decision open.
- **Not done.** The `openhora-script-*` containers and networks of the project-script package (pull request #416) are not in the stop's names, because #416 has not merged: backlog item R19.

## 2026-10-16: channel restrictions are policies; D8 becomes the default (POL-1)

The maintainer decided on 2026-10-16 that every channel restriction that is a fixed rule becomes a policy whose default keeps today's behaviour, and that the person may widen it per channel as far as they choose; the seven recommendations of the spec (`SPEC-POLICIES`, section 7) were approved as written. The repository's wording: "A channel restriction is a policy: the default is today's restrictive rule, and the person may widen it in the project's configuration."

- **What it changes, and what it does not.** It changes where an approval can be given, never whether the exact content is approved (principle 11). Before it, the policy mechanism only narrowed: `channels.<channel>` was a subset of the ceiling and `project_config._channels` refused anything wider. Widening is a new, closed literal, `operations.GRANTS`: a group a channel reaches only when the project's own entry for that channel lists it (`operations.granted`; never through `DEFAULT_POLICY`, never through "no entry keeps the ceiling"). `operations.effective_groups(channel, cfg, sender=None)` is the one seam every check reads (the sender is not used yet: a later package gives each listed sender a profile through it).
- **Phase 1, built.** A messaging app may be granted one effect word at a time (`approve-effect-create`, `-write`, `-send`, `-publish`) and networked plans (`approve-networked-plans`, on top of `approve-plans`; the P9 rule of limit L36 becomes a grant); the MCP mode may be granted plans (with their hash), acceptances, reject-cancel and retry, off by default, with a sentence saying that a model then authorises runs. The umbrella `approve-effects` stays the page's alone, so a kind added later is never approvable from a phone until its own group is granted. `INSTRUCTIONS_CHANNELS` and the instructions writers stay the terminal's and the page's (phase 2, R22); the group that covers them is renamed `instructions-write` with no alias, because `instructions` is the read operation of the group `read` and listing it in a policy granted a write (FLOW-WORD fixed the same collision for flows with `flow-write`).
- **What stays code, and why** (R4 to R7, R9, R11 to R15): the approval is bound to the exact hash of the effect document; a card must have carried the whole text it approves (the body uncut, or the whole text as the attached document; a cut card, a `count` notice and a link approve nothing; WhatsApp through Twilio, with 1600 characters and no documents, approves only short effects); a kind says when its whole cannot be on a card (`effect_send.on_cards`: an attachment; `effect_publish.on_cards`: an image), and then no card approves it, the group granted or not, in the same change that adds `approve-effect-send` and `approve-effect-publish`; no typed line approves an effect; `accept-config` and everything that changes a policy stay in the terminal (a policy changed from the channel it widens would make the default mean nothing); the sender allowlist, the brakes and the terminal never narrowed are unchanged; on the MCP mode the effects, the networked plans and consent stay code, because an approval there is a model's and not a person's.
- **Accepting a widening.** `config` shows the grants a file lists, those the last accepted file did not list (`widening`) and `widening_sha256`, the sha256 of that list written one `<channel>:<group>` line each; `accept-config --sha256 <file> --widen <that hash>` accepts a file that adds a grant, and nothing else does. One hash instead of one flag for each group. The hash is a deliberate step and not a proof of reading: it can be computed by whoever can run `accept-config`, so it protects against a file accepted without the list ever being shown, which is what the command does when the flag is missing (it stops and prints the grants and the command that accepts them), and against nothing else. `--widen` covers the groups of `GRANTS` alone. The grants of the accepted file are recorded in the store's cursor `config:grants` (no migration), so `set-mode --accept` and the page's consent, which accept only the file that already was accepted, widen nothing.
- **After the security review of the pull request.** The card of a write that makes a table is a preview, so `effect_write.on_cards` is false for `create_table` and `append_rows` and true for a page; the card's file is sent first and the card with the buttons only after the provider confirmed the file (`Sent.document_failed`), so `whole` is set on a delivery and not on an intention; the pull request's `on_cards` is false (the card shows paths and hashes and not the code), so `approve-effect-create` is kept and approves nothing until a later package shows a diff card (revertible: the grant could instead be dropped); a plan with a networked step needs a whole card and a typed hash that is the card's. The word "grant" is used for `GRANTS` only; groups inside a channel's ceiling are added by listing them and are accepted without `--widen`. A file that lists the group `instructions` is no longer readable until it is edited to `instructions-write`.
- **After the second review.** `whole` is recorded from the delivery and not from the intention: a card that carried its file inline counts only if the provider confirmed it; a typed line approves an effect or a networked plan only if the card's ledger entry says it offered the approval (a plain plan or an acceptance keeps the rule it had before POL-1, the permission read when the person acts); a card whose file failed is recorded `blocked` and not re-sent by a tick (until the content changes; the person's own `/pending` tries it again once per request, and the card says so; the alternative, once a day, was left out as more state for little); a hash typed with `/approve` must be the card's for an acceptance too. The ceiling groups added without `--widen` stay as they are, for POL-2.
- **Cost of the safety.** A person who grants a word writes it once, and types the hash of a list they read once for each new grant; an `accept-config` typed without the flag shows what a file widens and accepts nothing. It is not a barrier against anyone who can run the command, and groups inside a channel's ceiling (`approve-plans`, `release`, `reject-cancel`, `retry`, `brakes`, `routine-edit`) can still be added by an edited `runtime.json` and accepted without `--widen`. No skill, no agent and no fingerprinted file changed: no bump and no lab run.
- **Not done.** Phase 2 (backlog T42): routines and consent cards on messaging, standing approvals on the page, the other rows of R16 to R22. Policies per person (`SPEC-POLICIES`, "Addendum: policies per person") are a later package that fills the seam: nothing here names a sender.
- **Superseded in part (2026-10-17, POL-2).** `--widen` no longer covers `GRANTS` alone: every group a channel or a listed sender gets that the accepted file did not effectively give them is a widening, so the groups inside a channel's ceiling are no longer accepted without it; and the policies per person that this entry left for a later package are built (see the POL-2 entry below).

## 2026-10-17: quick tests and the pooled gate (P11)

The maintainer decided on 2026-10-15, in chat, that a skill's evidence comes in three kinds of test, and that many cheap quick tests from a large community can make a skill reliable. An independent review of the first build found that a pool reading every line could be re-drawn; the maintainer decided on the first-lines rule below.

- **Decision.** A **quick test** (`eval_run.py --skill <name> --quick`) runs every case once with the skill, on one model or more, with no baseline: an event of the kind `quick` (`runs` 1, baseline `none` for every case, no gate), resumable and closable like the others. Alone it moves the score and satisfies the guard assertions it runs, and never moves a band or the gate. The with-skill lines of one skill, one model and one evidence source (lab, local, community: never mixed) that weigh, whose version and content hash are a line of the skill's version file (a draft's lines never count) and that are in the current set, from full, partial and quick tests together, form a **pool**, one for each `X.Y` and era.
- **The gate reads, per case, only the first `runs` (3) lines of the pool in time order**; later lines count for the score and nowhere else. This removes the re-draws a pool that read every line allowed (a targeted partial rerun, a quick top-up, a newest-30 window): a failing pool is cleared by a fix (a new `X.Y`), never by more runs of the same version. Simulated on the real status code (1,500 skills a cell, 8 cases): a pool after nine more quick tests, or six partial reruns of its worst case, passes exactly the skills a fixed test of 3 runs a case passes (15.5%, 38.1% and 54.3% at true means of 0.75, 0.78 and 0.80), where the review's simulation of a pool reading every line gave about 33% against 16%.
- **The pool closes** when every current case that is not pending has 3 counted lines and a baseline in force (the rule that exists, in the pool's era) and, in the community source, when the counted lines come from at least 2 contributors (the attested fork's owner, what the per-contributor cap counts), none holding more than two thirds of them; this holds by construction, because a contributor's lines in one case count up to floor(`runs` x 2/3) (2 of 3) and the rest count for the score alone, so a fork that fills a case first cannot block the others (a re-review found that counting the first 3 lines of each case could block a pool forever). The community cap, 30 lines of one contributor, is applied to the counted lines in time order and the pool reads the files before the newest-30 window of the score column, so the cap cannot push an earlier line out. A closed pool is evaluated by `measure.gate_passes`, unchanged, on the means of the cases' means, and counts as a full test for `done`, for rule (a) of `needs a test` and for the count of Y changes; a failing one is rule (c). It is **frozen like a full test's gate**: a case added after it closed is pending; a case changed after it closed, once, is pending with the band `watch`, and changed again makes it unable to be computed (`needs a test`); an epoch leaves its result standing with the note `pooled, baseline expired` and the band `watch`. The cause and the gate say `pooled`; an open pool shows its progress and the dates of its lines. A baseline is the model's own: the lab's first for a community pool (the lab's is the most trusted), the source's own first for a lab or local pool, each falling back to the other. The route is on only for a model whose evidence of the source holds a quick line that weighs, so a skill tested by full and partial tests alone is gated as before: `eval_status.py status` on the committed evidence is byte-identical before and after. The community workflow gains `quick` (exclusive with `cases` and `full`), and `scripts/verify_community.py` accepts quick files unchanged.
- **Version-file filter.** It also keeps a draft full test out of a full test's gate (`gate_of`; the status on the committed evidence is byte-identical), and the local figures say how many of a person's lines it set aside (`unnamed_lines`, with the cause `N lines of content the version file does not name: bump, or run on the committed content`).
- **Adding a skill** iterates with `--quick --scratch` trials (no evidence, no blocked event), then, with the content final, runs the quick test that writes evidence and the first full test; the same is recommended, not required, for an X change.
- **Autonomy is unchanged** (D34, D36). A pair that is reliable only through community evidence, pooled or not, is ranked and used with its output reviewed (`community_only`); unattended release still needs the lab's or the person's own evidence.
- **Saved** (principle 13): the cost of a full test spread over several people and over time (about a third of the with-skill runs per quick test: US$ 0.91 against US$ 3.10 on the reference model, the figures stated when the package was specified, not measured again), the baseline not run again, and repeated full tests of a skill that is still wrong. **Given up**: nothing of the gate: the run count (3 a case), the rule and the means are a full test's, and the lines that decide are fixed by time. The costs are stated in the reliability model, section 2: later runs cannot rescue a pool (the strict side), lines of different machines meet in one mean (answered by the attested, digest-pinned image), and a pool of a changed case set is judged on the cases that were run, shown as `pending` and `dropped`, as for a full test.
- **Not changed.** No fingerprinted file, no committed evidence, no measurement version. While there, the community workflow no longer passes `--floor-model ""`, an option the runner lost in package P3 (it would have failed on its first run); `--model` once names the one model of the file.

## 2026-10-17: a skill from outside: brought, checked, vetted and tested on the person's machine (IMPORT-CLI)

The maintainer's vision (extra 1) asks for an environment to test and validate skills, useful when a person wants to use a skill from outside. The spec of the package (SPEC-IMPORT.md of the delegation plan) left eight open questions, each with a recommendation, and the maintainer decided them by those recommendations: a user-level home; an audit before any test; the person runs the vetting; the vetting record is copied out of the checkout with a warning; no runtime use; the page is read-only later; no archives or registries; no helper for writing cases. This entry records what was built under those decisions.

- **Decision.** `python3 scripts/skill_import.py` (standard library, Python 3.9) brings a skill that is not in `skills/` into a home folder outside the checkout (`<XDG data home>/openhora/skills/<name>/`, `WB_IMPORT_HOME` or `--home` another; refused inside the checkout by device and inode, and under any folder an adapter's `skills_dir` names, read from `adapters/*/eval.json`), in verbs whose state is computed from the folder and `evals/import/`: `fetch` (a local path or an https repository URL into a quarantine folder; links, special files, hard links and more than 2,000 files or 20 MB refused; a download waits for an explicit yes and runs shallow, with no hook, submodule, tag, checkout or credential helper, in a throwaway environment; the listing and the pack are read before the checkout, so the caps apply before anything is written, though not before the shallow pack is downloaded), `normalize` (by shape: an Agent Skills folder or one Markdown file; the body byte for byte, the frontmatter rewritten; foreign keys in the record; `requires` and `side_effects` must be answered; a flow, an agent file and a settings file stop the import), `check` (the scan, the component list, `validate.py --foreign` and the preflight, in parallel; the effect stop), `audit --record`, `cases-template`, `test`, `status`, `refresh` and `bump`. The runner takes `--skill-dir <folder>`; staging does not change.
- **Evidence.** Always local, under `<local evidence base>/imported/<name>/`, with `"imported": true` on the event line (a closed key); the validator refuses a committed file that carries it. A quick test alone is preliminary and never moves a band (`status --skill-dir` says `preliminary: quick test only`); every figure says it counts on the person's machine only and is measured against cases nobody else has read (principle 10). The battery and the community workflow read `skills/` only, so the lab pays nothing.
- **Hard limits are code** (principle 11). The runner refuses a real event of a folder that holds a link or a special file, has no audit record, has the verdict `do not install`, has changed since the audit, has a web case without `--allow-web-cases` and the verdict `install`, or comes with `--pass-env` or `--harness`. The import tool executes no file of the skill (its scripts and their tests included). The vetting is `core-security-audit` in vetting mode, run by the person in their tool; the tool reads the verdict from the record and ties it to the content hash.
- **Four things built beyond the spec's eight verbs, each needed by a step the spec states.** (1) `bump`: the effect stop asks the person to edit SKILL.md (declare the effect, write the `## Confirmation gate` section), and the body is never rewritten by the tool; the edit changes the content hash, so a verb records it (class by `eval_status.change_class`, a line appended to `evals/versions.jsonl`) and the version file keeps naming the content, which the filter of the pooled gate needs. (2) `refresh` stops with the diff and its class and waits for `--apply`, because applying voids an audit and replaces files the person audited. (3) The runner, not only the tool, checks the audit and the folder (the spec put the refusals in `test`; a person could call the runner directly). (4) The rule of the scan that checks the workbench's own sentence for skills that read external content (`untrusted-content`) is reported under `conventions` and stops nothing; the spec says a scan error stops, and an error of a rule written for the authors of skills of the repository would stop nearly every outside skill. A finding the skill silenced with its own allow comment is still a finding.
- **`shared/references/security.md` item 10 is unchanged.** It says a third-party skill is applied and run in a scratch copy, never in the user's working tree. An imported skill is never applied or run in a working tree: the default home is outside every working tree (a `--home` inside a project's git-ignored `.workbench-local/` is inside that project's working tree, and is never run there either), nothing is installed, and the lab runs a copy in the container. The wording still covers the import.
- **Saved** (principle 13): a person tests a foreign skill without hand-copying it into the checkout, in the order vet, then run, with the audit tied to the content and the refusals in code. **Given up**: the evidence is measured on unreviewed cases of the person's own, so it is shown as local and preliminary and never promotes anything; the vetting stays a step the person runs, so the tool can read a verdict and cannot judge one.
- **Also in this package** (follow-ups of P11 that touch the same files): the runner prints a warning, never a refusal, when a full or a quick test starts on content that is not the last line of the version file (since `gate_of` sets such lines aside); `docs/community-evidence.md` says that the first fork to reach a case holds up to two thirds of its weight and that a failing community pool is frozen for its `X.Y`; the timing test of a long line in `evals/tests/test_measure.py` compares the CPU time at two sizes instead of a wall-clock bound.
- **Not changed.** No fingerprinted file, no committed evidence, no measurement version: `eval_status.py status` on the committed evidence is byte-identical before and after.

### Review of IMPORT-CLI (2026-10-17): what the independent review found and what was changed

The review of PR #447 said merge after fixes. Changed, each with a test: (M1, M2) the frontmatter closes only on a line that is exactly `---`, the new SKILL.md is the generated head, a line end and the body, and it is read back by a line walker that shares nothing with the splitter (`validate.py` closes its frontmatter the same way); (M3) the harness folders, the settings names and the reserved names (`evals`, `scripts/tests`) are compared without regard to case, a home is also compared by identity against `<ancestor>/<skills_dir>`, and two origin entries that are one path on a case-insensitive file system are refused; (M4) a `.git` file in any case and a folder shaped like a git directory are refused, and version-control folders are left out in any case; (M5) a home or a `--skill-dir` inside any checkout of the workbench is refused; (m1) the scan reads every file under `scripts/` whatever its extension, the fenced blocks of `references/*.md` and Ruby's and wget's writing calls, the repository's scan staying clean; (m2) the audit and the runner need a passing check at the content hash; (m3) the runner copies the folder once per event and stages from the copy; (m4) install markers and cache names are dropped or refused at fetch and refused by the runner; (m5) the spend is an estimate with a worst case and a list of what they leave out, in every document; (m7) a clone is not checked out until its listing and pack are within the caps. The accepted deviation (the scan's `untrusted-content` rule reported, not a stop) now has its consequence: the cases-template adds a `guard` placeholder, `check` requires an assertion tagged `guard`, and `status` lists the convention.

Second re-review of the same pull request: a checkout of the workbench is now an ancestor (links resolved) that holds `evals/eval-gate.json`, or both a `skills/` folder and `scripts/validate.py`, because an older checkout lacks the import tool and may lack the gate file, and the runner makes the same check; a file named `HEAD` or `commondir` in any case is refused anywhere in an import (a git directory can be split over two folders); the scan's any-extension rule leaves `scripts/tests/` out (its `.py` and `.sh` files keep their coverage); a SKILL.md with a byte order mark or CRLF line ends is read with both removed and the record says so; the per-event copy left by a SIGKILL is swept after a day by the next imported event.

The sweep of stale per-event copies deletes, so it is narrow: only a real folder of the same user, mode 0700, with the runner's marker file, older than a day, and whose lock file it can take without blocking (a running event holds that lock for its whole life); and an event checks the hash of its copy, as well as of the original, before it writes evidence.

## 2026-10-17: e-mail is a conversation channel; delivery is confirmed by the reply (EMAIL-CHANNEL: M-41)
