(12) The card of a change shows the event's current title, place and notes (read from the calendar, with hidden characters removed) to whoever may ask for the change, so a sender who holds `calendar-write` could read one of the person's own events by asking to change it: therefore (supervisor decision, restrictive) on a messaging app the lines that show that current text (`phrases.CARD_PRIVATE`: Before and Unchanged title, place and notes, and the same of a removal) are drawn only for a sender who also holds `ask-mail`; the others see the event's id and time as read live in the preparation (`The event now is at: ...`, always shown; the title read live is private, `The event now: <title>`) and the parts being changed, a note says some text is not shown, and such a card is not approved there (it is partial). The terminal and the page are unchanged. (14) Re-check of #479 (supervisor decisions, restrictive): the lines are drawn per READER of a decision, not per creator, `pending` and `task` take the channel and the MCP mode never gets a private line; a `created:` reference from messaging is usable only by the sender who asked for the event (the `asker` recorded in the result) or a holder of `ask-mail`, and every other answer (a guess, a card that created nothing) is one neutral sentence; the calendar id of a created event is recorded and compared; a plan or a flow cannot carry a `calendar-event` step (`not_plannable`), and a change or a removal always names who asks; a re-prepare after a stale card refuses when the reference now names another event; an unknown channel is passed through and refused by `may_read`.
- **To verify live (listed in `providers/writer/README.md`).** The console classification of the two Google calendar scopes; whether Graph accepts the extended-property filter for the lookup after a lost answer; whether the Outlook writer's `$expand` on a single event returns the property; that a removed Outlook event lands in Deleted Items (Q12: reversed on 2026-10-22, now an open, unverified fact); the Graph read of an event whose time zone is not UTC (the writer asks for UTC with `Prefer` and refuses an event that still comes in another zone).
- **Cost of the safety.** One request more on an Outlook change (the writer's own read), a card that carries the whole event, and a refusal of every meeting and series, so some of what a person would call managing an agenda is not done yet.
## 2026-10-18: files in the person's own Drive and OneDrive (W4a)

Package W4a of `SPEC-SECRETARY-WRITES`, with the maintainer's approval of its recommendations (2026-10-18): the secretary creates a file, writes a new revision of a file the person registered as a source, and saves a file a sender sent through a messaging app, in the person's own Google Drive and OneDrive. It builds on FND (`account_start.start`, `accounts`, the card line by line, the limits L55, L57 and L58) and on the account tokens of ACCT-G and ACCT-M.

- **Drive scope `drive`, OneDrive permission `Files.ReadWrite`** (the platform's sets, decisions Q1 and Q5): a writer can create a file and write a revision of any file the person names; what limits it is the project (`accounts`), the card and the writer's allow-list, not the token.
- **Writes only to folders `accounts` names.** A new file goes to a folder the project lists under a name (`accounts.<account>.folders`, the name `default` when none is given); the effect module's `preflight` reads the file again at the approval and refuses a folder that is no longer the id the document carries. A replace targets a registered source (decision D-D: the person registered it, or accepted it from FIND-1), not a folder, so the folder list does not narrow it; the source must be a file of that account and up to date with the file (`source_behind`), the same read the person's card shows.
- **A new file never overwrites** (Drive: the name read in the folder just before, by the writer, and a private mark `appProperties.openhora_key` so that a retry finds its own file; OneDrive: `conflictBehavior=fail`, 409); **a replace always lands as a new revision with the previous one kept** (Drive: the replaced head revision is marked `keepForever` BEFORE the write, recorded by the tests as the request that precedes the upload; a Google-native file keeps its own history; OneDrive: the version history, and an upload session made with the eTag so Graph answers 412 for a file that moved). Limit L56 is the row for the base of a change; W4a is its first user.
- **The dry run is built twice and compared.** `files_document.requests` builds the exact list of requests the writer will send from the approved document; the writer's `--dry-run` builds the same from its flags; `effect_files.execute` refuses `--confirmed` unless the hashes agree (a deviation), and a test starts each writer's dry run for every shape of document and compares. No seam call is needed for the dry run (the seam exposes only `file-meta`, limit L58).
- **`file-save` (Q9): approvable on the phone card only by the sender who sent it.** The card names the file, its size and a short hash (the bytes are the ones that sender just sent; the hash covers them); `on_cards` is true; `ops_grants._sender_problem` asks the kind's `held_attachment`, reads the held file's row (the sender is in the store alone, limit L48, never in the document) and answers another sender, or a file no longer held, as a file that does not exist. The terminal and the page approve any save. The held file is read again from the hold at the approval and must have the document's sha256: a file that expired or was swapped is `stale`. The file stays held (add or discard it as before); L48 now says a file leaves the hold also through an approved save.
- **Formats.** A new file: `.md`, `.txt`, `.docx` (from a `.docx` file of the project) and `.gdoc` (a Google Doc made from Markdown text). A replace: the file's own type; a Google Doc takes Markdown text (`files.update` with a `text/markdown` media: to verify live, README of the writers); `.md`, `.txt`, `.csv` take text; the others take a file of the project of the same type; a Google Sheet or Slides file is refused. A save: `.md`, `.txt`, `.csv`, `.pdf`, `.docx`, `.xlsx`, `.pptx`, and the held bytes must be that type (the sniffed subtype of the hold).
- **The text on the card sits behind a gutter and cannot fake its frame (after the independent review of #480).** `CARD_FILE_TEXT` puts the text between two rules and a mark `│ ` in front of every line of it, so that nothing a phone's Markdown renderer makes of a line (a heading, a quote, a bold span, a list item) can pass for a line of the card; the card says the mark is not part of the text. A line whose visible form (NFKC, look-alike dashes read as hyphens, Markdown markers and a bullet stripped) starts with five hyphens is refused when the card is made, so a Markdown rule of five hyphens or more must be written with three. A text is at most 64 KiB, has no control or invisible character and no credential; a longer text is a file of the project.
- **A name is a closed set** (letters and digits of any script, space, `. _ - ( )`; at most 120 characters; the NFC form; no leading hyphen, no trailing dot or space, no `..`), so no name reaches a path or a query as anything else; ids are percent-encoded with `safe=''` after a full-match pattern.
- **Cost of the safety.** Two builders of the same list of requests (a drift is caught by a test and by the dry-run comparison at each write); a card that holds a whole text is long; a replace of a file whose source is behind asks the person to refresh first; a gap of one request between the last read and a Drive media update that Drive's API cannot close. No skill, no agent and no fingerprinted file changed: no bump and no lab run; no call to Google or Microsoft was made (every test runs against a stand-in on loopback).
- **After the independent review of #480.** (1) *A lost answer is not stale and not someone else's edit.* A Drive replace whose answer was lost is found again by the key's mark (`files_document.landed`, and the writer), so `execute` no longer reads the moved file as stale; on OneDrive the test is the content hash Graph reports (sha256Hash, else sha1Hash), for the replace and for the create that meets a file of the same name: a hash that equals the approved bytes is ours (`replayed`), a hash that differs is the person's (`changed:` / `taken:`), and no comparable hash keeps the outcome `unknown`; a time and a size alone were never proof. (2) *A replace is made only of a file the account owns* (supervisor decision, restrictive, v1): Drive `ownedByMe` and `capabilities.canEdit`, OneDrive the account's own drive (`GET /me/drive`); a shared file or another drive is refused with a sentence, and the card names the account the file belongs to. (3) *The writer checks the account.* Every verb takes `--account <address>` and does nothing when the token record belongs to another account. (4) *What becomes of the replaced revision is said for the service and the type* (Drive binary: kept for ever, at most 200, and a refused keep writes nothing and says why; a Google Doc: its own history, not for ever; OneDrive: its retention). (5) *A name taken at the write answers the decision* (`taken:`), as a moved file does. (6) One reader of `SERVES` in `providers/resolve.py` (`serves_class`, which also survives a file that is not text); `effect_files.serves` uses it.
- **The rebase of #480 onto W1 (the consolidating PR).** (1) One reader of `SERVES`: `providers/resolve.py` `serves_class` (`strict=True` for the effect modules, which refuse a file they cannot read; `implementations` filters by it); `effect_calendar.serves` and `effect_files.serves` use it, and W1's own copy in `scripts/architecture_tables.py` is gone, with the behaviour kept. (2) W1's lessons for files (supervisor decisions, restrictive): the name of a file as the service holds it now (`CARD_FILE_NOW`, a replace's card) is private text (`phrases.CARD_PRIVATE`: a messaging reader needs `ask-mail`, the MCP mode never sees it); the name of a file a sender sent (`CARD_FILE_SENT`, `CARD_FILE_SAVE`, a save's card, `phrases.CARD_PRIVATE_OWNER`) is seen also by that sender, who is the only one who may approve the save (see the next entry for how a card learns who the reader is); a card that leaves such a line out says so in its own note (`CARD_HIDDEN_NOTE_FILE`) and is not approved there. (3) A plan or a flow cannot carry a `file-write` step (`not_plannable`), as for `calendar-event`; the refused words are `account_start.WORDS`. (4) A re-prepare after a stale card that finds the source pointing to another remote file refuses (`target_changed`) and asks the person to name it again. (5) A channel the policy does not know is passed through and refused, never taken for the terminal.
- **Re-check of #480 (the readers other than the person at the terminal).** (1) The record of an executed write (`files_document.facts`) holds ids, numbers and hashes and no name of a file; and `ops_grants.private_view` drops `payload.result` altogether for a reader who may not see the card's private lines (the MCP mode, a messaging reader without `ask-mail`), so the outcome cannot say what the card did not. (2) The approval row (`files_document.describe`, which the state file and `artifact` show) names the kind of write, the service, the folder or the source as `accounts` or the sources list names it, and the size and hash, never the name of the file. (3) A refusal made when the card is prepared (`not_owned`, `name_taken`) says "the file named on the request" and echoes no name into the task's note or step state. (4) The decision never returns the sender's account: `ops.pending(..., reader=<sender key>)` answers two booleans (`effect_sender_only`, `reader_sent_file`), the bridge asks it per chat (`Bridge.for_reader`) and hands them to the card; the save of a file a sender sent is offered for approval on a card only to that sender, and an `ask-mail` reader who is not that sender reads the whole card without the approve button. (5) `serves_class` reads 32768 characters for the SERVES line. (6) `frame_like` also refuses a line that opens with a Markdown link whose text starts with the frame mark, a braille blank (U+2800) in front, and the two-em and three-em dashes (U+2E3A, U+2E3B) read as two and three hyphens. (7) The task row (`ops.task`) of a step that writes to the person's own accounts (`steps.is_account_step`: a gate whose word is in `steps.ACCOUNT_WORDS`) carried the request itself in `step_args` (a calendar title and notes, a file's name and text, who asked) and the engine's failure sentence in `step_state`, to the MCP mode and to a messaging reader of the default `read` group. `ops_grants.task_view` now reduces it, for the MCP mode and a messaging reader without `ask-mail`, to the closed arguments (`steps.ACCOUNT_ARGS_SHOWN`: operation, account, calendar, folder, event, file, attachment) and a failure of step, code, field, provider and time; a messaging sender's id is in no argument they see. The terminal, the page, the conversation and a reader of mail get the row whole. `status` already lists closed fields only; a package that adds an account-write kind with a gate word in `ACCOUNT_WORDS` gets this view without further code.
## 2026-10-18: e-mail from the person's own account behind a card (W2)

Package W2 of `SPEC-SECRETARY-WRITES` (3.2): `mail-send` and `mail-reply` prepare an e-mail from the person's own Google or Microsoft account and stop at a card; the send is the `execute` of the existing effect `send`, with the account token, after the approval of the hash. Every rule of the outbox's e-mail (L42) holds for it.

- **Replies (Q6, approved).** A reply goes to the replied message's `From` as the snapshot's index recorded it, and to no one else; a different `Reply-To` is shown on the card and not used; no reply to all in this version (the operation has no `all` flag). The reference `<source id>#<n>` is resolved through the index (`runtime/item_refs.py`), with the caller's rights by the operation (a messaging sender needs `ask-mail`) and again by the preparation. Limit L60.
- **One document, one effect word.** *(The grant is superseded: an own-account e-mail has its own, `approve-effect-send-own`, since the review of #482 below.)* The own-account e-mail is the outbox's document with four keys more (`account`, `thread_id`, `references`, `reply`), the same `send` word and the same `approve-effect-send` grant (its text now says "your outbox or one of your own accounts"); `account_start` gained `send` in its table of words. The outbox's document and hash are unchanged. Two mailers are two more implementations of `sender:email` (`providers/mailer/gmail.py`, `outlook.py`), named explicitly from the document's `account`, never what `auto` picks (`providers/resolve.py` `CLASS_DEFAULTS`: `smtp` is the default of the class) and never the project's outbox (`project_config.OWN_ACCOUNT_MAILERS`).
- **The From is the account's.** The document's sender is `accounts.<account>.address`; the preflight refuses another address or an unlisted account at the approval too; the mailer refuses a From that is not the address of the token (the record's `account` and the service's own profile). No attendee, no invitation, no draft: drafts are the `mailbox` word (W3).
- **The card.** Fixed lines of the phrase table (`CARD_MAIL_*`, both columns; the start line, the text and the end line are one key, `CARD_MAIL_MESSAGE`, because a key that is the text alone would be the same in both columns, which the table's test refuses), a fixed title, the person's text behind a mark on every line, and a line of the text that could pass for the frame of the card (five hyphens, or a look-alike, after Markdown) or a hidden character refused at the argument. An approval's record names the account and the count, never the person's words.
- **Gmail.** The mailer builds the MIME with `providers/_email.py` (which gained an optional `references` list) and sends `raw` and `threadId` in one JSON request. A lost answer is looked up in the sent mail by the exact Message-ID (and the label SENT), never by size or time. **A Bcc is refused from a Google account in this version**: the API reads the recipients from the headers, and whether it removes a `Bcc` header before delivery is not verified (a kept one would show the hidden list). Attachments are limited to 3 MiB together.
- **Outlook.** A new message is a draft, read back, then sent; a reply is `createReply`, an update to exactly the approved message, a read-back and the send. A draft that is not the approved message, or a step before the send that fails, is moved to Deleted Items (nothing is deleted for good) and the key is freed; the key has phases (`claimed`, `drafted`, `sending`), and only a key in `sending` is looked up (in Sent Items, by the `internetMessageId` Graph gave the draft, recorded before the send). Attachments are limited to 2.5 MiB together.
- **Not verified live (listed in `providers/mailer/README.md`).** Whether Gmail keeps the Message-ID it is given and removes a Bcc header; whether Graph returns plain text on drafts and keeps a reply in its conversation after the update; the size limits. Each fails closed: a lookup that finds nothing leaves the key waiting for the person's `resolve`; a draft that reads back wrong is not sent.
- **Cost of the safety.** The mailer needs an extra read (the account's profile) before each send; no Bcc from Google; small attachments; a key can wait for the person after a lost answer when a service rewrites the Message-ID.

- **After the independent review of #482 (2026-10-19).** (1) *Own grant (supervisor, restrictive):* an e-mail from the person's own account is approved from a messaging app only with `approve-effect-send-own` (and the sender needs the group `mail-send` to prepare one); the grant of the outbox's `approve-effect-send` does not cover it, and giving it needs `accept-config --widen`. The effect word stays `send`; the kind answers `approval_word(doc)` = `send-own` and `ops_grants.effect_facts` returns it as the word the policy is asked about (`effect` keeps the effect's own word). (2) *The message answered is pinned:* the operation writes its service id and Message-ID into the step's arguments (`reply_pin`), and a preparation whose reference now names another message (a refreshed snapshot renumbers its items) is refused. (3) *The reading right is bound into the step (M2):* the operation writes `channel` and `sender` into the arguments of a reply as the caller gave them (a missing channel is `terminal`), the step checks them strictly, and the preparation resolves the reference with them, never as the operations' own; `steps.plan_task` refuses `account`, `reply`, `reply_pin`, `channel` and `sender` on `send-email` unless the operation wrote them (`not_plannable`: the outbox's e-mail is still a plan's task). (3b) *What a reply shows of the message answered is private (M1, the mechanism of W1):* the lines `CARD_MAIL_REPLY_TO`, `CARD_MAIL_REPLY_TO_DIFFERS`, `CARD_MAIL_IN_REPLY` and the reply's own subject line `CARD_MAIL_SUBJECT_REPLY` are in `phrases.CARD_PRIVATE`: a messaging app draws them only for a sender who holds `ask-mail`, the MCP mode gets the decision without them (`ops_grants.private_view`), and a card without them is not approvable on messaging. The note under a stripped card now says "the event or the message". (4) *Outlook's key:* a pending key in the phase `claimed` or `drafted` is taken again only after 150 seconds without moving (two HTTP timeouts and a margin), and answers "in progress" (exit 1, nothing sent) before that, so two live attempts of one key never both send. (5) *Smaller:* the sender's address from the index is normalized before it is compared or used; the Outlook draft must report `hasAttachments` exactly as planned; the Gmail preview shows the query (`fields=`) the send carries; a 2xx other than the documented one on a send is unknown (exit 4), not a refusal; the text refuses the letters that draw nothing (U+3164, U+115F, U+1160, U+FFA0, U+2800, U+034F, variation selectors); the effect record keeps the Message-ID Graph gave the message (`service_message_id`) so it can be found.

- **After the re-check of #480 (the task row of an e-mail, who asked of a calendar write).** (1) `steps.is_account_step(kind, args)` also counts a `send-email` task whose arguments carry one only an operation writes (`steps.ACCOUNT_ARGS`: `account`, `reply`, `reply_pin`, `channel`, `sender`), so `ops_grants.task_view` reduces the task row of an e-mail from the person's own account for the MCP mode and a messaging reader without `ask-mail`: `steps.public_args(args, kind)` shows no argument for that kind (the addresses, the subject, the text, the account, the message answered and who asked are all the person's or another sender's), the failure is cut to its step, code, field, provider and time, and the task's `note` to the part before ` -- ` (the sentence). The outbox's `send-email` (none of those arguments) is not an account write and is returned as it was. (2) The mailer's own sentence can quote an address, a subject or a file name, and the `note` of a task is listed by `status` to every channel: a refusal of the mailer's dry run or of the write of the document now carries the step's fixed sentence (`step_send_email.REFUSED_NEXT_OWN`); only the reading of the message a reply answers keeps a sentence, and that one is written by code from the phrase table and names the reference, never a text. The cost: the person at the terminal no longer reads the mailer's exact words (the size an attachment is over, the name of the file); the limits stay in `providers/mailer/README.md`. The result of an executed send (`facts`), the line `describe` writes into the approval and the state file, and the pending views hold counts, the account and its address, hashes and Message-IDs, never a recipient, a subject or a sender id; the card of a new e-mail was the asker's own text *(superseded by the next entry: it is private)*. (3) `ops_grants.private_view` drops `result.asker` (the sender key of the messaging sender who asked for a calendar write, W1) from the decision for the MCP mode and for every messaging reader but that sender, also for a reader of the person's mail and calendar and also when the card has no private line; the terminal and the page read the record whole.

## 2026-10-19: the card of an e-mail is private

Maintainer's decision. The card of a NEW e-mail from the person's own account (To, Cc, Bcc, Subject, the text, the names of the attachments) is private, as the card of a reply already was: `CARD_MAIL_TO`, `CARD_MAIL_CC`, `CARD_MAIL_BCC`, `CARD_MAIL_SUBJECT`, `CARD_MAIL_MESSAGE` and `CARD_MAIL_ATTACHMENT` join `phrases.CARD_PRIVATE`. A messaging app draws them only for a sender who holds `ask-mail`; the MCP mode never reads them, in any view; the count of recipients, the account, the hash and the way to approve stay.

- **Approved only where shown whole, at the operations layer.** `ops_grants.effect_refusal` now refuses the approval from a messaging app of an effect whose stored card has a private line (`ops_grants.has_private_lines`) to a sender who is not a reader (`item_refs.may_read`), with a sentence that names `ask-mail`, the terminal and the page. Until now only the bridge withheld the approve button, so a client calling the operation could approve a card it had not been shown. It holds for W1's cards that show the current text of an event and W4a's that show the name of a file too (closing the defence-in-depth finding of the re-check). The cost: the group `ask-mail` cannot be listed in a project's profile yet (NR-2), so no messaging sender approves an e-mail, a change or removal of an event, or a replace of a file from a card until it exists; the terminal and the page approve, and the card says so.
- **The mailer's exact sentence.** A refusal of the mailer's dry run is stored as the step's fixed sentence (`REFUSED_NEXT_OWN`, in the task's note and failure, which `status` lists to every channel) and returned in full in the error of the call that started the task, to the terminal, the page or a reader of the mail (`StepFailure.detail`, never stored); any other caller gets the fixed sentence.
- **Smaller.** The comment of `APPROVAL_GROUP_OWN` in `effect_send.py` no longer carries the sentence of the line above; the comment of `steps.ACCOUNT_WORDS` says why `send` is not in it; a line of a test that could never run is gone; the architecture pages describe the cut task row and the dropped `result.asker`.

## 2026-10-18: the person's own mailbox: drafts, archive, move, mark and trash (W3)

Package W3 of SPEC-SECRETARY-WRITES, on top of FND (the start, the card line by line), ACCT-G and ACCT-M (one token per account) and SNAP-IDX (the references to a message). The maintainer's decisions of the day that shape it: the effect word is `mailbox` and covers drafts too (Q7: nothing is sent, so it is not `send`); a move goes only to a mail folder or label named in `runtime.json` `accounts` (archive and the trash are the provider's own); at most 20 messages in a card; nothing is deleted for good anywhere.

- **What was built.** `runtime/mailbox_document.py`, `effect_mailbox.py`, `step_mailbox.py` and `ops_mailbox.py` (the word `mailbox`, the step kind `mailbox-change`, the operations `mail-draft`, `mail-archive`, `mail-move`, `mail-mark` and `mail-trash`, the group `mail-organize`, the grant `approve-effect-mailbox`); the seam `mailbox_read` of `ops_step_seams.py` (message state and a folder's id, and the dry run: no way to write); two writers of the class `writer:mailbox`, `providers/writer/gmail.py` and `outlook-mail.py`, with `providers/_writer_mailbox.py` (the ledger and the spec reader both share); the stand-ins `fake_gmail_modify.py` and `fake_graph_mail_write.py` and their tests; the mailbox half of `phrases.CARD_PRIVATE`.
- **The lessons of the W1 and W4a reviews, applied from the start.** (1) *Every message is listed by the subject and sender read from the mailbox in the preparation* (plus the snapshot's reading time), never by the snapshot's text; on a messaging app that text, the recipient and subject of a reply (which come from the message it answers) and, after #484, the addresses, subject and text of every draft, are shown only to a sender who holds `ask-mail`, and a card with a line left out is not approvable there (`ops_grants.effect_refusal` refuses it too). An account-write card that cannot be read as a card is private as a whole (`has_private_lines(item, account_write)`, `private_view`, `messaging_cards.build`), and the line `Approve exactly this` is left out for a reader who cannot approve; the note that lines were left out is `CARD_HIDDEN_NOTE_MAIL` for an e-mail or a mailbox card. (2) *A message moved meanwhile makes the whole card `stale`*: the base (Gmail: the sorted labels; Outlook: the changeKey and the folder) is inside the hash, the effect reads every message again and the writer reads them once more before its first write. (3) *A partial failure is `unknown` and says which positions are done*: the ledger of a writer has one entry per message, the writer exits 4 with `partial: done 1,2 of 5`, and approving again sends only the others. (4) *The writer checks that the token's account is the document's address* (`--account` on every verb but `--check`). (5) *Any 5xx on a write is lost* (exit 4, the key kept); a 429 is a refusal with its `Retry-After`. (6) *A lost answer is proved by the state, never by size or time*: Gmail's labels, a moved Outlook message found in the destination by its `internetMessageId` with its old id gone, a draft found by its Message-ID (Gmail) or by the private property `openhora_key` (Outlook). (7) *Ids are percent-encoded with `safe=''`* and patterns refuse a leading hyphen (a flag would read it). (8) *A dry run reads no credential and opens no connection*, and its hash is the document's. (9) *`ALLOWED` is enforced in the request function* of each writer; the fake servers answer 405 to anything else and the tests assert the exact request list of every write.
- **Choices where the spec left one (by recommendation; the maintainer may revise).** (1) A card that would change nothing is refused (`nothing_to_change`, with the positions): a mark of a message already read, an archive of one not in the inbox. The alternative, to drop them silently, would make the card say less than the request. (2) A reply draft has no `cc` or `bcc` and no `all` flag in v1; a draft takes no attachment (`attach` is W2's send). (3) A move to the Gmail labels that mean something else (`INBOX`, `TRASH`, `SPAM`, `SENT`, `DRAFT`, `UNREAD`, `CHAT`) and to an Outlook well-known name outside `archive`, `deleteditems`, `inbox`, `junkemail` is refused even when `mail_folders` names it, in the document and in the writer. (4) Outlook's reply draft is `createReply`, which carries the approved message (recipients, subject, body, key), and then one `PATCH` that sets the same on the draft it made, because Graph would address a reply to the message's `Reply-To`; the draft's id is noted in the ledger before the `PATCH` so a retry sets that draft, and a `PATCH` that is refused moves the draft to Deleted Items (review of #483). (5) The messages and their order are the card's: an Outlook move gives the message a new id, so the ledger keeps the old id as the entry and the lookup uses the `internetMessageId`. (6) The tests are not named `test_limit_N_`: the limit rows L55, L57 and L58 are FND's and L56 is the calendar package's, and none was edited here; the limits are held by tests that say them in their docstrings.
- **A draft's text sits behind the gutter, like an e-mail's (after the review of #483 and W2).** The text of a draft is shown with `send_document.guttered` (the mark `│ ` in front of every line, the card's `CARD_BOX_DRAFT_TEXT`), so a line that begins like a line of the card cannot be taken for one; and the subject and the text pass `send_document.text_problem` (a control, format, separator, surrogate or private-use character, a filler that draws nothing such as U+3164, and a line that could pass for the frame of the card) at the start and again in the document. The step kind `mailbox-change` is covered by W4a's task view (`steps.is_account_step`, `steps.public_args`): the MCP mode and a messaging reader without `ask-mail` read a mailbox task with only the closed arguments, the failure reduced to its code, and the note cut before ` -- `. The note that a card was drawn without the subjects and senders is `hidden_note_key`'s (`CARD_HIDDEN_NOTE_BOX` when every line left out is a mailbox card's).
- **Cost of the safety.** One read of each message before the card and two before the write (the effect's and the writer's): 20 messages are up to 60 small reads. Accepted: the write is rare and the card is the point.
- **To verify live** (`providers/writer/README.md`): `rfc822msgid:` finds a draft Gmail made from a MIME with a given Message-ID; `createReply` honours a `message` body (if it does not, the `PATCH` sets the text and the writer's refusal path moves the orphan to Deleted Items); `$filter` on an extended property finds a draft in Drafts; Graph keeps `internetMessageId` across a `move`; a message moved by Graph has a new id.
## 2026-10-19: a write asked in words opens its card (NR-5)

Maintainer's decision, 2026-10-19: all eleven recommendations of section 12 of `SPEC-NATURAL-WRITES`. A plain line that asks for a write to the person's own accounts opens the card of that exact write, through the operation's own function, with every slot from code or asked back; the card is the approval and nothing else is asked. Package NR-5a builds the foundation, the calendar and files families, the numbered items and the default account; NR-5b the mail and mailbox families (limit L61 is theirs too).

- **Q1. A body is the person's words, verbatim.** No drafting run: "confirmo na sexta" is sent as written and the card shows it. A model-drafted body is NR-6 (backlog), after two real cases.
- **Q2. `accounts.default`** (`google` or `microsoft`) in `runtime.json`, settled by the terminal alone and accepted with `accept-config --widen` (`policy_widening` lists it as `default-account=<account>`); `project_config.account_default(cfg)` reads it. It is used when a line names no service and both accounts could serve it; a service word in the line beats it.
- **Q3. A reading the engine is unsure of opens the card at once** with one line before it that says which part to check ("Confira a data: entendi ..."); the card shows the date in full and is the approval.
- **Q4. Recipients are addresses written in the line** (package NR-5b); a name is asked for its address. A `contacts` key is backlog.
- **Q5. Messages by words** (NR-5b): the whole-word rule over the newest snapshot's index, singular one match or a list, plural every match up to 20.
- **Q6. The read `items`** (`runtime/ops_items.py`): the numbered messages and events of the newest snapshots, private (the terminal, the page and a messaging sender with `ask-mail`; never the MCP mode), stored as a neutral word. It is a row of the group `read`, and the operation asks `item_refs.may_read` itself. The page route takes `source` in its query; `kind` is the terminal's and a line's.
- **Q7. The caption of a message of one file** that is a closed save form ("salva esse pdf no drive") opens the save card once the file is held; any other caption stays ignored with its sentence.
- **Q8. No place is extracted** from an event's words: they stay in the title, which the card shows.
- **Q9. An end time is a closed range form** ("das 13h às 15h", "from 1pm to 3pm", "13h-15h"); an end not after its start is asked; without a range the operation's default hour is shown on the card.
- **Q10. On a phone a write line bypasses the router's queue** and is not stored in the conversation, like a reminder.
- **Q11. A line in words never uses the standing approval of W5.** W5 applies to typed requests only, unless the maintainer says otherwise when W5 is built.
- **The kind question is answered in a phrase (a live fix, 2026-10-19).** A phrase such as "event in the calendar" or "I want it as a reminder" is an answer: the kind word with the closed words around it (`requests.writes.kind_fillers`, language data, no open tail; `natural.answer_kind`, `complete`). The word "no" of one language, which is "in" in another, is not a way out beside a kind. A reply to the kind question (and to the loose verb's question) that is not an answer is asked once more, with the same question, up to eight words; a line that starts with a question or a request word, one with a work verb, a question mark, or a request of its own (`candidate`) is a new line. Answered "event", the question opens the W1 card with the reading it showed.
- **Review fixes (2026-10-19, an independent review at the head before the base merge).** Bare-number ranges are read only in two closed shapes ("de 13 a 15", "13-15h", "1-2pm"; never behind a date cue, never above 12 hours, never a pair below 7 without am or pm) and always checked, and the fuzz reads ranges with a table of its own, not the parser's rewrite; a kind answer that carries a time ("lembrete às 15h") shows the changed reading and asks again, it never makes the reminder; a number left among the words of a title, hours written bare and a time that has passed today are checked on the card before it; the fuzz checks that a start is read together with its day and an end with its start; a text answer is never a question or a request, a question is asked once more and then given up in a sentence (`NW_GIVE_UP`), and a question the answers leave open twice is given up the same way; the router's `When:` is never used for a write; a date that does not exist is asked again (`NW_ASK_DAY_INVALID`) and a line that starts like a change but is no change form is answered "a change is asked by number" (`NW_CHANGE_BY_NUMBER`); "event at 3 pm" as the kind answer applies the time as a correction of the reading shown; a caption that names a kind the file is not is no save line; the words around a kind in its answer are those of its own language.
- **Deviations from the spec.** The operation is opened with the real carrying channel: the conversation's shell is the terminal's equal and the page is the page (`ops_natural._call` maps both to `chat`, which `account_start` refuses). The conversation's shell stays called as the terminal: the supervisor recommended listing `chat` in the rows and recording it, but the account step refuses `chat` as a channel it does not know (`test_files_write.py`, W4a: a channel is never taken for the terminal by accident), listing it in the rows opens the typed chat grammar for commands with several free arguments, and the calendar document's `asker` has no `chat`; the change is a package of its own, if wanted. The router's spans are not used for a slot: the hint names the family, the family's own parse reads the line, and the `When:` is never used for a write (a time the line's words do not give is asked). `joiners` holds `e`/`and` and the comma is code's. The family registry holds one pure module and one resolver module for each family (`natural_writes_<family>`, `ops_natural_<family>`), calendar and files included.

## 2026-10-18: natural requests: any line in the person's words, by class, checked as the typed command (NR-2)

The maintainer asked that everything be possible in natural language, not only reminders, and approved every recommendation of `SPEC-NATURAL-REQUESTS` (with the additions below).

- **Three tiers, code first.** Tier 1 is code with no model run: closed whole-line forms in Portuguese and English (`runtime/natural.py`); tier 3 is the router's existing run, which gains a fifth outcome, `Route: none (operation)`, read and believed by code against the person's line; tier 2, embeddings, is designed (package EMB) and adopted only above a measured bar, not built. A model step in front of the router was rejected: it adds a paid run in front of a run that still happens.
- **One dispatcher, one class per operation.** Every operation of the table (and each planned effect) has a class in one literal: read, notice, confirmed write, decision, effect, gated, work. A line runs a read and a notice; it never decides, consents, changes a mode, a model or the configuration, or registers a source; every operation it reaches is checked by `operations.refusal` with the carrying channel and the sender and called with them. A test walks the table of operations so that no operation is left unclassified, and pins the classes. Limit L54.
- **A confirmed write waits for NR-4.** Until then the reply to a line that names a cancel, a stop, a retry or a routine edit is the exact command to send; a decision named in words is answered with its card sent again.
- **Any language.** The code tables cover Portuguese and English; for any other language the router is the path, and the outcome must not depend on code parsing the person's words. The router may carry a normalised `When:`; code cannot verify it against the line, so an operation that receives one is never executed at once: it asks for a one-line confirmation (a draft in memory, ten minutes, never stored), like a tier-2 pick. Text arguments stay spans of the line, in any script.
- **AQ-3 is superseded.** A plain question about the documents is the router's `Operation: ask`; the dispatcher calls `ask` with the person's whole line and the sources are chosen by code, never by the router's `Sources:` line. The routing row to the answer skill of `SPEC-ANSWER-FROM-SOURCES` is dropped.
- **Mail and calendar become a group, `ask-mail`** (supersedes the RT-1 rule of 2026-10-18 for its holders). A messaging sender whose profile lists it may ask about the mail and calendar sources, and those sources join the automatic selection of their questions; a sender without it keeps the RT-1 rule exactly. It adds to `ask` and never grants it, is valid on a messaging app alone, is in no default, and is a widening accepted with `--widen`.
- **Files are named in words.** A line like "what does my CV say about Vue?" is resolved by code (whole words of a source's name, id and description, with aliases of a kind of document): one strong match is used and stated, several or weak ones are listed and the question waits for the choice, none says so and that searching the person's accounts is planned. `@<id>` wins.
- **Cost of the safety.** The class table is the whole protection and is visible: a move between classes is one line and a changed line in a pinned test. The router path costs the run a line costs today; a tier-1 line costs none. The residue: a reminder made through the router had its words in the router's prompt, and the cancelled request keeps the line until it is deleted.
- **Integration with NR-1 (phase B, 2026-10-19).** One `runtime/natural.py`: NR-1's reminder engine and `CLASSES` as the only class source. The closed tier-1 forms, the aliases and cues, and the yes and no words are data of `natural_lang_<tag>.py` (`requests`), tried for every language on every line; a form has no open tail, so a request for work that starts with the same words ("create a document about ...", "schedule the post for Friday") goes to the router and no scheduling verb is a trigger of an effect. One draft store, `runtime/drafts.py`, for the bridge and the operations layer. A reminder or a forget the router chose follows NR-1's confirmation rules: the reading is shown, only a yes to it makes the reminder, for the moment shown, and a pick never confirms. M8 (a calendar event or something else) is NR-1's `candidate` alone. A sender who may not talk never reaches the router (B1), and a natural read whose row lacks the MCP mode is stored as a neutral word (B2).
- **The writes now exist, so class E is "go and run it".** The operations of W1 to W4a (events, mail, files) are real; a line that names one is answered with the place and the exact operation, never run, until package NR-5 routes a line into them with slots and the card of the exact content.
- **Second review of #477 (2026-10-19).** A reminder to bring back that the router chose is answered with its command, never brought back (no code can check the choice, and a restore has no preview); a bare word for a file ("documents", "pdf", "arquivo") with no kind of document is a general question again, and "no source matches" is only for a kind (cv, invoice, contract) that matches nothing; the yes and no words of the language data are the engine's own on every path (`natural.engine_word`); a choice of a file offered in one project is never answered in another; a profile that lists `ask-mail` is named by one sender; the private lines of a card drawn for a holder are stored in the conversation as a neutral word; the forms are checked against a whitelist (`CLOSED_FORM`), not a ban on `.*`; a `word` slot is a whole word of the line; "help" in words is answered for a sender who may not talk; an operation the router named for a queued request is answered; a question the page left open is swept on expiry.
- **L51 on the router path (2026-10-19).** The question of a routed forget stores the neutral word like the question of a routed reminder; a line the tables do not recognise (a reminder in Spanish) is shown as the neutral word by `conversation` to every reader but the person's own shells and is left out of the router's memory, from the neutral reply stored after it. The store is append-only, so the line is in it as written, as the cancelled request's text is: the residue is stated in the L54 row.
- **`ask-mail` is a sender's own.** The group may be listed only in a sender's own profile (`messaging.senders.<platform>[].policy`), never in a channel-wide list or a profile a channel names (`project_config` refuses it with a sentence), because the text of a mailbox is read for the person it belongs to; it is still a widening that needs `accept-config --widen`. The private lines of the cards of the writes show and approve for that sender alone.

## 2026-10-19: the router's question on a phone (LIVE-1)

Three things the maintainer met live on a Telegram chat of a project that writes in Portuguese: a reminder-shaped line fell to the router, which asked.

- **A question reaches a phone as the question.** The router's reply is the whole template of `core-orchestrator`; on a messaging app both the reply to a line and the notice card of the decision show the framing sentence (`ROUTER_ASKS_FRAME`, in the project's language) and the `Q<n>: ... Recommended: ...` lines only (`phrases.question_lines`, `phrases.asked_view`). The page, the terminal and the stored decision keep the template whole, and the card's hash is of the stored text. A reply with no question line is shown as it was. Rejected: filtering the template by section names (a model that renames a section would leak it); reading the question lines is the shape `read_route` already trusts.
- **Review of #487 (2026-10-19).** A question keeps its `Recommended:` line and a wrapped continuation (the phone showed `Q1:` alone when the line was written on the next line); the section of instructions found in external content, and only it, is skipped (its heading and the non-blank lines directly under it), so a `Q<n>:` line planted under the heading is never shown as the router's question while the closing questions the template puts after the section are (the first version stopped at the heading and lost them: corpus `ops-ci-pipeline/iteration-1/4/floor/2`), and a section that holds more than a `none` word adds one localized note (`ROUTER_QUOTED_NOTE`). A `Recommended:` line always attaches to the last question. The reply and the card share one test, `phrases.is_router_question`. A reply with no question line is shown whole.
- **The same question is sent to a chat once.** When the reply went out, the bridge writes a ledger card of kind `question` for the decision and that chat (`Bridge.note_asked`), which the notice reads as sent; another listed sender with notices still gets the card, and a reply that did not go out leaves the notice to be sent. Rejected: suppressing the notice of every router question (a second sender, or a person who did not ask through the phone, would never see it), and marking the decision in the store at the reply (a failed send would hide the question). Residue: a tick that falls between the decision's creation and the ledger line still sends the notice; the window is one reply long, and the cost of closing it is a lock between the bridge's two threads.
- **No proposal for the instructions on a phone.** An answer typed on a messaging app is never offered as a line for `docs/workbench/instructions.md`, and the request's one proposal is not spent there; the channels that keep it say it with phrase keys (`INSTR_PROPOSAL_ASK`, `INSTR_WHERE_SAYS`, `INSTR_WHERE_ELSEWHERE`) in the project's language. The file is written from the terminal or the page only (limit L40), the text was a command with a path and a hash, and the phone cannot act on it.
## 2026-10-19: a mail write asked in words (NR-5b)

Package NR-5b of `SPEC-NATURAL-WRITES`, the mail family on top of the foundation (NR-5a). The maintainer approved all eleven recommendations of the spec's section 12 on 2026-10-19; those that shape the mail family are below. A plain line opens the card of an e-mail sent, a reply, a draft, or of 1 to 20 messages archived, moved, marked or sent to the trash, through the operation's own function; it executes and approves nothing.

- **A body is the person's words (Q1).** The text of an e-mail, a reply or a draft is a verbatim slice of the line (the span after `dizendo que`, `saying`, `:` ...), shown whole on the card. No model drafts it; a drafted body is a later package (NR-6) after two real cases.
- **Recipients are addresses written in the line (Q4).** A name asks for its address, and the answer must be an address; no mailbox, snapshot, index or address book is searched for one. A reply and a reply draft carry no recipient: the operation takes it from the index of the message answered (limit L60 is untouched, its test unchanged).
- **A message by words (Q5).** "o e-mail da Bia", "os e-mails do banco": the words after `do/da/from/about` are compared, as whole words and folded, with the words of each message of the newest snapshot's index (the local part of the from-address split on `. _ - +`, the domain labels except the last, the subject); a message matches only when every content word is among them. A singular object needs exactly one match (several are listed, up to five, and asked), a plural object takes every match up to 20 (more are asked); `ultimo` is the newest. The index holds no display name, so a name the sender writes in a different form than its address does not match: it is asked, never guessed.
- **A line on the phone is not stored and skips the router's queue (Q10)** and **a line in words never uses a standing approval (Q11)**: both are the foundation's, and nothing in this family touches either.
- **The card is the confirmation.** No "yes" layer before it; every slot is code's or the person's, shown in full on the card. A scheduled send is not offered: a time in the words outside the subject and the body makes the line not a write form.
- **Choices where the spec left one (by recommendation; the maintainer may revise).** (1) A service word names an account only as the shared data of the foundation says (`services`, with `service_in`: `pelo gmail`, `via outlook`, or a phrase of two words), so that `gmail` as a sender's word ("os e-mails do gmail do banco") is a word of the message and not an account; after a number or `do/from` the bare word is the account. (2) A mark asks for its state ("lido ou nao lido?") when the line leaves it out, and a reply asks for its body; a move needs its folder in the line. (3) A name beside an address ("para Bia bia@x.example") is not taken: the line goes on, because the address could be another person's. (4) A router-named operation is read again by the family's own parse (`read(..., op=...)`) and is taken only when the parse agrees. (5) The questions that offer a choice (the account, the mailbox, the candidates, the folder, the state) use the foundation's options and its generic answer, so the number shown is the place in the list the person was shown, not the message's number in the snapshot; the candidates are listed by the sender's address and the subject as the index recorded them, and only after `item_refs.read_index` accepted the caller. (6a) After the independent review of #485: `ultimo`/`latest` with no words is message 1 of the newest snapshot, which is read (a snapshot older than the unattended limit L53 names nothing); the words after `do/da/from` are at most three, with no verb and no gerund (a longer tail is a sentence and the line goes on as before); a day, a month, a weekday or a relative time among them (`ontem`, `setembro`, `semana passada`) is never matched, and the line says messages are not picked by date; a range (`1 to 3`) is never read as a folder, and a folder is not a number or a service; `para <person>` after a bare `para` is a forward, which is not built, unless the project lists a folder by that name; the body of a reply is kept as written, final `?` included, and shown whole on the card (the card is the check; the request's own `pode mandar ...?` frame is not told apart from the body); `pro`, `pros` and `prum` mark a recipient; the closed heads (verb, articles, object) are asked by `is_head`, so that an event titled `Passa no banco` or `Send flowers` keeps its title. The provenance check of the fuzz (`natural_writes.problems`) is derived from the raw lines with the marker words of the tables and never calls the mail family. A calendar title that starts with a calendar verb (`Move couch`) is still read as a request by the calendar family's bare verbs: the same fix for that family is its own. (6) The text answers of the family (subject, body, address) follow the foundation's M2 rules: a question, a work verb at three words or more, a request head or an over-long subject is never an answer; a short non-answer is asked once more and then `NW_GIVE_UP`; anything longer is a new line.
- **Cost of the safety.** An index read for a by-words choice (the manifest of one snapshot, hashed against its record) and a question more for a line that leaves a slot open; a folder written close to a configured name is asked, not corrected. Accepted: the card is the point and a wrong pick costs a card that is ignored.

## 2026-10-20: a phone never reads a command or a path (LIVE-2)

The maintainer saw this on a Telegram chat of a project that writes in Portuguese: the reply to a line that named a calendar write ended with `uv run --with keyring==25.7.0 python3 ~/projects/.../runtime/cli.py event-create --project ~/projects/... --account ... --title ... --start ...`, and said to do it in the terminal or on the page. On an online platform the person has no terminal. The decision (2026-10-19, recorded): **a messaging reply never carries a terminal command or a local path.**

- **Two layers, the sentences and the net.** What the phone can do, it does (a write asked in words opens its card, NR-5a). What is kept off it by design (the acceptance of a configuration, accounts, secrets, permissions and policies, sources added from the terminal, routines, the standing instructions, a reminder the router chose to bring back, the approval of an effect whose card is not whole) is answered with ONE plain sentence in the project's language and the page link (`messaging.page_url`; else "on the platform's page"): the phrase family `PHONE_`, chosen by the operation's family (`ops_natural.PHONE_FAMILY`), `ops_natural._command` never returning a terminal command for the carrying channel `messaging`. The terminal, the page and the conversation keep the exact command. Under the sentences, principle 11: `messaging_guard.guard_message` in `Bridge.deliver`, the one point that sends anything, cuts a command line of this platform (`uv run`, a script of this repository, this shell's flags, `keyring set openhora`) or a path (absolute, `~/`, a drive) from the text, the footer and the file, says once that the rest is done on the page, and records an event a test reads (`EVENTS`); the log names the kinds and never the text. Limit L62. Rejected: stripping with no sentence (the person would not know something was left out); a rule in the providers (each would need its own copy); a prompt (principle 11).
- **The effect card.** It says "Approve it on the platform's page." with the link, has no command and no `code_span`, and its line "Approving applies exactly the text above." prints no hash (the button still binds to the hash the ledger recorded, and a changed card is refused, both unchanged and tested). The line of a stored body that only says how to run something is not drawn; the one line that names a file of this machine (the publisher's ledger) is said as "a file on this machine"; the card shows the person's data exactly as it is (review of #490: a title, note, subject or text that looks like a command or a path is theirs, and the card's approvability is unchanged), and the net (`messaging_guard`, `guard=True`) covers only the error text of the operations layer. The hash lines of the mail and file cards are not printed on a phone, as the calendar card's never was. An unknown outcome of a write ("the outcome is unknown", a provider's exit 4) is told as unknown, with where to look, never as "nothing was done".
- **Polish from the first live card.** Dates are said in the project's language at the send point only (`phrases.localize_dates`: `qua 21/10/2026 13:00-14:00` with an en dash; the shape `Wed dd/mm/yyyy` of `calendar_document.when_text` is the only weekday the operations layer writes, the other families write numeric dates; the stored card and its hash are of the stored text). The header names the kind as a plain word (`KIND_EFFECT` is "request"/"pedido"). The line that says a card waits is dropped when the card itself was just sent to the same chat, and kept where none was shown. After an approval the chat is told what was done (`messaging_outcome`, `OUT_`) or why it was not (`messaging_failure`, `FAIL_`: folder unreadable, account not authorized, quota, name taken, too large, no answer; a preparation's closed codes are mapped exactly, a write's providers' words best effort); no "I will tell you when" because no effect is queued today.
- **A stale question of the router.** On a phone an open question is answered by a plain line only within two hours (`ops_say.ROUTER_QUESTION_TTL`, a constant); older, the next line is new and the old request is cancelled by code, with no notice. Within the window a line that code recognises as a request (`ops_natural.recognises`) is new; a line taken as the answer says so (`ANSWER_TAKEN`) and ends with a plain phrase instead of the `/new` line. The file-save part of the live case is ATT-3's.
- **Review leftovers of LIVE-1.** The section of instructions found in external content lasts until the next field of the template, and inside it a `Q<n>:` counts only when it follows a blank line or a question and is exactly the next in order; a `Recommended:` line after a field line is no question's. The NR-5a cache of time facts is keyed by a digest of the tables' content, bounded, and cleared after every test.

## 2026-10-20: images and files saved to Drive or OneDrive from a messaging app (ATT-3)

The maintainer sent a PNG on Telegram with "Salva no drive" and was told photos are not taken; then a PDF with the same caption got the source-add card and a numbered question for the account. "Images are the basics."

- **Images are save-only holds.** A photo (the platform's word, a JPEG) and a PNG, JPEG, WebP, GIF or HEIC file are held like any file (same size, day and held limits, the bytes must be the declared image) but never converted, never a source, never shown to a model; the hold has no source id, `attachment-add` answers "Images are not added to the project; I can save them to Drive or OneDrive." and leaves the file held, and the grant `add-sources-direct` never adds one. Voice notes, videos, audio, stickers and other images stay refused, with a corrected sentence. AVIF is not taken (its brand is not in the list). A GIF sniffer was added to the intake.
- **One card for each file, not two cards and a question.** "I received <name> (<size>, <type>). What should I do?" with a button for what the sender's profile permits: save to each account the project lists with folders, add to the project (a type that can be a source), discard. Never a second card or a numbered question for the file. The button opens the W4a save card (its approval of the exact content is unchanged). Account words are the service ("Google Drive", "OneDrive"), not "Google/Microsoft" with addresses.
- **The caption only preselects, by closed word lists.** One service named (with a save word or none), or a save word with exactly one account listed, opens the save card at once and no other card is sent; a caption that asks to add to the project, names both services or says nothing gets the one card (a note when it asked nothing the caption can do). If the save card cannot open, the file's card is sent so that no file is left without one. A caption naming a kind the file is not preselects nothing. The lists are `requests.writes.files` of both language tables (`save_verbs`, `bare_save`, `pronouns`, `add_words`, `add_lines`, `discard_lines`, services `drive`, `gdrive`, `nuvem do google`...), no open expression.
- **A later line acts on the last held file.** `salvar no drive`, `manda pro onedrive`, `pode salvar` (save forms with no object, in the infinitive or the imperative), `descarta`, `adiciona ao projeto` (whole-line closed phrases): with one held file of the sender it acts on it, with several their cards come back (no numbered question), with none the sentence is "Send the file and say where to save it." These lines never reach the router. A save word with no service and two accounts brings the file's card back.
- **Constraint found, not changed: a file comes in through `add-sources` alone.** `attachments`, `attachment-hold`, `attachment-add` and `attachment-discard` are one group, and the policy invariant is that an operation is in one group; so a sender who may only write files (`files-write`) cannot send a file to be saved, and a sender who can send one is offered "Add to project" for a document (never for an image). Splitting the hold from the add (a group of its own for sending a file to be saved) is a policy change of its own and is the maintainer's to decide.
- **Cost of the safety.** The writers' media table gains the image types for a save only; the document check refuses an image type in a create or a replace, and a held image is saved only under a name whose extension is the image its bytes are. The bytes are written as they are. Not decided: a discard by saying "no" to a card is the button or the line; a held image that nobody answers expires in 7 days like any file.
- **After the independent review of #489 (2026-10-20).** (1) A photo's generated name carries six characters of the hash of its reference (an album arrives in one second), and a file that was sent is saved as `name (2).ext` when its name is taken (a name the person chose is never changed); a name a sender gave is made one the platform writes (`_` for a character it refuses), and the cut of a long name keeps its extension. (2) A caption that asks to save is never the grant that adds a file as a source. (3) The lines about "the file" (`save_lines`, `add_lines`, `discard_lines`; the loose phrases such as `never mind` and `delete it`, and their Portuguese equivalents, are gone) act only on a file held for less than an hour whose card is in the chat, else they are ordinary lines; the bare save forms (`salvar`, `save it`, `keep it`) are such lines too, and no longer save forms of the pure reader. (4) A caption naming a service the project does not list, or a tap on a removed account, is one message: the file's card with one line that says the service is not set up in this project; a folder list with no `default` opens no question from a caption (the file's card) and a save button takes the first folder listed (the card of the save names it). (5) A service named beats "add" ("adiciona no drive" saves); a caption on several files is read for none and says nothing. (6) A refused save is a plain sentence, never the name of an operation; a second tap on Save sends the open card again; the writers refuse an image type on update and replace; the lobby page draws no "Add as a source" for an image.

## 2026-10-20: the default run of the natural-writes fuzz is a sample (principle 13)

The dialogue fuzz of the natural writes (`runtime/tests/test_natural_writes_dialogues.py`, `test_no_write_is_opened_with_a_guessed_slot`, nine cases of configuration and state) walked
every line of the corpus, now with the mail lines, 50 answers deep one level and two levels from every ninth line: about 465 s on a CI machine, and the `tests` and `python39` jobs
hit the 25-minute limit of a job. **The saving:** the default run walks the requests for work, the first two lines of each operation in each language and one line in seven of the
rest, the starting line rotating with the case: about 110 s for the file in one process (62 s with four workers), a fall of about three quarters. **The loss:** in one run a line of the
rest is walked in the cases whose rotation reaches it (at least one of the nine, not all nine), so a guessed slot that only a particular line in a particular configuration provokes can
wait for the full run. **What stays:** every request for work and every named line are walked in all nine cases; the other fuzz tests are unchanged, and the mail family keeps its own
fuzz (`test_natural_writes_mail_dialogues.py`, every line, 20 answers, four configurations). **The full walk** is `WB_FULL_FUZZ=1` (`runtime/README.md`); the maintainer runs it before a
release and after a change of a language table or of a family, and a failure found only there is added as a named line of the corpus.

After the merge of ATT-3 the files family's verbs also read `Send flowers` and `Envia proposta` as a head. `ops_natural_writes._starts_with_head` now counts a bare verb of a family without `is_head` as a head only when an object noun of the family follows it after the articles (`cria um documento`, `salva esse pdf`, `marca na agenda`): a title that begins with a bare verb stays a title in every family.

## 2026-10-20: the gated operations split in three kinds, and the interpreter's served catalog (IX-C0)

The interpreter experiment (SPEC-IX-PHASE1) reads a line in a person's words with a model and needs to know, for each of the 43 operations of class G (`natural.CLASSES`), whether a sentence may ever lead to it. Class G said only "gated elsewhere", which hides three different things.

- **Three kinds, in `runtime/gated_ops.json`.** `operational` (17): within the policy, asked in words, always behind a confirmation the code writes (`routine-resume`, `instructions-set`, `instructions-add`, `source-refresh`, `source-describe`, `flow-save`, `stage-file`, `sync`, `hand-over`, `dispatch`, `verdict`, `run-next`, `run-task`, `run-step`, `proof`, `revoke-policy`, `stop-runs`; the ceiling of each still decides the channels, so the terminal-only ones stay out of a phone). `widening` (16): never from a channel; the reply is one plain sentence naming the place (D10, POL-1, L62). `internal` (10, the S8 kind): machinery no person asks for, in neither list of the catalog and without corpus items, so the completeness test has an explicit, reasoned exemption.
- **The borderline rows were decided by recommendation (supervisor, reversible), pending the maintainer's confirmation (SPEC-IX-PHASE1 section 6, question 1).** `instructions-set` and `instructions-add` are operational (standing instructions steer every agent, so the confirmation shows the exact text; by today's ceilings they reach only the page, the terminal and chat). `source-import` ("adds content the models read"), `deps` ("installs code on the host") and `config` are widening; `config` is itself a read and can move to operational later, which is one row and the literal lists of the test. `revoke-policy` and `stop-runs` are operational because they narrow.
- **The catalog lists what the model may name.** `ops` (62) and `refused` (21: the five D and the widening G, with their class and meaning but no slots, so that a decision gets the card again and a widening gets the plain sentence). A name in `refused` is not actionable: code never executes it, whatever the model returns (S5).
- **Cost of the safety.** A new G operation must be classified in `gated_ops.json` before the catalog is regenerated, and the epoch of the evidence changes with the catalog's hash. The catalog sha256 at this commit is in the pull request of IX-C0.
- **No path from words.** `source-refresh`, `stage-file` and `hand-over` take a host `file` argument in the table of operations; as operational rows they carry no slot for it in `natural.NATURAL`, their `why` says the file comes only from the page or a held attachment, and a test fails if an operational row ever gets a path-like slot.

## 2026-10-20: the class model:text and the interpreter's routes (IX-2b)

**Decided and built.** A runtime-only class `model:text` (`providers/model/chat-http.py`, `runtime/interpreter_route.py`, `runtime/interpreter_routes.json`) for the interpreter experiment of `SPEC-IX-PHASE1`. 0 model calls, 0 lab runs, the measurement fingerprint unchanged.

- **S3, the file name.** The provider is `chat-http.py`, not `chat_http.py`: the resolver keeps only stems that match `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]*`, so an underscore name never resolves.
- **S7, the key.** The interpreter's key is the existing secret `WB_RUNTIME_KEY_OPENROUTER_API_KEY` (metered, capped); no new secret. `runtime/secrets.json` lists `runtime/interpreter_route.py` among its readers, `contracts/secrets.md` says so. The provider never reads the store: the caller passes the value as `MODEL_TEXT_KEY` in the child's environment, the writers' precedent.
- **D8 route rules, in code.** A route runs only when its sha256 (canonical JSON of the object the provider is shown) is in the request's `accepted` list; the provider refuses before any connection a route that is not accepted, an address that is not `https`, `allow_fallbacks` other than false and `data_collection` other than `deny`; it follows no redirect. The request always carries the `provider` object with those two limits (a route without a pin included), with `order: [pin]` when there is a pin; a reply that names another provider than the pin is `E_PIN` and its text is dropped.
- **Nothing leaves but a code.** On failure the provider prints one line, `chat-http: <CODE>`, and the caller reads only that line: a request, a reply body, a URL or a key is never printed, returned or logged, not even from an exception. No prompt or reply is written to disk.
- **The resolver.** `invoke` and `call` take `stdin` (a `str`): no command line, no temporary file; it is refused with `config`; old `run` stand-ins keep working.
- **Zero data retention is not claimed.** All three routes have `zdr: false` and a `retention` sentence that says so. The public pages read (the usage accounting and zero-retention guides) do not list which pinned provider offers it, and the list is an API endpoint, which this package does not call. A route's `zdr` becomes true only when the public list shows the pinned provider with it; IX-3a checks it on the real route.
- **The residue (L51).** What stays outside the code: how long the pinned provider keeps a prompt under its own terms and OpenRouter's terms. `data_collection: deny` asks for no training use; it is a request to a service, not a guarantee, and the widening prompt of IX-4 quotes the route's `retention` sentence.
- **Cache write field.** OpenRouter documents the cache reads as `prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens` and the writes as `prompt_tokens_details.cache_write_tokens`; the provider reads both, each as an integer or null. The top-level `provider` field that names the serving provider is not on the pages read; the provider treats it as optional (null when absent) and IX-3a confirms it on the real route.
- **Review fixes (same day).** A route's `key` must be on a literal allow-list (the interpreter's own key) and list `runtime/interpreter_route.py` among its readers, so a routes file can never send another registered secret (a subscription token) to a model service; every route must be pinned (D8), the loader refuses `pin: null`. The provider's deadline is a hard timer over the whole exchange (`signal.setitimer`), and the caller's margin over `deadline_ms` is 0.5 s, so a 6 s deadline is a 6.5 s ceiling. A lone surrogate in the text is `E_USAGE` (the caller sends the request ASCII-escaped and answers an encoding error with the code alone, never reading the error's text), and the provider prints its reply ASCII-escaped.

## 2026-10-20: forwarded and quoted messages are never requests (FWD)

A line the person forwards was written by someone else; reading it as the person's request (a forwarded "send an e-mail to ..." opening a card, a forwarded "yes" confirming a reminder, a forwarded caption naming a source) is the one way a third party's words could steer the bridge without the person typing anything. Decided in `SPEC-IX-PHASE1.md` section 1.12 and built in package FWD.

- **The key.** Every messenger update may carry `"forwarded": true`. `messaging_provider.valid_update` always carries the key as a boolean: absent or `false` is false, any other value (a string, a number, null) is true, so an unreadable mark fails closed. `messaging_inbound.parse` makes `{"kind": "forwarded"}` of a forwarded line before the `/` test and every matcher; the bridge answers it with one sentence (`FWD_NOT_A_REQUEST`, in the project's language) and returns before any draft, reminder, question about a write or store is read. A forward neither answers nor drops an open question. A forwarded file is still an attachment intent with `"text": None`: its card opens, its caption never names a source or preselects a save.
- **Detection per provider, each from the platform's own field.** Telegram: `forward_origin`, the older `forward_from`, `forward_from_chat`, `forward_sender_name`, `forward_date`, and `is_automatic_forward`; `quote` and `external_reply` alone never set it (they mark a reply that quotes a message, which is the person's own text). Discord: `message_reference.type` 1, `message_snapshots` or the `HAS_SNAPSHOT` flag. WhatsApp unofficial client: `ContextInfo.IsForwarded` or `ForwardingScore` > 0. WhatsApp Cloud: `context.forwarded` or `context.frequently_forwarded`. E-mail: the subject prefix alone (`Fwd`, `Fw`, `Enc`, `Rv`, `Tr`, `Wg`, `I`, `Vs` after any `Re:`), a forward marker line of the body in English, Portuguese, Spanish, French, German or Italian, or an attached `message/rfc822`.
- **Twilio is not forward-blind.** Twilio's inbound webhook documents `Forwarded` and `FrequentlyForwarded` (read 2026-10-20 at twilio.com/docs/messaging/guides/webhook-request); the provider uses them, so open question 2 of the spec (record a forward-blind provider) does not arise today and no `forward_blind` capability is declared. The documentation and the field names are the evidence; no live Twilio message was sent (a live check of a forwarded message is the maintainer's).
- **Residue, stated.** An e-mail forwarded with no prefix and no marker cannot be told from the person's own words and can open a card; the card of the exact content is the only guard.
- **Cost.** 0 model calls, 0 lab runs, fingerprint unchanged. The Portuguese sentence is for the maintainer to review in the pull request.

## 2026-10-20: the corpus tooling of the interpreter experiment (IX-1, tooling)

The interpreter experiment (SPEC-IX-PHASE1, package IX-1) measures a typed-frame interpreter against frozen labels. This entry is the tooling's; the
data (the dev split, the test split's summary, the frozen taxonomy and thresholds, their hashes) is a pull request of its own.

- **One tool, data apart.** `scripts/interpreter_corpus.py` (verbs generate, adversarial, view, diff, resolve, freeze, relabel, similarity, sample,
  hashes, check), split by job into `interpreter_corpus_format.py` (the item format, the frame's checkable rules, the catalog read as data) and
  `interpreter_corpus_build.py` (the two builders), each under the 1,200-line budget. Standard library only; it reads
  `runtime/interpreter_catalog.json` as data and imports nothing of `runtime/` (scripts never reach the runtime's code). `evals/interpreter/` holds
  data only, and its README is the folder map, the formats, the procedure and the labelling guide L1-L10.
- **Formats the specification left open (by recommendation; the maintainer may revise).** (1) An answer's `span` is a list of accepted values, like a
  slot's, so agreement and the scorer read both the same way. (2) The labeller's opaque id is `w-` and the first 8 hex of sha256 of `<seed>:<id>`;
  `diff` and `resolve` take the view's seed, so no mapping file exists for a labeller to see. (3) `generate` and `adversarial` take `--split` (from the
  base for adversarial) and `--start`, so provisional ids of the three authorship buckets stay apart; `freeze` renumbers each language from 00000 in an
  order by the hash of the provisional id (deterministic, and it does not group the items by author), keeps the relabelled ids, and maps every `base`.
  (4) A weekday value means the next such date strictly after the clock's date; template and value files may come as one file of language sets, a value may carry its own gold text (the digits of an `ints` slot), and a pool may fill no slot (the author session's formats). (5) `check` also holds that a moment is after the clock and at
  most 366 days on, that a forwarded line has the gold `forwarded` and the tag `forward`, and that a multi-turn item has a frame.
- **The dev relabelling is code's output.** `relabel` maps each line of the three older corpora (575 + 414 + 355 = 1,344 items): a time-only line
  becomes a multi-turn item answering a reminder confirmation (frame item `i1`, `age_s` 60), the others time-scoped items; the writes corpora become
  op-scoped items or `route`. Two choices the specification did not state: the channel is `messaging`; and an expected moment outside (clock, clock
  + 366 days] is no acceptable reading (the interpreter's `time-far`), so the one such line (a date 368 days ahead) is `clarify`, tagged
  `far-future`. The schema test rebuilds the file and compares it byte for byte.
- **The similarity check never prints a real line.** It prints `PASS` or `FAIL <count>` (with `--ids`, corpus ids only); a failure to read the file is
  one generic sentence, and no traceback is ever printed. Its test plants a unique marker in a fixture file of real lines and asserts it never
  reaches stdout or stderr.
- **After the independent review of #494 (2026-10-20).** A weekday value on the clock's own weekday is refused (no gold can say which week);
  `check` refuses a moment the zone skips or repeats (time-gap, time-fold); typo, slang and code-switch never touch the time words of
  `adversarial.json`'s `protect` list and skip a base whose time may rest on one outside every span; an agreed item keeps the intent's `also`,
  and `diff` prints `<id> also` when the label's alternatives differ; the view seed is fresh random hex of at least 32 characters (a date seed
  let the opaque ids be walked back to the authorship buckets); `similarity` fails closed (threshold in (0, 1], at least `--min-real` real
  lines, corpus files that pass `check`) and reads each Markdown table cell and a labelled line as candidate lines; a tempt phrase is the D or
  widening-G request alone (a request joined to one is multi-intent, L9); relabelled time-only answers carry the reading and no span; `freeze`
  keeps a base that is a relabelled id.
- **Until IX-2a merges** the schema test skips the check that needs `runtime/interpreter.py` (`frame_problems`); this package merges after it, so
  it runs at merge. Against the served catalog of C0 the relabelled file has 0 problems.

## 2026-10-20: the interpreter's evidence kind and band (IX-E, decisions D3 and D3')

The natural-language interpreter is a model whose output the code checks, and it is not a skill, so the skills' evidence, gate and bands do not describe it. The maintainer approved a new evidence kind (D3, D3') and this entry records how it is built; the rules are section 5 of the reliability model, "Interpreter bands", and `AGENTS.md` and `evals/README.md` say the same.

- **One evidence kind, `evals/interpreter/evidence/ix-<test id>.jsonl`**, written only by the interpreter harness (a later package), append-only like every evidence file (a committed file that differs from the base is refused; a new one is read strictly), with **no free-text field**: event, item and aggregate lines have closed keys and every value is a closed word, a pattern, a hash, an id, a number or a bool. A test split's lines carry item ids and hashes, never the held-out text. Alternative rejected: reusing the lab's `lab-` form with an interpreter "skill" name; it would have put the interpreter in the skills' gate, bands and fingerprint, and its inputs (route, prompt, catalog) are not skill content.
