- **Bands per model, route and language** from the **Wilson upper bound** at z = 1.96, never the point rate: at 50 items a 3% bound cannot be met, and at fewer than 189 items not even a zero count reaches a 2% bound. The current set is emptied by a change of the route object, protocol, prompt, taxonomy, thresholds, served catalog or test split, so a stale run cannot carry a band. Per item the worst of its runs counts.
- **Catalog epochs** (`evals/interpreter/epochs.json`): the test split's evidence counts for the served catalog only; a change of the operations is a `pending-rerun` epoch until the split has run on it. **The cost of the safety:** adding or changing an operation costs one run of the test split before the interpreter's band is `reliable` again; the saving is that no catalog drifts under a measured band.
- **Local lines demote and never promote**; **`go` is computed** (a route that meets every overall condition, the cost included, and beats the current pipeline; the cheapest qualifying route; the languages where the reference route is `reliable`).
- **After the independent review of #493 (2026-10-20).** An item line states the outcome word of its gold (`gold`: `op`, `answer`, `clarify`, `route`, `none`, `refuse`, `forwarded`; a class, never content), because the denominators of `correct_direct` and of the clarify rate leave out the clarify golds and an approximation from the observed word under-counted them (the clarify rate read 0.14 where it was 0.165); a line without it is not valid evidence. Go qualifies a route only if it is reliable, after local lines, in a language where the reference route is reliable, and checks the cost per language; a local `wrong_effect_unconfirmed` demotes the overall band too; the current-pipeline run passes the B-prime set's checks; a set covers the whole held-out split (`test-split.json`: `items`, `counts.lang`), so a sample is `needs a run`; a deleted committed evidence file is refused; `error` is a closed list and `upstream` the route's pin. **Not done:** provenance of an event whose hashes match cannot be checked. The latency and cost come from a sequential sample whose size is published, and go requires at least 100 sequential calls for a route (`SEQUENTIAL_MIN_CALLS` in `evals/interpreter_status.py`: this plan's choice, not measured, because `thresholds.json` is frozen with the corpus).
- **Limits found, not changed:** the status script reads the routes, protocol, prompt, catalog, taxonomy, thresholds and test split as data and imports nothing of `runtime/`. The package changed no fingerprinted file and made no model call; every row is `needs a run` until the first test event.

## 2026-10-20: isolated tests during development, the whole suite at the end of a delivery (TESTSEL)

The maintainer: "It makes no sense to run the whole suite every time we change only a few things. Keep the whole suite for the end of a large delivery; before that, only isolated tests." Until now the hook mapped a staged file to a test FOLDER (any change under `runtime/` ran all of `runtime/tests`, about 23 minutes with several agents on the machine) and CI ran every folder on every pull request.

- **The rule.** During development (every commit, every pull request into an integration branch, every review round) only the **selected tests** run: the tests of what changed plus a fixed set of fast guards. The **whole suite** runs only at the end of a large delivery: a push to `main`, a pull request into `main`, a manual run (`workflow_dispatch`, on the integration branch) and a pull request labelled `full-suite` (the last pull request of a phase); in CI both are the one job `tests`, in two modes. At most 3 heavy test runs share one machine (`WB_TEST_SLOTS`; the hook waits `WB_TEST_SLOT_WAIT` seconds for a slot).
- **The selection** (`scripts/select_tests.py`, its rules at the top of the file; conservative, when unsure it selects more). A changed test selects itself. A changed module selects the tests that import it (a changed test, the tests that import it and theirs), load it by name (`importlib.import_module("x")`, `st.load("x")`, `sys.modules["x"]`) or name its file (`"x.py"`, which also covers a script run by path), the tests named `test_<stem>.py` or starting with `test_<stem>_`, and the tests of every module that references it up to three steps away (`PLAIN_DEPTH`; any form of reference) and, beyond that, only of those that re-export with a top-level `from M import ...` (how `ops.py` re-exports its siblings); a module whose name a data file holds as a whole quoted string (`natural_lang_pt_br` in `natural_languages.json`) also gets rule 4 for that file, and `UNLINKED_MODULES` lists by pattern the data modules (`phrases_*.py`, `natural_lang_*.py`) whose tests no edge finds, with a test that each pattern still matches files. A changed data file selects the tests that name it, and the tests of the modules that read it (a short string without whitespace, outside docstrings), a name shared by several files (`README.md`) counting only with its folder's name beside it; under `interface/` and `flows/` also the tests that name the folder. A `conftest.py`, `pytest.ini`, `pyproject.toml`, a test kit, a module or data file nothing references and a file that does not parse select the whole test folder that owns it (a root `conftest.py` or `pytest.ini` every folder); the selector, the slot helper, `.githooks/`, `.github/`, `packs/`, `templates/` and `agents/` select `scripts/tests`. A change inside a skill other than its `scripts/` selects only the guards (the lab measures skills, the validator checks them). A shared script, its generated copies and the identity tests of the copies go together. The guards (the validator and security-scan tests, the layer map, the runtime rules, the Python 3.9 check, the architecture tables, the wiring of the hook and CI, the test isolation and the selector's own tests) always run, about 20 seconds. When the diff cannot be computed (an unknown ref, a shallow clone) CI selects every folder rather than none. Fuzz files run in their sampled mode; only the whole-suite job sets `WB_FULL_FUZZ=1`.
- **What it saves, measured** (principle 13), in test files of the 597 of this tree (targets, the 15 guards included): a leaf such as `runtime/interpreter_catalog.py` or `runtime/handlers/social.py` selects 16 to 22; `runtime/README.md` selects 40; a document-only change the 15 guards; but a change that reaches `ops.py` is most of the suite, because everything runs through it: `runtime/natural_time.py` 294 (58 when only one step is followed, 209 for two, about 500 for the whole closure), `runtime/phrases_pt_br.py` 365, `runtime/ops_core.py` 411, `scripts/validate.py` 478 at the depth of three. The independent review of the first version found that one step missed tests that really import the changed module through another one (15 tests asserting Portuguese sentences, 27 importers of a test, the language tables), so the depth was raised to three steps (measured against the closure of real imports: three steps leave out 4 of the 266 tests of `phrases_pt_br.py` and 49 of the 264 of `natural_time.py`, tests four or more steps away; the whole closure is most of the suite). The saving is therefore real for leaf and middle modules and small for the hubs: that is the architecture, and the cost is paid knowingly, in favour of safety (principle 13: the stricter rule stays when the saving cannot be shown to exceed the loss).
- **What it can lose, and why the end of a delivery catches it.** A breakage in a test the selector does not link to the change: a module reached only through `import M` two steps away (a long call chain without an import edge), a chain of four or more plain `import`s, a dynamic import whose name is built at run time, a test that reads a document or a data file through a glob it does not name, a skill's `SKILL.md` that a runtime test reads. The fallbacks (a file nothing references selects its folder; an importer of a changed module is always selected) and the guards narrow this; the rest waits for the whole-suite run, where it is more expensive to locate. The supervisor runs the whole suite at the end of every phase (label `full-suite` on the phase's last pull request, or the workflow on the integration branch). **One job, one required name (changed before merge):** the `main` ruleset requires the checks `validate` and `tests`, and GitHub counts a job skipped by its `if:` as passing, so a separate `full-suite` job would have let a pull request into `main` merge with the whole suite red or never run. The jobs `tests` and `validate` therefore have no `if:` at all and are never skipped on any event (a label added to a pull request reruns them: a red check cannot be hidden by a label; the same skip on a label would have masked it), and the steps of `tests` choose the mode: the selection on a pull request into a branch other than `main`, the whole list with `WB_FULL_FUZZ=1` on a push to `main`, a pull request into `main`, a manual run and a pull request labelled `full-suite`; on a pull request into `main` every event, a label included, runs the whole suite. There is no `full-suite` job; `test_checks_wiring.py` evaluates the conditions for eight events and fails on a design that skips `tests` for a pull request into `main`.
- **Not changed.** `python3 scripts/validate.py`, `scripts/security_scan.py --strict` and `scripts/architecture_tables.py --check` run in the hook and in CI as before; the `python39` job is the explicit list it was; `--no-verify` is still the user's decision, never an agent's.

## 2026-10-20: the interpreter's protocol and validator (IX-2a, no model call)

`runtime/interpreter.py` is the security core of the natural-language interpreter (plan `PLAN-NL-INTERPRETER-FINAL.md`, section 1; `SPEC-IX-PHASE1.md`, 1.5 and 1.6): the model's JSON is parsed, then every part of it is checked by code against the line, the frame and the clock, over an unchanged `natural.check_operation` (the test asserts the hash of its source and that it is called as `(op, slots, None, line)`). It is pure: no model, no network, no file written. The prompt (`runtime/interpreter_prompt.txt`) is versioned by its hash and is tuned only on the dev split in IX-3a. What the spec left open, and what was decided:

- **Spans are stricter than the router's rule.** A text slot passes `check_operation` (normalised substring) and must also be a substring of the line as written (case, accents and punctuation as typed; a run of white space may be one space, because the model reads the line with its breaks collapsed). A list of numbers is digits joined by commas and nothing looser. An `inherit` span must be a piece of the frame's recorded words by the normalised rule and as written; a time slot is never inherited; a slot is either given or inherited.
- **`allowed` is explicit.** An op outside the set is `refuse_policy`; `None` allows nothing (fail closed). D and widening-G operations are refused before the policy is asked, whatever the policy says.
- **Who decides the time (D4, the guard of DECISIONS 213), from the whole line.** The model chooses the span, so the source is never read from the span alone (the first review of #495 showed a model unlocking its own time with `when: "Dana"`, a truncated span, or `"no"` in an answer). Code reads the whole collapsed line: `code` only when the span holds every time phrase `natural_time.find` reports in the line, `natural_time.parse` reads the span as one clear reading equal to the line's, and neither `doubt`, an assumed year nor an unread date word (`leftover_doubt`) is present; `model` only when the line has no time phrase, no doubt and no word of a table (three letters or more), or when code read an hour only (no day named), the line holds words no table has and none that a table does (`recuérdame mañana a las 15:00`): the model's date is then used, its hour must be the hour code read, and the card shows the date. Anything else is `time-ask`. The residue, written down: an English line of words no table holds with an hour only (`call Dana at 15:00`) takes the model's date, shown on the card. The model's date must have the weekday it names (`E_WEEKDAY`); every used time, code's or the model's, goes through the project zone (`zoneinfo`: `time-gap`, `time-fold`), must be after now (`time-past`) and within 366 days (`time-far`), and a year outside the last year to two years ahead is asked before any zone arithmetic. When code's reading wins over a different `time_iso`, the decision carries `time_disagree`.
- **Hard limits do not depend on the catalog handed in.** `check` and `build_messages` take the catalog only if its canonical hash is the served `interpreter_catalog.json`'s; the decision (D) and widening (G) operations are refused by `natural.CLASSES` and `gated_ops.json`, not by the catalog. A model `confirm` is accepted only when `natural.confirm_kind` says the same of the line (the answer's own span taken out), else `clarify`; every answer about an item with an op, and every op a `clarify` names, is checked against the sender's policy; an `inherit` span must come from a last request of the same operation; a word slot (an address) must also be as typed; output nested deeper than four brackets is refused by a string-aware scan before any JSON reader; an item's `op` and `slot` are the catalog's in `frame_problems` and `typed_frame`, and a value's code must be a key of the accepted configuration.
- **A line is sent on one line.** Every run of white space, line separators included, is one space, so a sender cannot forge a line of the five-line user message. A line over 1,000 characters is not sent (`precheck`: `route`, or `clarify` `long-line` for a private line).
- **The frame is built by a whitelist.** `typed_frame` copies the keys of the schema only; a value's label comes from the accepted configuration's map; words come from the field `own_words` that the caller marks, never from an item's `words`; a router item is its id, kind, need and age; an item older than a day and a `last` older than an hour are left out. `frame_problems` states the schema and the rules R1 to R7 that code can check, with sentences that never repeat a value. `TIME_SLOTS` is defined in the module until package IX-C's catalog lands; a test compares the two once it does.
- **Residue.** The validator cannot tell a true span from a true lie that is also in the line (the model picks the wrong piece of the line); the cards of the write families are the guard there. The frame schema file and the served catalog are package IX-C's; the tests build the catalog by the rule of its text and compare with the served one when it is in the tree.

## 2026-10-20: the interpreter corpus frozen: the dev split, the test split's summary, the taxonomy and the thresholds (IX-1, data)

The corpus of the interpreter experiment (SPEC-IX-PHASE1, package IX-1) is frozen. No model under test wrote a line or a label, and no model call was
made.

- **The splits.** The dev split is `evals/interpreter/dev.jsonl`, 310 new items: gen 166 (96 from `templates-dev.json`, and 70 from
  `templates-dev-coverage.json` with seed 20261023, one per dev item still missing for a catalog op with fewer than 3, the relabelled items counted),
  opus 72, adv 72; pt-BR 89, en 79, es 51, fr 30, de 30, it 31; 68 hard. With it, `dev-relabelled.jsonl` (1,344 items, code's output). The test split
  is 800 items (gen 320, opus 240, adv 240; pt-BR 280, en 210, es 124, fr, de and it 62 each; 223 hard). It is held out outside the repository until
  the measurement has run; only its summary `evals/interpreter/test-split.json` is committed. Every catalog op has at least 2 test items and at least
  3 dev items. The dev inputs are committed (`templates-dev.json`, `values-dev.json`, `templates-dev-coverage.json`, `adversarial.json` with the dev
  plan, the protected time words and only the word-list entries the dev items used, computed from `dev.jsonl`: the full lists are shared with the held-out test split and are committed only after the measurement ran, so until then `dev.jsonl` cannot be regenerated byte for byte from the committed lists); the test split's templates, values and adversarial plan, the author files, the labels, the decisions and the view seeds are not.
- **How the lines and labels were made.** An Opus author session wrote the templates, the value pools, the adversarial rules and its own lines with
  their intent in a file kept apart. Code generated and derived the rest (seed 20261021 for test, 20261022 for dev). A fresh Opus labeller session
  saw only the view (opaque ids under a random view seed, shuffled, no gold, author or tags), the catalog and the guide, and labelled blind. A fresh
  Opus resolver session decided every disagreement citing a rule of the guide. 0 items were dropped. Its clarifications of L1-L10 are now in the
  guide of `evals/interpreter/README.md`. The adjudication of a subset by a model family that is not a candidate is a model call and is phase 2.
- **The maintainer's checks.** The similarity rule (0.80, both splits against his own file of real lines, run on his machine) printed PASS, and he
  approved all 50 sampled pt-BR test items.
- **The taxonomy and the thresholds are frozen.** The taxonomy gains 23 worked examples (32 in all, at least 3 per rule); IX-2c's scorer test runs
  every one. The C0 test of the taxonomy now asks for at least 30 examples with C0's nine first. The thresholds are unchanged.
- **A limitation of the tool, recorded.** `generate` draws from one generator seeded by the seed and the language, so the same seed at another
  `--clock` gives the same sequence of templates and values (only the moments move). Batches at several clocks take different seeds when they
  must not repeat lines.
- **The hashes** (sha256; `bytes` over the file, `canonical` over the parsed object as spec 1.1 says):
  `evals/interpreter/dev.jsonl` 5457214cc64dde42e15301b51155d40b193964c2393d843dcfe5d230f9898284;
  `evals/interpreter/dev-relabelled.jsonl` f759b30645351f67a0aaf380f127b608db0768c94f8b0fdaad80ebeb00c8833d;
  the held-out `ix-test-v1.jsonl` e9fb5a526c2cd022d2bd26be890582be2f46899094814d643369854b1d87b627;
  `evals/interpreter/test-split.json` f82837d7dec2c3b412bf880027937e4045316a5e31ae0c0e77bbc7c596465dd6;
  `taxonomy.json` bytes b4efce8315a8727d509d5abf4c31e041a8873b2fa0a494d1853fcdea62ec4ce5, canonical
  72d02a19aea3a586d64b48ea2236cd5b8cec2ab27ff1dcb349947bf9c4c06fbb; `thresholds.json` bytes
  1034fc6492e7930ca7fa5ca5bd16af776eeb4924ba7ba3babafafc6c559ef220, canonical 2b15b94c5a78b9aa7b606cebb22ab9693c901bd0b0c48e30428776a5e0f624a1;

## 2026-10-21: the interpreter's measurement harness (IX-2c, no model call)

`runtime/interpreter_measure.py` and its three modules (`interpreter_pipeline.py`, `interpreter_score.py`, `interpreter_evidence.py`), with seven offline test files, run the experiment of `PLAN-NL-INTERPRETER-FINAL.md` (spec `SPEC-IX-2C.md`): this package makes 0 model calls and 0 lab runs, and no fingerprinted file changed. The choices that the merged code and the spec's open questions fixed:

- **The evidence is IX-E's, written by the harness.** The event of the test split and of local lines carries the hash of the BYTES of `test-split.json` (F1; the local event never hashes the maintainer's file, principle 8), a dev event the hash of the corpus file; `items` is the number of items of the corpus file, so a sequential sample event is valid and adds runs (F2); `error` is the call's code and a parse error is only `parse: invalid` (F4); `upstream` is the route's pin when the call succeeded and the provider named one (F5); a test event must use the configured number of runs (F7); the served catalog is always the harness's (F10). `cost_usd` is null when a kind of token has no price (F9: the cache prices of the Anthropic routes are a separate small PR).
- **The B-prime arm** stops at the first stage that decides: a forward (before the length check: a forward is never a request), the precheck, a closed answer (a model confirm only when `natural.confirm_kind` reads the same yes or no), tier 1 and the typed command, then the model. `allowed` is every operation of the served catalog (F12: gold is what the platform should do for the owner); `refuse_policy` is never measured. The mapping from a decision to `observed` exists for measurement alone and never gives `executed`. A private line that asks for work is scored by the frozen rules as a clarification and its count (`private-route`) is printed beside the clarify rate (supervisor decision on open question 2).
- **The current arm is a runtime test** (`test_interpreter_current_arm.py`) on the `lab` world: the router stubbed (`route`), eight lookups stubbed to succeed (account, calendar target, calendar event, mail messages, mail folder, mail account, file to replace, held files; a test proves each is reached and that without it the line would not open a card), five reminders seeded so that a cancel with no question shows as `executed` by a store diff. Three choices not in the spec: an accepted answer to a seeded open item is `answer` and ranks above a card or a question that follows it (the other arm stops at the answer; ranking the card first would punish today's code for the next turn); the family states' own slot names are translated to the catalog's (`STATE_OF`) and the time slot's span is the time engine's own span of the line, so that a card is not wrong for the names of today's internals; a question about the state is recorded as a read of `status`. A `choice` item, a write confirm and a reminder confirm whose `when` has no single reading have no equivalent state and run with no open item: 8 of the 310 dev items (2.6%), counted and stated; up to 5% is accepted (supervisor decision on open question 3). A reminder question is seeded without the day the original line named (the frame does not carry it).
- **Sequential samples** draw only among items that reach the model and write a line (F3), `--sample` on the test split needs `--sequential` (a sample of the split is never a band), and the Phase 2 figures are 60 items twice, 120 calls per route. **Record, resume and replay** keep the raw answers as they arrive (mode 0600, the prompt never written); a replay refuses an answers file whose route, prompt, catalog, protocol or corpus hash differs, makes no call and writes no evidence; a resume keeps a recorded failure instead of paying for it twice. A call that fails with `E_KEY`, `E_ROUTE` or `E_USAGE` stops the run at once.
- **Nothing a person said is printed.** A fault in an item prints the exception's class and the item's ordinal; the lines of a corpus fault name a line number and a field; the summary has counts, categories, ids of the dev and test splits and `E_` codes. The answers of the held-out split must be kept outside the checkout.
- **Not measured, by construction:** the families' seed builders and per-slot provenance, resolution failures, the card's rendering and the code-written confirmation text, the per-sender policy, the rate limit, the daily cap, the circuit breaker, the `nw.read` shadow, and approval by word. `card` and `confirm` mean "code would show this exact operation and these spans".
- **The current arm on the dev split** (0 calls, `billing: none`): the numbers are in the pull request and in the evidence file the harness wrote.
- **After the independent review of #500.** A first call of the process that fails with `E_HTTP` (a revoked key, no credits, forbidden) is a configuration fault like `E_KEY`, and no thread calls after the first fault; a resume calls again the pairs whose recorded failure was transient (`E_RATE`, `E_TIMEOUT`, `E_NETWORK`, `E_UPSTREAM`); a run that would write evidence and had more than 1% of its calls fail writes none, prints the counts and is resumed. The full current-arm run checks that nothing crashed and that at most 5% of the items are unseeded before it writes its event. A forward is read on the messaging channel only, in both arms; an item's `lang` must be a language of the corpus and agree with its id; the harness hash also covers the current-arm test; the alternatives of `also` are part of the matching, so a clarification an item lists as acceptable is `correct` (stated in `evals/interpreter/README.md`); a list of numbers is compared as a set.

## 2026-10-21: the first whole-suite run of the delivery, three failures and their causes (SUITE-FIX)

The whole-suite run (`WB_FULL_FUZZ=1`, GitHub Actions run 39538023816, 29 minutes) had three test failures; none was a defect of the product.

- **The echo over the whole dev file** (`test_interpreter_echo.py`). The set of ids off the mark differs by one id, `dev-en-00040`, and not by the clock: the pipeline and the scorer read only the item's own clock (a run with every wall-clock read made to fail gives the same fourteen ids). The item has the gold `forwarded` on the `page` channel; a forward is read on the messaging channel only (decided with the harness, `evals/interpreter/README.md`), so the item reaches the model, and the echo's `forwarded` object is not a protocol kind. The test of the whole file is skipped without `WB_FULL_FUZZ=1`, so the list had never been checked against the whole file. The id is now listed with that reason, and a test that runs on every run (the nine forwards of the file) checks by the raw input that a forward is off the mark exactly when its channel is not messaging. The corpus itself is the maintainer's to change (a forward on a page has no meaning today); that is a question for IX-1, not a test change.
- **A list request and a report about reminders** (`test_messaging_natural.py`). A race in the test, not in the bridge: the bridge keeps one waiting line per chat (`QUEUE_PER_CHAT = 1`) and answers a second line "still working" while the planning agent's job holds the slot, so lines sent back to back reach the planner or not by the speed of the machine (the third line was refused in the failing run; the same happens with the planner held, checked). The tests that send several plain lines in a row now wait for each job to end (`say_plain`, the `settle` the file already used), in the five tests that had the pattern.
- **The natural writes fuzz** (`test_natural_writes_dialogues.py`, `[none-two]`). The test passes alone (47 s, 108 MB at its peak; two-two takes 87 s) and a worker died in it after more than three minutes on the loaded runner; the cause of the death was not found (the log has no fatal error, no memory sign and no signal). The nine cases are now split, with `WB_FULL_FUZZ=1`, into four shards each (one line in four, by its index; `FULL_SHARDS`), the longest of 36 tests taking 26 s with four workers, so that no test runs near the 120 s at which a worker dumps its stacks and the work is balanced across the workers. The default run is unchanged. If a worker dies again in a short test, the cause is elsewhere and the next whole-suite log is the evidence.

## 2026-10-21: no Claude model over a metered key, and the shape of a key in code (ACCT-B, no model call)

`SPEC-ACCT-ROUTE` sections 3 and 7, package B. 0 model calls, 0 lab runs, no fingerprinted file changed (the measurement fingerprint is unchanged), no skill touched. The rule (maintainer, standing, 2026-10-21): every test or measurement with a Claude model runs on his own Claude account, never through the OpenRouter key; OpenRouter only for DeepSeek. The pilot of the interpreter experiment had sent 48 calls of a Claude model through the OpenRouter key before the rule was written; this package makes the same thing impossible in code (principle 11) instead of leaving it to a prompt or a habit.

- **No Claude model on an HTTP route.** `runtime/interpreter_route.load` refuses the whole file when any route's `model`, `upstream_model` or `pin` contains `claude` or `anthropic` in any case, so no route of it runs. The two OpenRouter Claude rows (`or-claude-haiku-4.5`, `or-claude-sonnet-5.5`) left `runtime/interpreter_routes.json`; the guard also refuses them if they come back. The match is on the three fields as text, a little wider than the spec's (`anthropic/` and a `pin` equal to `anthropic`), because a narrower word list is an easier one to get around.
- **KEY-SHAPE.** A closed table in `runtime/` (`KEY_SHAPES`, OpenRouter: `sk-or-v1-`); a route's `key_prefix` must equal the entry of its address's host and a host outside the table is refused. `interpreter_route.complete` returns `E_KEY` for a resolved value that does not start with the prefix before the provider is started, and `providers/model/chat-http.py` takes a required top-level request key `key_prefix` (4 to 32 printable characters, else `E_USAGE`) and refuses a `MODEL_TEXT_KEY` that does not start with it with `E_KEY` (exit 3) before any connection. The provider names no vendor: the prefix arrives in the request. The route object, its hash and the body sent upstream are unchanged, so the DeepSeek route keeps the hash `1365ea7c385a28f38103f9b2d3b959faee6012691ed6ef50478a560d1014c9fd` and the pilot's DeepSeek evidence stays current. The follow-up of the pilot, a foreign credential stored under the OpenRouter name and sent to openrouter.ai, is the case this closes; rotating that credential remains the maintainer's (question 6 of the spec).
- **An allow-list of models beside the deny-list (after the review of #507).** A deny-list of vendor words is not enough: an OpenRouter alias (`openrouter/auto`), a preset (`@preset/...`) or a router lets the service choose the model, so a pin to a cloud host does not say which vendor answers. `runtime/interpreter_route.py` therefore also has `HTTP_MODELS = {"openrouter.ai": ("deepseek/",)}`: an `http` row's `upstream_model` must start with an entry of its host's tuple (an unlisted host has none), and `model` must be `MODEL_PREFIX` (`openrouter/`) plus `upstream_model`; the vendor-word check stays as a second layer. In `chat-http.py` a reply whose `model` field is present and does not start with the route's model (case-insensitive; a non-text value too) is `E_PIN`, so a service that answers with another model than the route names is refused after the call (defence in depth; the call was made). The route hash is unchanged. **For ACCT-C: the vendor-word and allow-list checks apply to `http` rows only**; an `account` row is checked by its own rules (its `upstream_model` is the vendor CLI's `--model`, its `lab_model` names the gate file's model).
- **`kind` and the reference.** Every row gains `kind` (`http` is the only value this module accepts; the account kind arrives with ACCT-C). The loader takes at most one `reference` route and the file has none until the account route: `evals/interpreter_status.py` already reads no reference as no reference route, and no band or go changes.
- **D8 amended for Claude.** D8 ("every model goes through OpenRouter, each pinned to one upstream, Claude models to `anthropic`") no longer holds for Claude: a Claude model goes through the maintainer's own account (the account route of ACCT-C), and `ALLOWED_KEYS` still admits the OpenRouter key alone, so the account token is never sent to an HTTP route. Every other D8 limit (`allow_fallbacks` false, `data_collection` `deny`, a pinned provider) stands.
- **Tests.** `providers/model/tests/test_chat_http_keyshape.py` and `runtime/tests/test_interpreter_route_guards.py`, with literal oracles (the rows of the spec, the raw routes file, the DeepSeek hash from both modules, `sk-ant-oat01-AAAA` against `sk-or-v1-` with the stand-in counting 0 connections) and a clean twin or a vacuity case for each. The existing tests that used the Claude rows as data (`test_interpreter_route.py`, `test_route_prices.py`, the two measure tests) now use the DeepSeek row.

## 2026-10-21: code never reads an hour with no day for a line that may name one (TIME-GUARD, no model call)

- **The bug.** Replaying the dev pilot found `dev-es-00005` (`agéndame comida con Javier el jueves a las 15:00 en el calendario Gimnasio`): the model's answer equalled the gold (Thursday 2026-10-29), but `_classify` returned `code` and read 15:00 of the clock's own day (Tuesday), a card on the wrong day. Spanish has no time table, so the engine read the hour alone; the branch that hands an hour-only reading to the model (or asks) was skipped because `_known_outside` was true (`calendario` is a word of a Portuguese table). The same held for any line in a language without tables that held a weekday word, an hour and one table word elsewhere.
- **The rule now.** When code's reading has an hour and no date, and the line holds words no shipped table holds (`leftover_doubt(unknown=True)`): with no word of a table outside the time, the model's date is used as before (`model`); with a word of a table outside the time, the source is `ask` (`time-ask`) unless a trigger word of a table stands before the hour in a time phrase the engine read (`at 3pm`, `às 15h`: the line is then in that table's language, and a day word of that language would have been caught by `leftover_doubt` as an unread date word). Everything else in `_classify` was checked for the same pattern: a dated reading is code's own day and an undated one reaches only this branch, so no other branch lets an undated reading win over an unexplained word.
- **The cost, written down.** An English or Portuguese line with a bare hour (no `at`, `às`) and a word no table holds (`remind Dana 15:00`) is now asked where it was `code`: a question where there was a guess, never a card on a wrong day. The residue that remained (a code-switched line, `jueves at 15:00`: a trigger word of a table stood before the hour, so the line passed as one language) is closed by a targeted detector, not by the blunt rule: `runtime/interpreter_day_words.json` is a closed data list of weekday names, month names and relative-day words (today, tomorrow, the day after, next week) of es, fr, de and it, accent-folded, with a few unambiguous abbreviations, read only to detect (`DAY_PHRASES`, `_day_word_outside`). When code read an hour only and the line holds one of these words outside the time phrase, the source is `ask` whether or not a trigger word stands beside the hour. Names and other unknown words keep their behaviour (`remind Dana at 15:00` is `code`). A line with no word of a table at all is still the model's (its date is shown on the card). The list is data: a language is added by a key, and a word that is also a name (`August`) is listed when it is far more often a month, since a question costs less than a card on a wrong day. Tested in `test_interpreter_time_guard.py` (the mixed lines, the names, the file, and a vacuity case with the list emptied).
- **Review of #505 (second round).** The review replayed 22 wrong-day probes (`/review-505/cases*.json`); each is a literal line in `test_interpreter_time_guard.py`. The detector was completed: every weekday abbreviation of es, fr and it (`lun mar mie jue vie sab dom`, `mer jeu sam dim ven`, `gio`; a collision with a name or a word, `Sam`, `Dom`, `mar`, `dim`, is an accepted false ask); lengths of days (`dias`, `jours`, `Tagen`, `giorni`, `semana(s)`, `semaine(s)`, `Woche(n)`, `settimana`, `mes(es)`, `mois`, `Monat(e/n)`, `mese/mesi`: "en dos días", "dans 2 jours", "in zwei Tagen", "tra tre giorni"); the German two-letter abbreviations only with their period (`Mo. Di. Mi. Do. Fr. Sa. So.`, a regex over the `dotted` list; a bare `Fr` or `do` is a word and stays `code`); and `weds` and `2moro` in the English table. A day written as a number outside the covered time spans (a whole number from 1 to 31, or `29/10`, `29.10.`, `29-10`) with an hour-only reading is asked ("remind me on the 29 at 3pm", "lembra 29 às 15h", "remind me el 29 at 3pm"). A DATED reading with a conflicting unread day word or a date written as numbers outside its span is asked too ("remind me tomorrow at 3pm el jueves"); for a dated reading a bare number or a length-of-days word is not a conflict ("tomorrow at 3pm to buy 2 apples", a Portuguese "tomorrow at 15h, pay the month" line stay `code`).
- **Accepted false asks, written down.** A name or a common word that is also in the list: `Jan` (German month), `Julio`, `April` (already asked by the English table before this package), `vie` (Spanish abbreviation; French "life"), `ago` ("sent 2 days ago"), `Sam`, `Dom`, `Mar`, `tag`, and a number that is not a day (`task 12 at 3pm`, `about 2.5 kg`). Most names keep `code` (`remind Dana at 15:00`, `Javier`, `Marta`). Not covered, by choice: languages outside es, fr, de, it (Dutch `donderdag`, Catalan `dijous`, Czech `zítra` are still `code`); add a key to `interpreter_day_words.json`.
- **Measured on the dev split (310 items, no model call).** Against the code before this package: 3 whole lines change from `code` to `ask` (`dev-es-00005`, `dev-es-00016`, `dev-es-00017`) and 4 of the 30 gold time spans (the first two items, two spans each); no pt or en dev item changes. The second round changes no dev item against the first round (0 of 310 lines, 0 of 30 spans).

## 2026-10-21: the account reference is judged on accuracy only (ACCT-D, no model call)

`SPEC-ACCT-ROUTE` sections 3.4, 4 and 2.4, package D. 0 model calls, 0 lab runs, no fingerprinted file changed (`evals/evidence_lines.py` and `evals/interpreter_status.py` are outside the measurement fingerprint), no skill touched, `evals/interpreter/thresholds.json` unchanged (its hash, and so the DeepSeek evidence, stay valid). The maintainer approved the recommendations of questions Q1, Q2 and Q7 of that specification.

- **Evidence (guard 4).** `IX_BILLING` gains `subscription`: `metered` is a key of an HTTP route, `subscription` the maintainer's own account (an account route), `none` the current pipeline. `evals/evidence_lines.py` refuses an interpreter event whose `billing` is `metered` and whose `model` contains `claude` or `anthropic` (case-insensitive), and an item line with a non-null `cost_usd` under any event that is not `metered` (it was `none` alone). The committed evidence file `ix-20261021T025640Z-4cbd16f0.jsonl` still validates. Together with the loader's refusal of a Claude model on an HTTP route (ACCT-B), a Claude model can be measured only on the account, in code at both ends.
- **D3'-a: the account reference is judged on accuracy only (Q1).** For a route of kind `account` the band has the overall conditions of any route but the p95 latency, a sequential event is not required of it, and it is never priced, never chosen as the daily route. A call on the account includes the start of the vendor's tool and costs nothing per call, so `p95_latency_ms <= 4000` and `median_cost_usd <= 0.002` cannot be met or computed on it; both stay required, unchanged, of the route that runs daily. The status computes the p95 whenever a sequential event ran and publishes it labelled "not a condition on an account route" (Q7: Sonnet's sequential sample is published, principle 10). The alternative D3'-b (the full gate for the reference too) would make the go "no" on latency alone and was not chosen.
- **The proxy figure.** Beside the band the status prints the median list-price equivalent of a call: each call's usage (input, output, cache read, cache write tokens) at the price of `runtime/prices.json` (`anthropic/claude-sonnet-5-5`, found by the route's pin and model, then the model, then an alias), with the price's source and accessed date, labelled "proxy, not paid, not a go input". A call without usage, or using a kind of token the price entry leaves out, is left out; without a price table the figure is unknown and the band is the same. The CLI's own cost figure is not read. The status also prints the settings the route measured with ("reasoning low, temperature as the CLI sends it"), read from the row's `params`.
- **Off languages (Q2).** The go's `languages` stay the reference route's `reliable` languages (D3'); the daily route is the cheapest HTTP route that qualifies by every current rule; its `route_languages` run the interpreter, and the other `languages` are `off_languages`: tier 1, clarify-by-code and route, the interpreter off. This is the restrictive default: the reference model is not run daily (it would put the account's token in the production call path, against plan section 4). The output key `off_languages` is `[]` when the daily route serves every language and when the answer is no. The earlier text, "the others run on the reference route", no longer holds, for any reference route.
- **The hash of an account row** is the sha256 of the canonical JSON of every key but `retention` and `reference`; `evals/interpreter_status.py` computes it as `runtime/interpreter_route.py` does (ACCT-C); the hash of an HTTP row (a row without `kind` is one) is the earlier object, so the DeepSeek hash `1365ea7c385a28f38103f9b2d3b959faee6012691ed6ef50478a560d1014c9fd` is unchanged.
- **Tests.** `evals/tests/test_interpreter_account_status.py`: literal oracles (model ids, hashes computed once offline, bands, costs worked by hand) and a vacuity case for each invariant. The tests of the status script, of the evidence forms and of the epochs are unchanged and green.

## 2026-10-21: the account rows of the interpreter's routes, and what the probe found (ACCT-C, no model call)

`SPEC-ACCT-ROUTE` sections 2.1, 2.3, 2.4, 3.5 and 7, package C, with the maintainer's answers to questions Q3 (the Haiku row is kept, for the pilot only), Q4 (extras accepted, listed here and published with the band) and Q5 (the credential is the lab's `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`). 0 model calls, 0 lab runs, no fingerprinted file changed (the measurement fingerprint is unchanged; `adapter.json` and the text runner are outside it), no skill touched. The values below are those of the maintainer's own probe (`run-text.sh --probe`, harness 2.1.283); nothing was probed by the executor, and the probe's own outputs are not committed.

- **The rows.** `runtime/interpreter_routes.json` gains two rows of kind `account`, both with `lab_model` `claude-sonnet-5-5` (so the gate file is not touched), `pin` `anthropic`, `temperature` null, `deadline_ms` 60000 and `runner_version` `run-text/1 cli/2.1.283`. **`acct-claude-sonnet-5.5`**: `model` and `upstream_model` `claude-sonnet-5-5`, `params` `{max_tokens 2000, reasoning "low"}`, `envelope_sha256` `3ef9b0668fc2798353994e820aab652bc745a106b1655353636bad8ff54b232f`, the reference. **`acct-claude-haiku-4.5`**: `claude-haiku-4-5`, `params` `{max_tokens 300, reasoning "off"}`, `envelope_sha256` `7203a61429bff45e54353b8cef836dd38c0307a2a058ece600455b649a75f646`, not the reference, kept for the 50-call pilot only and not for IX-3b (Q3: on the account it can never be the daily route, and its evidence would not hold for a later metered Haiku route, principle 9). `retention` of both: "Sent to the vendor under the maintainer's subscription account terms; zero data retention is not confirmed." The route hashes (the sha256 of the canonical JSON of every key but `retention` and `reference`, equal in `runtime/interpreter_route.py` and `evals/interpreter_status.py`, with a test on every committed row) are `23657eec67a80cb5f4e2ca075cace2acca74c128904addb27eaa3b2838b0aa1b` (Sonnet) and `7a7bcd7764488bbc0fd583b6c989f3dddf4ac543303f5e4d57ade85df8e06d02` (Haiku). The DeepSeek hash `1365ea7c385a28f38103f9b2d3b959faee6012691ed6ef50478a560d1014c9fd` is unchanged.
- **`runner_sha256` and the probe fix.** The maintainer's probe gave `runner_sha256` `96f02aa927a2265841521ae459e68196ddb93904e9dc16312d51b33bb3c02eed`, and it also found that on macOS the system temporary folder's `TemporaryItems` cannot be listed by the user's own process (the operating system's privacy protection answers EPERM, errno 1), so `outside_scan_complete` was false on every run. `scan_outside` in `adapters/claude-code/run_text.py` now counts a folder whose listing fails with EPERM in `outside_protected_count` (a new probe key; counted, never named) and does not let it make the scan incomplete: a folder the system keeps from this process is kept from the harness it starts too (the same user), so nothing the harness writes there can be read here or by anyone through this user. EACCES (errno 13) and every other listing error are unreadable and make the scan incomplete, as before. That edit changes the runner's files, so the value the rows carry is the one computed offline with the runner's own function over `text_files` after the last edit, `e265b8c3c726e1bc4edf82c3f42737359b5197446be04384723c4635ae5a7efd`; a test recomputes it from the files, so an edit of the runner without a new probe fails there, and at the first call of a measure process (the preflight). The `envelope_sha256` of each row does not depend on the runner's files (it is a hash of the request the harness sends), so the probe's values stand.
- **The extras the harness adds (Q4, accepted and published).** The system text and the line reach the model verbatim, in one request (`system_verbatim`, `user_verbatim` true, `messages_requests` 1). The harness also adds text blocks of its own, listed here by place and size, as they were found: Sonnet, `system[0]` 74 characters, `system[1]` 62 characters and `messages[1].content[0]` 329 characters; Haiku, `system[0]` 74, `system[1]` 62 and `messages[0].content[0..3]` 241, 167, 86 and 61 characters. The text of these blocks is not recorded here; the sizes are published beside the band (principle 10), and a change of any of them changes `envelope_sha256` and so the route hash.
- **Deviations from the protocol, stated.** The protocol (`reasoning` off, temperature 0) is unchanged and so is `protocol_sha256`. The harness has no temperature setting, so the account rows say `temperature` null and measure what the harness sends: Haiku is sent temperature 1 (the harness default); Sonnet is sent no temperature, with adaptive thinking (its text omitted from the reply) and effort `low`, because Sonnet 5.5 refuses a request with reasoning off. The status prints "reasoning low, temperature as the CLI sends it" beside the band of an account route (ACCT-D), and the setting is part of the route hash through `params` and the envelope.
- **The call path.** `interpreter_route.complete` on an `account` row refuses a hash that is not in `accepted` (`E_ROUTE`), imports `runtime/lab.py` only then, and calls `lab.account_text(row, system, user)`; the row's `params` and `deadline_ms` are the call's (the caller's `temperature`, `max_tokens`, `reasoning` and `deadline_ms` are not read). `account_text` runs the adapter's text runner (found by the gate file's adapter of `lab_model` and `text_runner` of its manifest): the first call of a process runs the runner's probe once under a lock, with no credential, and compares `runner_version`, `runner_sha256` and `envelope_sha256` with the row; any difference is `E_ROUTE` before the credential is looked up, cached for the process (a CLI update or an edited runner is never measured under an old hash). The credential is found by the lab's own lookup (`resolve_pass_env`, the environment then the OS secret store), the environment is put back as it was (the pattern of `credential_missing`), the value is kept in the memory of the process (one lookup per process, not one per call) and reaches the runner's environment alone, with `PATH`, `LANG`, `HOME` and `TMPDIR`; no other variable of the caller (the OpenRouter key included) is passed. A runner failure is its code from the last stderr line (a code not on the closed list, or any other text, is `E_INTERNAL`) and nothing of the text. `adapter.json` already lists `runtime/lab.py` among the readers of `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` (ACCT-A); `contracts/secrets.md` says so.
- **Account routes are for tests.** `interpreter_route.daily_routes()` returns the rows of kind `http` only; the go and the runtime take their route from it. The loader now wants exactly one `reference` across both kinds (ACCT-B had "at most one"; the file has had none until now).
- **Billing and cost.** `runtime/interpreter_measure.py` writes `billing` from the route's kind (`subscription` for an account route, `metered` for an http route), and `runtime/interpreter_evidence.cost_usd` is None for an account route, so every item line of an account event has `cost_usd` null and the validator (ACCT-D) accepts the file. `harness_sha256` changes with these two files (recorded, never a condition).
- **Two minors of the review of ACCT-D.** `evals/evidence_lines.py` now refuses an interpreter event whose `model` contains `claude` or `anthropic` unless its `billing` is `subscription` (it refused `metered` alone, and `none` slipped through). The early return of `status()` (an input missing) now carries `route_languages` and `off_languages`, both `[]`, as the other returns do.
- **Tests.** `runtime/tests/test_interpreter_account_route.py` (the rows against this file's literals, the hash of every committed row from both modules, the loader's refusals, `daily_routes`, `complete` with a stand-in lab, the lines an account run writes) and `runtime/tests/test_lab_account_text.py` (the preflight, once per process and in parallel, the credential and the environment, the runner's request, every failure code, nothing written), each with vacuity cases; `adapters/claude-code/tests/test_claude_code_run_text.py` (EPERM and EACCES); additions to `evals/tests/test_interpreter_account_status.py`. The tests of the routes (`test_interpreter_route.py`, `test_interpreter_route_guards.py`) and of the prices (`test_route_prices.py`: an account call is paid by the subscription, so it has no price) were adapted to the two kinds.
- **Not verified here.** The first real call of the account (the harness's reply to a real system text and line, its token counts, `finish`, whether a `finish` of `length` occurs on 1 of 50 calls) and the preflight on the real harness are the maintainer's to run in IX-3a; the 2.1.284 harness of the eval image is a different version from the 2.1.283 of the probe and, if used, is probed again.

## 2026-10-21: robustness of the interpreter's account route (ACCT-E, no model call)

Three minors of the independent review of #509 (`SPEC-ACCT-ROUTE` sections 2.1 and 2.4). 0 model calls, 0 lab runs, no fingerprinted file changed, no adapter file touched (so `runner_sha256` is unchanged), `runtime/interpreter_routes.json` unchanged: the `route_sha256` of the three committed rows are the same (the tests hold their literals).

- **m1: the credential cache and the preflight cache are keyed by what they depend on.** `lab.account_text` kept one credential for the process whatever names it was asked for; it now keeps one per tuple of the model's `pass_env` names, so a row whose `lab_model` has other variables looks its own up. The preflight's key gains the row's `lab_model` and the runner's path. With the gate file as it is (one Claude model) nothing changes in a run; the guard is for the day a second model or adapter is listed.
- **m2: a call past its time ends the runner gently.** `subprocess.run(timeout=)` sent KILL to `run-text.sh`, which `exec`s `run_text.py`, so the runner could not send TERM to the harness's process group and remove its temporary folder: the harness could go on running with the token in its environment. `lab._start` now starts the runner with `Popen`, sends TERM at the deadline (`row.deadline_ms/1000 + 5`), waits 3 s (`TERM_GRACE_S`), sends KILL, drains the pipes for 1 s at most and closes them (a descendant that holds a pipe cannot keep the call waiting), and the call is `E_TIMEOUT` as before. The exception it raises carries the command line only: no output, no environment.
- **m4: `lab_model` of an account row is checked at load.** It must start with the vendor's model prefix the module already requires of `model` and `upstream_model`, else the whole file is refused (`RouteError`): a row cannot send the account call through another provider's adapter and credential. Defence in depth; the committed rows (`claude-sonnet-5-5`) are unchanged.
- **Tests.** `runtime/tests/test_lab_account_text.py` (two models with other credential names get a lookup and a preflight each; the same row twice looks up and probes once; a stand-in runner that traps TERM writes a marker and the call ends within the deadline plus the grace; one that ignores TERM is killed after the grace; a descendant holding the pipes does not hang the call; the end-to-end `account_text` is `E_TIMEOUT` with no token in the result or the output) and `runtime/tests/test_interpreter_account_route.py` (a `lab_model` of the floor model or another provider is refused; the committed hashes are the literals).

## 2026-10-21: the IX-3a dev pilot evidence and its answers are published (IX-3a, no model call here)

Five dev events of arm `bprime` are committed under `evals/interpreter/evidence/`, each with its complete answers file under `evals/interpreter/replay/<route>/<iteration>/` (the folder form `interpreter_measure.py --replay` reads: one answers file per folder; each answers file has an exact-path `english-only` line in `.security-scan-allow`, as `evals/interpreter/README.md` says). Principle 10: a result is published whether it passed or not. Every event is a sample of 50 dev items (seed `20261025`), 1 run, not sequential; the corpus and catalog hashes are the dev corpus's. The figures are the categories the evidence lines state (50 item lines each); `correct_direct` is the share of the 47 items whose gold is not a clarification that came out `correct` (the three items whose gold is a clarification are `correct` too and are left out of it); `clarify` is the count of `clarify` lines (a clarification no alternative of the item lists), wrong is the count of `wrong_*` lines. None of these events is test-split evidence: the standing in `python3 evals/interpreter_status.py status` stays `needs a run` for every route, and no go follows from them.

| Test | Route (model) | Billing | Iteration | Prompt hash | correct_direct | clarify | wrong |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `20261021T124445Z-ea9f0c2f` | `or-deepseek-v4.1-flash` | metered | 1 | `c492b43b` | 43 of 47 (0.915) | 3 | 1 (`wrong_card`) |
| `20261021T124738Z-d0fc5d83` | `or-deepseek-v4.1-flash` | metered | 1 | `c492b43b` | 43 of 47 (0.915) | 2 | 2 (`wrong_card`) |
| `20261021T155019Z-f148397b` | `or-deepseek-v4.1-flash` | metered | 2 | `5bee55ce` | 43 of 47 (0.915) | 4 | 0 |
| `20261021T184159Z-cda87ebe` | `acct-claude-sonnet-5.5` (`claude-sonnet-5-5`) | subscription | 1 | `5bee55ce` | 45 of 47 (0.957) | 2 | 0 |
| `20261021T184159Z-7ceca7ad` | `acct-claude-haiku-4.5` (`claude-haiku-4-5`) | subscription | 1 | `5bee55ce` | 29 of 47 (0.617) | 15 | 3 (2 `wrong_card`, 1 `wrong_item_binding`) |

- **Replay.** The three files whose prompt hash is `5bee55ce` (the checkout's) replay with 0 live calls and give the evidence's categories exactly (`--runs 1 --sample 50 --seed 20261025`, the values their headers state). The two iteration-1 files carry prompt `c492b43b`, which the checkout no longer has: `--replay` refuses them by design (the `prompt_sha256` differs), so they are published as a record and not as a replayable input.
- **Reading the two Claude rows.** Sonnet: 48 of 50 lines `correct`, 2 `clarify`. Haiku: 32 of 50 lines `correct`, 15 `clarify`, 3 wrong; 45 of its 50 answers were fenced (`parse: fenced`) and 3 `invalid`. The account events carry no cost (`cost_usd` null: the subscription pays) and their latencies are an account harness's, not a route's.
- **Not published.** The attempts of the same pilot that went to Claude models through OpenRouter (`replay/or-claude-*`) are not committed: the maintainer forbade that route for Claude and had those answers deleted. Failed starts (answers files with a header and no call) are not published either; they hold no result. The sixth evidence file of the folder, a later `bprime` event not part of this pilot, is not part of this entry.
- **Principle 8.** The corpus is fictional; the five answers files and five evidence files were checked for emails, hosts, keys and phone numbers: the only address is a `.example` one from the dev corpus, every `span` an answer quotes occurs in `dev.jsonl`, and the rest is hashes, dates and costs. Tooling wrote every file; none is edited.

## 2026-10-21: the IX-3b test-split evidence, the spent split and its answers are published, and the maintainer proceeds with B-prime (IX-3b, no model call here)

The IX-3b test ran on 2026-10-21 on the held-out split `ix-test-v1` (800 items, frozen 2026-10-20, sha256 `e9fb5a52...d87b627`, the hash `evals/interpreter/test-split.json` states). Five test events are committed under `evals/interpreter/evidence/`, the split itself as `evals/interpreter/ix-test-v1.jsonl` (the file the other dev files sit beside; its bytes hash to `test-split.json`), and the four recorded answers files of the two routes that ran as `evals/interpreter/replay/<route>/test-full/` and `.../test-seq/` (the folder form `--replay` reads: one answers file per folder). Principle 10: a result is published whether it passed or not. The split is **spent**: it was read by a measurement, so it is published and no further route or prompt is tuned against it; a new measurement needs a new split (the backlog item IX-1B names `ix-test-v2`). Tooling wrote every file; none is edited. The Haiku route was dropped from IX-3b by the maintainer, so it has no test event and its band stays `needs a run`; the spec's seven evidence files are five.

Each route's full event is 800 items, 2 runs, not sequential (1,600 lines); the sequential event is a sample of 60 items, 2 runs, seed `20261026` (120 lines). The standing reads each item as its worst run, and a route's items are the worst over its full and sequential events (the numbers `python3 evals/eval_status.py interpreter` prints); `correct_direct` is over the 723 items whose gold is not a clarification, `clarify` counts items whose worst run was a clarification no alternative lists.

| Route (model) | Billing | correct_direct | clarify | wrong_card (items of 800) | wrong_card Wilson upper bound (limit 0.02) | wrong_effect_unconfirmed (limit 0) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `or-deepseek-v4.1-flash` | metered | 627 of 723 (0.867) | 67 (0.093 of 723) | 32 (0.040) | 0.0559 | 0 |
| `acct-claude-sonnet-5.5` (reference) | subscription | 643 of 723 (0.889) | 70 (0.097 of 723) | 10 (0.0125) | 0.0229 | 0 |
| current pipeline (arm `current`, 0 calls) | none | 155 of 723 (0.214) | 20 | 6 (0.0075) | 0.0163 | 1 |

- **The full events alone.** DeepSeek: 628 of 723 `correct_direct`, 68 `clarify`, 32 `wrong_card` (Wilson 0.0559). Sonnet account: 643 of 723, 70 `clarify`, 9 `wrong_card` (Wilson 0.0212); the tenth Sonnet item comes from the sequential sample. The other wrong categories of the full events (items): DeepSeek `wrong_reminder_reading` 10, `missed` 10, `wrong_item_binding` 3, `wrong_route` 3, `wrong_read` 1; Sonnet `missed` 2, `wrong_reminder_reading` 1, `wrong_item_binding` 1. The current pipeline's items: 525 `wrong_route` (the pipeline answered `route` where the gold was another outcome), 46 `wrong_item_binding`, 28 `wrong_reminder_reading`, 10 `missed`.
- **The computed go is false.** `python3 evals/eval_status.py interpreter --go` prints `go: no (the reference route is not reliable in any language)` and exits 1: both routes are `below the gate` in every language, with the cause `wrong_card Wilson upper bound is 0.0559, over 0.02 (32 of 800)` for DeepSeek and `0.0229, over 0.02 (10 of 800)` for the Sonnet account route. The Sonnet route misses the bound by 0.0029; the daily route misses it by 0.0359. The sequential samples are published and not a band (60 items): DeepSeek 2 `wrong_card` items, Sonnet 1; the Sonnet account route's p95 latency is 6,618 ms over 120 sequential calls (not a condition on an account route); its list-price equivalent is 0.00177 USD per call, a proxy that is not paid and not a go input.
- **The maintainer's decision.** On 2026-10-21 the maintainer, who has these numbers, decided to proceed with B-prime anyway: the integration is Phase 3 of the plan, the daily route is DeepSeek (`or-deepseek-v4.1-flash`) and the account route is test-only. This overrides the computed go and nothing else: **the published band stays `below the gate`** for every route and language, the thresholds in `evals/interpreter/thresholds.json` are unchanged (the 2% Wilson bound is not loosened to fit), and `go` stays false in the computation. The way back under the bound is the backlog item IX-1B (guards in code, then a new split and a new run).
- **Replay.** With the checkout's prompt, catalog and harness hashes, `interpreter_measure.py --replay` of each of the four answers files (from a folder outside the checkout, as the test split requires; `--runs 2` for the full ones, `--runs 2 --sample 60 --seed 20261026 --sequential` for the sequential ones) makes 0 live calls and gives the evidence's category counts per language exactly. A few answers are recorded failures (`E_TIMEOUT`: 2 in the DeepSeek full file, 1 in the Sonnet full file, 1 in the DeepSeek sequential file), kept as recorded and under the 1% rule that lets the evidence be written.
- **Principle 8.** The split and the answers are fictional: the only addresses are `.example` ones, there is no URL, key, phone number, home path or handle; every string a slot quotes occurs in the item's line, except lists of ids (`10,11`, `2,9`) and a few phrases the model rewrote, all invented. Three of the new data files carry an exact-path `english-only` line in `.security-scan-allow` (the split and the two full answers files; the two sequential files and the evidence files need none).

## 2026-10-21: the interpreter reads the plain lines of a messaging sender (S1, no model call, no lab run)

The maintainer confirmed the three recommendations of `SPEC-IX-PHASE3` section 3 on 2026-10-21 and decided to proceed with the interpreter although the computed go is no (IX-3b: `wrong_card` Wilson upper 5.59% for DeepSeek and 2.29% for the reference, limit 2%; correct per item 84% against 21% for the current pipeline; actions without confirmation 0). 0 model calls, 0 lab runs, no fingerprinted file, no skill.

- **Enabling is a dated decision in a committed file.** `runtime/interpreter_enable.json` (read by `runtime/interpreter_enable.py` and, as data, by `evals/interpreter_status.py`) holds entries with `basis` `maintainer-decision`, the date, the route (`or-deepseek-v4.1-flash`, taken from `daily_routes()`, kind `http`), the hashes of the route's object, the prompt (`5bee55ce`), the served catalog and the protocol, the languages (all six, D3' superseded for now) and the reason. Only the NEWEST entry counts, and only while every hash equals the checkout's and the project's language is listed: a change of the prompt, the catalog, the protocol or the route turns the interpreter off until a new dated entry. The status prints "enabled by maintainer decision 2026-10-21, below the gate" beside the route's band and never moves a band. The account route is unreachable from this path: the one call site checks the kind again.
- **Q1, hybrid order.** The code that claims a pt/en line keeps it (a typed command, a question about the state, tier 1, a write or a reminder in the words of the tables, the answer to a draft); the interpreter reads the rest; no hook in `messaging_bridge.py`. The order can be scored later by replaying the recorded answers (0 calls).
- **Q2, a model failure.** A line that is not private, in a project in pt-BR or en (the only tables that detect private words), goes to the planning agent as before; any other line gets one fixed sentence (`IX_OUTAGE`).
- **Q3, who gets it.** The group `interpret`, per sender or per channel list, in no default, a kind-only group of the messaging channel (it adds to `talk` and never grants it); `accept-config --widen` prints the daily route's retention beside it.
- **S1 choices, by recommendation and reversible.** Limits in memory: 10 calls per sender per 60 s, 300 per project per local day, a breaker open 300 s after 3 failed calls in a row (the provider's 6 s deadline is the call's). `none` is answered by code and not routed. The line is stored only after the decision, as a neutral word unless it went to the planning agent. A router reply that names no route is one plain sentence on a phone (`PHONE_NOT_UNDERSTOOD`) and its request is cancelled by code (`code:unclassified-phone`). The interpreter's decision for a recorded answer equals the measured pipeline's (`ops_interpret.decide`), and the replay of the three recorded development answer files at prompt `5bee55ce` is a regression test (`runtime/tests/test_interpreter_replay_gate.py`).
- **Where code cannot enforce it (limit L63).** What the model service keeps of a line is the route's terms; a wrong card is caught only by the person who reads it before approving it; a private word in es, fr, de or it is not detected, so a reminder or write in those languages may reach the model service.
## 2026-10-21: a decision card is decided by a word, and a table command on a phone is a sentence (S2, no model call)

The maintainer typed "Aprovo" under a calendar card on Telegram and nothing happened (only the button approved), and "/cancel 8" answered the raw JSON of the operation. This slice takes the salvaged `card_word` of the stopped conversation-layer work (decision 233; the spec's section 3.5 and limit L64) as a small standalone module and wires it into the bridge, without the rest of that layer. 0 model calls, 0 lab runs, no fingerprinted file changed.

- **The words** (`runtime/card_words.py`, pure, standard library): the whole line must be one closed approve or reject form; polite words, an article with a number or an ordinal, or "all" may come with it; an open tail is never a form ("pode marcar amanha as 3" is a request). The maintainer's own list ("sim", "ok", "aprovo", "aprova", "pode", "pode marcar", "pode mandar", "pode salvar", "manda ver", "aprovado" (+ "pode aprovar"); "yes", "ok", "approve", "approved", "go ahead"; "nao", "rejeito", "rejeita", "cancela", "deixa"; "no", "reject", "cancel") is in the module's own lists because the language tables (`natural*.py`) were another slice's files; the ordinals, articles and "all" are this slice's choice.
- **When a word decides** (`Bridge.word_line`; every condition, checked again by `word_act` against the ledger and the decision as they are now): a private line of a listed sender in the card's chat, not forwarded; the card is the LAST message the platform sent to the chat (`Bridge.last_sent`, memory only, set by `deliver` for every message and emptied while a send is in flight); it is the only card a word could decide (sent whole, offering the act), else the numbered list is sent and nothing is decided, and a position within 600 seconds, while the list is the last message, decides; "approve all" is never done and "todos nao" rejects each card of the list; the decision is open and its hash is the card's; then `Bridge.act`, the button's own path (the policy for the sender, the hash, the same operation). A word sent as a reply to a card decides that card, last or not; a platform whose acts are replies (e-mail) decides only by a reply. The sentence that refuses "approve all", and the doubt or rolled-day line a write says in the same turn as its card, leave the card the last message (`keep_cards`), so the live case (a card, then a note, then "Aprovo") works. Anything else goes on unchanged: no resend, no hint.
- **No buttons.** `messaging_cards.build` records the acts a card offered (`offers`, in the ledger) and, on a platform with no buttons, ends an approvable card with `REPLY_WORDS_LINE` ("Reply yes to approve or no to decline." / "Responda sim para aprovar ou nao para recusar.") in place of "Send /approve 12" and "Send /reject 12".
- **Phone replies as sentences.** `Bridge.command_sentence` turns the JSON that `say` returns for `/cancel`, `/stop-task`, `/retry`, `/status` and `/attachments` into one sentence of the project's language (`CMD_*` phrases: "Request 8 cancelled." / "Pedido 8 cancelado."); a result it has no sentence for is "Done."; the number is the one the person typed. `ops_say.py`, which writes the JSON, is not touched.
- **Deviations from the spec, each the smaller safe choice.** The phrase key is `REPLY_WORDS_LINE`, not `CARD_REPLY_WORDS` (the prefix `CARD_` is the family of a stored card's lines, `phrases.CARD_FAMILY`). The time limit is the list's 600 seconds; a single card has none beyond "last message and still open" (the spec gives none). The list shows the kind and the title as `/pending` does, with no id. After "approve all" is refused the list or card stays the last message (`keep_cards`), so the next word can still decide. "aprovo o 2" with one card open, and a reply with a position or "all", decide nothing. The limit is L64 and the rules are 60 and 61 of the messaging page, the numbers the conversation-layer spec reserved; L63 and rule 59 belong to that layer and are not written here.
- **After the independent review (same PR).** (1) No weekday and no hour is a position: the feminine ordinals that are days ("segunda", "quarta", "quinta", "sexta") are gone, "a" is an article only before an ordinal word (never "a 1", the accented "\u00e0 1" of a time), and "pode", "pode marcar", "pode mandar" and "pode salvar" take no position at all. (2) `last_sent` is stored by the send that started last, under a lock and a per-chat sequence (`Bridge.deliver`), and `keep_cards` and the list are put back or recorded only by a compare and set when nothing else was sent to the chat since (`Bridge.reply_alone`); tested with a fake platform whose sends the test finishes. (3) A card that is not whole (notices `count`, a cut body) on a platform with no buttons keeps the typed lines, because a word decides only a whole card. (4) A sentence made from a command's result passes the net of `messaging_guard`, a held task's reason is one cut line, and a number in it is a whole number of the result or the one typed.
- **Tests.** `test_card_words.py` (the words, against the test's own oracle lists, a walk of about 12,000 generated lines, a vacuity case), `test_messaging_card_words.py` (the guard of L64: one condition broken at a time, the list, the reply gesture, an effect, no buttons, e-mail-shaped, a vacuity case), `test_phone_command_sentences.py` (both languages, no JSON character, a vacuity case); `test_messaging.py` and `test_runtime_language.py` change only the no-button card's footer.

## 2026-10-21: the router's question is bound to its asker for an hour, and the interpreter answers it by a typed frame (S4, no model call, no lab run)

The maintainer's decision 229 (1) of 2026-10-20 (one hour, bound to the asker) is built, with the scope the supervisor took from the recommendation of `SPEC-IX-PHASE3` section 5.6: the router question is the only frame item. A reminder draft, a write draft, a file choice and `choices` as buttons wait for IX-6, because the bridge consumes those drafts before the operations layer sees the line.

- **The binding.** `runtime/pending_items.py` (salvaged from the stopped CONV-1a store, reduced to the one kind this slice uses) keeps, in memory, one binding of the router's question for a project, a channel and a sender. `ops_say._bound_question` answers only when the key has a binding whose decision is still open, still the newest question of its request, and less than `ROUTER_WINDOW` (3600 s) old by the store's clock. Another sender, another channel, a later line and a restart find none, and the line is a new request. Nothing is cancelled by code when the hour runs out (`ROUTER_QUESTION_TTL` and `code:stale-question` are gone); the old request stays `requested` and its question stays open. Page, terminal and chat keep the conversation's question as before.
- **The typed frame.** A line that code recognises (`ops_natural.recognises`: a request of its own, the answer to the sender's draft) is a new line before any frame, for the interpreter's senders too (the review of #515; this supersedes the wording of `SPEC-IX-PHASE3` 5.1 (4), which skipped it): the interpreter decides only the lines no code list claims. For those the model is shown one frame item, `i1` (kind `router-question`, need `text`, age), and never the question's words or number. `answer` of `i1` sends the line to the router as the answer (told with `ANSWER_TAKEN`); `route` is a new request and replaces the binding; an operation is carried out with the question left open; `clarify`, `none` and refusals say S1's sentences with the question left open; with no decision the earlier rule applies (a request code recognises is new, else the answer). A new line that code recognises is claimed by code first, as it is without a question.
- **A reply is stored under the request it closed.** The replies to a line whose request was cancelled by code (`code:unclassified-phone`, `code:direct`, `code:operation`) and the drains of the page's queue are stored with that request (`extras["task_id"]`), so `_last_request` no longer falls back to an older request whose open question would take the next line. The reply of an `ask` keeps no request (limit L50). Such a request is marked by a cursor (`conversation:code-closed:<id>`, written by `_close_by_code`), and `chat_memory` neither reads its reply as a settled exchange nor includes it, so the router's memory is what it was before (the review of #515 found the first version resetting it). The `ask` reply is stored the same way (the display keys on its run, limit L50).
- **Whole-line confirmations.** `natural_lang_en.py` `requests.yes` gains vale, claro, de acuerdo, dale, d accord, bien sur, volontiers, genau, klar, gerne, jawohl, va bene, certo, volentieri; `requests.no` gains nee, nicht, pas question, no gracias, non merci, nein danke, no grazie. They confirm or refuse a reminder the interpreter opened (a whole-line equality on the normalised line). The maintainer may strike any.
- **By recommendation, reversible.** A binding lost on a restart makes the next line new (as every item in memory). The bridge line that records the question's message id (the id of the last piece of the reply, kept on the sending thread by `reply` and passed to `note_asked`, because the shared `last_message` can be changed meanwhile by another thread's send) (`note_asked`, after S2 merged) lets a reply gesture to the question answer it at any age through the existing reply path, which is decision 229 (1)'s "afterwards by reply-to".
- **Where code cannot enforce it (limit L63).** A free-text line that is meant as new work can still answer a live question within the hour; the hour, the binding and `ANSWER_TAKEN` are the protection.

## 2026-10-21: the planning agent's questions are readable on a phone (S9, no model call, no lab run)

The maintainer read the router's question on Telegram in a Portuguese project: the questions were glued together, `Q1:` and `Recommended:` were English, a stray code fence closed the text and the name of a flow (`flow-brand`) was shown. This slice changes how code draws the question; the router's prompt, its output and the agent files are not touched, so the measurement fingerprint does not move.

- **Layout.** `runtime/phone_questions.py` (pure, standard library) renders what `phrases.read_question` reads as `Perguntas:` / `Questions:` and numbered items, the recommendation on the line below (`Recomendado:` / `Recommended:`), one blank line between the items and before the closing sentence. `phrases.asked_view` calls it, so the reply to a line (`ops_say._say_route`) and the notice card (`messaging_cards.build`) change together; the page, the terminal, the stored decision and the card's hash keep the whole reply.
- **Clean-up** (`clean`, also the fallback when no question can be read: the text is cleaned, never guessed): code fences (fence-only lines, then any fence left in a line) go; blank lines collapse to one; the fixed labels Route, Why, Next, Question and Recommended are said in the language; the name of an installed skill or flow is replaced by a plain label from the closed `PQ_NAME_` family of the phrase table (twelve flows and eight skills), and a name with no label is dropped with the parentheses around it; slash commands, paths to files and requirement classes (`integration:calendar`) are removed (limit L62). Only names that are folders of `skills/`, files of `flows/` (as `flow-<name>`) or labelled are touched, so "product-market fit" stays.
- **The closing sentence** is `Responda numa mensagem simples.` / `Answer in a plain message.` The second sentence ("a request I recognise is never taken as an answer") is dropped from the phone text; the rule is the same (S4) and is in the contracts.
- **Phrases.** New keys `PQ_LABEL_*` and `PQ_NAME_*`; the values of `ROUTER_ASKS_FRAME` and `ASK_NEXT_PHONE` were changed instead of adding two keys that would leave the old ones unused. By recommendation and reversible.
- **Where code cannot enforce it.** A name the router invents that is not a folder of `skills/` or a flow file is shown as written; the table of labels is small on purpose and a new flow gets its label by adding a phrase key.

## 2026-10-21: a greeting gets a greeting back and a summary of what the assistant can do (S5, no model call, no lab run)

The maintainer sent "Ola" from his phone and was told that no request was seen in the message (`IX_NONE`) The line reached the interpreter, which found no request in it. A greeting is now answered by code, never by the model, and the interpreter's prompt (pinned by `runtime/interpreter_enable.json`) is untouched.

- **What a greeting is.** `runtime/greeting.py` (pure): the whole line is made of closed phrases of the six languages (pt-BR, en, es, fr, de, it): a greeting ("oi", "bom dia", "hello", "hola", "bonjour", "hallo", "ciao", "tudo bem?"), a request for help ("ajuda", "o que voce faz", "help", "what can you do") and polite fillers, with accents, case, punctuation, emoji and a stretched last letter ("oiii") ignored. A line that goes on to ask for anything ("oi, lista os emails", "bom dia, me lembra de X") is not a greeting. With no tag every list is read (a person writes in any language whatever the project's); the lists are this module's own data, as `card_words.py`'s are.
- **Where it sits.** In `ops_say._turn`, right after `talks` is known and before the open question, the state, the tier-1 forms and the interpreter's seam: for every sender who holds `talk`, interpreter enabled or not, on every channel. A greeting is never taken as the answer to the router's open question (as a request code recognises is a new line, LIVE-2), takes no route lock and is never queued (`ops_say._greets`). A sender who may not talk reaches the refusal of today.
- **The reply.** `ops_say._greeting_reply`: a greeting in the project's language, then one plain line for each family of capabilities the sender's policy permits on the channel the line came on (`operations.permitted`: mail reading by `ask`/`mail`, `remind`, `event-create`, `mail-send`, `mail-draft`, `file-create`), the approval of a card by a word when an `approve` kind is permitted on a messaging app, and the line about asking in one's own words when `ops_interpret.enabled` holds. A policy that lacks a family never mentions it; a sender who may use none gets the one line that says a request may be sent in a sentence. No slash command and no id (L62); it reads no store and no source. The phrases are `GREET_*` in `runtime/phrases.py` and `runtime/phrases_pt_br.py`; a tag with no table gets English, as every phrase does.
