- **The phone.** The bridge answered a help line in words ("ajuda", "help", "what can you do") with the fixed list of commands, which carries slash commands. From a sender who holds `talk` it is now the greeting (`Bridge.greets`; `Bridge.needs` asks `talk`, not `read`); a sender who may not talk keeps the fixed list.
- **Storage.** The line and the reply are stored as any code-claimed line and reply are (limit L63 keeps only what the interpreter reads, and a greeting is not sent to it); a greeting holds no private data.
- **By recommendation, reversible.** The reply speaks the project's language, not the language the greeting was written in; the sender in an `es`, `fr`, `de` or `it` project gets English until those columns exist. No contract changes: no limit is touched.

## 2026-10-21: an event is named by its words, and a write that cannot be prepared says what was understood (S6, no model call, no lab run)

The maintainer's Telegram test (a pt-BR project, the interpreter enabled): after an event was created he wrote that he wanted to remove it for tomorrow and was told to ask in Portuguese or in English, a false sentence for a Portuguese line. The cause was in `ops_interpret._carry_out`: a change or a cancellation reaches the family only through the number of the event in the line, and when the family read nothing the fallback always said `IX_WRITE_LANGUAGE`.

- **The event is found by code from the person's own line.** `runtime/event_lookup.py`: the calendar snapshots are read as the numbered form reads them (the group `ask-mail`, a snapshot younger than 26 hours, hashes checked), the candidates are the events of the next 30 days (the named day alone when the line names one), and an event fits when its title, folded, is whole words of the line (for a change, of the line without the spans that hold the new time and the new title, found in the line with its white space collapsed, a span not found being a question; the words the time engine reads as a day or a time, and a confirmation word at the edges of the line, are not matched against a title). One fits: the card opens through the family with a seed of the source and the number, the same card as "cancela o evento 2", approved by its hash as always. Several: a numbered list with the snapshot's own numbers. None: one plain sentence. The new time of a change is only the code-verified time span; its day is not the old event's day.
- **A day code cannot read is asked.** A date word the engine doubts, or finds and cannot turn into a day, gets a question, not a guess. A word for a day that the engine does not know ("ontem", "semana que vem") narrows nothing: the list and the card show the day, and the card is approved by its hash. Residual risk, by recommendation.
- **The language sentence is true only where there are no write forms.** `IX_WRITE_LANGUAGE` is said for a project language outside pt-BR and en; there, a write that cannot be prepared is answered by one sentence that says what was understood and what is missing (`IX_CANNOT_EVENT_CREATE` for an event with no time, `IX_CANNOT_WRITE` for any other write).
- **Not done.** The number the model gives in a slot is not used (a number in the line may be a day: "dia 22"); "cancela o 2" without the word "evento" is still not the numbered form.
- **A miss reads the calendar again, once (the maintainer's "Sim", 2026-10-21).** When nothing fits, or every snapshot is older than its limit, `event_lookup.refresh` calls `ops_sources.source_refresh` once for each registered calendar source, which is the provider read the snapshots come from (`sources_mail.snapshot_refresh`; `step_ingest.py` calls it the same way), and the search is made again; still none is the not-found sentence, and a failure is dropped (no error text on the phone). Only a sender who already passed `item_refs.may_read` (the group `ask-mail`) gets here: no new group, no row of the operations table (`source-refresh` stays a terminal and page operation; this is an internal call of the read the sender is already allowed to see). The refresh writes a new snapshot version of the person's own calendar where snapshots already live (the sources folder and the store's source rows), only when the listing changed; nothing of the line is stored. One refresh per line, never a loop; the interpreter's own limits (10 calls per sender per minute) bound how often.

## 2026-10-21: a file is read by its name from the account's configured folders (S8, no model call, no lab run)

The maintainer's use of 2026-10-21 showed two lines the platform could not answer ("bring me the content of the file X", "read the file named X from my OneDrive"): a Drive or OneDrive file was readable only as a project source. He approved, the same day: "Yes: build S8, read a file by its name in the folders already configured for the account, before the release" (translated from Portuguese). Sources stay what they were; the folders do not become sources.

- **The operation.** `file-read` (class R; `file`, a span of the line, and `service`, one word of it) in `runtime/ops_file_read.py`. Code finds the file: only a file whose name equals the given one (`natural.normal`: case, accents and punctuation; the extension optional; never a part of a name) among the DIRECT contents of the folders the project lists under `accounts.google.folders` and `accounts.microsoft.folders`, through the providers of `integration:documents`. Subfolders are not walked (the providers list one folder, up to 200 items), and a folder cut at 200 or one that did not answer is said in the reply when nothing was found. One match is read and shown (plain text, 3,500 characters, then how many remain), several are listed (name, configured folder name, account) and none is read, none names the folders searched by their configured names. A text that looks like a credential (limit L14's scan) is withheld; a provider's failure is a fixed sentence.
- **A provider verb that did not exist.** The OneDrive provider already had `children --path`; it gains `--folder-id` (the folder's item id, the other form `accounts.microsoft.folders` takes). The Drive provider had no listing at all (it served the files a person picked): it gains `children --id <folder id>`, one GET of the files list, read only, same host, the account token's `drive` scope already covers it. Both are read verbs of the same class; no new host, no new scope. The tests' stand-ins grow the two routes.
- **The group.** `read-files` (operation `file-read`; plain, not kind-only, because it grants an operation of its own and adds a capability to none), in no default policy and never on the MCP mode (a model client must not be handed the person's files). A sender who lacks it gets the refusal sentence of the operation and no provider is called. `accept-config --widen` prints "shows the text of the files in your configured Drive and OneDrive folders in the chat, found by their names; a messaging app's servers see it as they see any message, and no model reads it".
- **What is kept.** The conversation stores a neutral word for the reply (`extras["stored"]`, the same word as a read that is not on the MCP mode) and the interpreter's own neutral word for the line; the bridge's ledger holds no text of a reply; nothing here logs a name, a folder or a text; the bytes live in the private staging folder of the sources for the length of the call and are removed; no source, version, catalog row or copy is made. The interpreter decides the operation and the name from the person's line and never sees a file.
- **A question about the file is not answered from it.** The brief asked for the quarantined answer reader (`ask`, limit L50) with the one file as its only text if that could be done without widening anything else. It cannot: `ask` reads registered sources (`task_copies`), selects them by code and runs the contained answer skill on their copies, so a file that is not a source would have to be registered or the selection widened. Neither belongs to this slice; the text is shown and the contract says so.
- **The catalog changed, so the interpreter's decision is renewed.** Adding the operation changes the served catalog (63 `ops`, hash `90c93343...`; the prompt, the protocol and the route are unchanged). `runtime/interpreter_enable.json` gets a new dated entry (basis `maintainer-decision`, the approval quoted) with every hash recomputed by `interpreter_enable.checkout_hashes`, so the interpreter stays on and an entry with a wrong catalog hash turns it off (`test_file_read.py`). `evals/interpreter/epochs.json` retires the epoch `a43735d2...` and serves `90c93343...` (the rule asks for the test split to have run on a new catalog first; there is none and the maintainer chose to go before the release, as for the first entry: stated here, not hidden).
- **The replay gate stays meaningful.** The recorded development answers were made with the catalog `a43735d2...`. A copy of that file is committed (`evals/interpreter/replay/catalogs/catalog-a43735d2.json`) and `test_interpreter_replay_gate.py` puts it in the place of the served catalog for each replay, checks that every in-use answers file names that hash in its header, that the served catalog now differs by exactly `file-read`, and that a planted change to the copy changes the counts. The gate is not weakened and no recorded file is touched; the dev corpus is not changed (adding an item would change the sample the replay draws).
- **Corpus coverage waits.** The completeness test (D10) asks 3 dev and 2 test items for every operation of the catalog. `file-read` has none: the dev items would change the replay's draw and the test split is held out and spent. It is listed in `PENDING_CORPUS` with its reason, exempt only while it has fewer items than the minimums (an exemption no longer needed fails), and gets its items at the next revision of the corpus, after the release. Until then the operation is unmeasured by the model-reading experiment; its dispatch is covered by `test_file_read.py` with a stand-in model.
- **Known limits (stated, not hidden).** Several files with the same name in two folders of one account cannot be told apart by the next line (no draft of a choice for this read yet: the reply asks for the full name with its ending and the service); a file in a subfolder is not found; every reply on a messaging app passes the bridge's rendering (`Bridge.deliver`: the dates are said in the project's language and this machine's home folder is removed), so an ISO date in a file's text may be shown written the project's way (a reply that is not a converted command's result is not cut by the net of L62); the 3,500-character cut is this slice's choice.

## 2026-10-21: an e-mail asked in free words gets a card with a text the AI wrote (S7, one extra model call, no lab run)

The maintainer's Telegram line "Envie um email para <address>. Quero falar de marcar uma reuniao para amanha as 16horas" answered that the request should be made in Portuguese or English (the fixed sentence `IX_WRITE_LANGUAGE`): the interpreter had decided `mail-send`, but `_carry_out` re-read the line with the closed pt-BR forms, which did not match. He had said on 2026-10-20 that for e-mails he would ask the AI to write a better-worded text from what he describes, and, after the guided dialogue worked, that the message would need to be worded by the AI.

- **The seed.** For `mail-send` and a new `mail-draft` (no message; a reply is untouched) `ops_interpret._carry_out` opens the family's card through a seed (`mail_compose.seed`, read by `ops_natural_writes._read` like event-create's seed), never through the closed forms, so the same path serves es, fr, de and it. The recipient is an address only when it is a whole address token of the sender's line (`natural_writes_mail.tokenize`, on top of S1's substring check); a word without `@` is a name the family asks the address of; any other `@` text is dropped and the family asks. No subject span means the model writes the subject too.
- **The text.** A second call on the SAME daily route, with its own fixed prompt (`runtime/mail_compose_prompt.txt`), counted in S1's rate, day and breaker limits. It sees five lines (date, chat language, typed recipient addresses, subject span, the person's own words), never third-party text. Code validates `{subject, body}` (two keys, plain text, no header line, HTML, fence, link, web address or address other than the typed ones, nothing the card refuses); on any failure the person's own words are the text (and the first sentence of them, cut at 60 characters, the subject when none was given): the card always opens. The card shows the From, To, Subject and the whole text and nothing is sent before the person approves its hash. This is the one exception to "no model-written text is ever shown to the person" of the plan, by the maintainer's decision.
- **The guided dialogue.** The answer to the family's body question (`ops_natural_writes._classify` marks it `_compose`) is worded the same way, the person's subject kept as typed; a body that starts with a closed marker ("exact text:", "texto exato:", and the same in es, fr, de, it) is sent as written with no call. The body question says so (`MC_ASK_BODY`) when composing is on for the sender. A closed form with its own body ("... assunto X dizendo Y") is the person's own text and is not worded.
- **The pin.** `interpreter_enable.json` gets a third dated entry (2026-10-21, the maintainer's words quoted, every current checkout hash of the catalog with `file-read` kept, so the interpreter stays on after S8) with `compose_prompt_sha256`; `interpreter_enable.compose_active` is `active` plus that pin, so a changed compose prompt turns composing off and leaves the interpreter on. The status script reads the same optional key.
- **Where code cannot enforce it (limit L63).** The person's words reach the model provider under the route's retention terms, as an interpreter line does; a composed text can still be wrong (a card the person reads before approving is the protection); a first-time recipient is not flagged, because the family keeps no record of correspondents (backlog EM6).
- **Not done / by recommendation, reversible.** Composing needs the group `interpret` (a sender without it gets the guided dialogue with the words as typed); a text over 1,000 characters is sent as written; the compose call is not retried.

## 2026-10-23: a calendar card on a phone is plain, and "Encontrei ... E este?" comes before it with the time the card shows (S16c, no model call, no lab run)

The maintainer's retest (Telegram, 09:52): "Remova o evento corte de cabelo da agenda" opened an Outlook removal card that the phone drew with the raw event id ("Evento: AQMkADAw..."), "Nomeado como agenda-outlook#1, do instantaneo lido em 2026-10-23T12:51:32Z.", "Baseado na versao 6ZUdoypAA0yc do evento." and "Removendo, titulo: corte de cabelo"; and a separate message "Encontrei: corte de cabelo, sab 24/10/2026 14:00-15:00. E este?" came AFTER the card with the snapshot's time (14:00) when the card's live time was 10:00-11:00.

- **What is hashed does not change.** The hash (`payload_sha256`) is the hash of the effect document, which holds the event id, the snapshot reference and the version as before; the card's lines are stored beside it (`payload["card"]`, with the English body) and a phone's text is a rendering of those stored lines. `messaging_cards.effect_card_text` already had a phone branch (the approve command and the hash line are left off); it now also leaves off, for a phone only: the event's id (`CARD_CAL_EVENT`), the snapshot reference and the time it was read (`CARD_CAL_SNAPSHOT`), the version of the event (`CARD_CAL_BASE`), and in the mail cards the message id of the message answered (`CARD_MAIL_IN_REPLY`) and the snapshot of a mailbox change (`CARD_BOX_SNAPSHOT`). A line that said a read time or a path inside a sentence has a phone sentence without it (`PHONE_CAL_CHANGE`, `PHONE_MAIL_REPLY_TO`, `PHONE_MAIL_ATTACHMENT`, `PHONE_BOX_READ_AT`, `PHONE_BOX_DRAFT_REPLY`, `PHONE_FILE_CODE_REPLACE`, `PHONE_FILE_CODE_TRASH`, `PHONE_FILE_PROJECT`, `PHONE_FILE_BASE`, `PHONE_CAL_DROP_WHERE`, `PHONE_CAL_DROP_NOTES`: appended, both columns). A removal that said the event twice (as it is now, and as what is removed) says it once. The account, the calendar, the title, the time now and the time after, the reminder and the line about where a removed event goes (Google's trash, Outlook's possible loss) stay. The page and the terminal show every line; the stored body, the English lines and the hash are the same as before (a test compares the phone's, the English and the stored hash).
- **"Encontrei ... E este?" (`event_lookup._found_fields`).** It is written from the stored card's own lines (`CARD_CAL_NOW_TITLE`, `CARD_CAL_NOW_WHEN`: the event as it was read live when the card was prepared), not from the snapshot or the ledger row; the row's values only when the card holds no such line. The operations layer returns it apart (`result["lead"]`, from `ops_say.say` and `ops_natural_writes.natural_write`) beside the reply that still holds it, and `Bridge.lead_first` sends it as its own message BEFORE the card, so the card is the last message (the word that follows decides it, limit L64); the reply's other line (that the card waits) is dropped after the card as before. It is shown only when the name was not exact, and exact now also skips the calendar nouns outside the title ("o evento corte de cabelo" has the title "corte de cabelo"): the live line had been shown "Encontrei" for an exact name because the noun "evento" stood before it.
- **Deviation from the brief, said:** the line comes in a message of its own before the card, not as the first line of the card's message, because the card is drawn from the stored decision (whose text the hash and the approval are bound to) and the line is about this one request.

- **Review round of #544: what names the target stays.** A phone's text leaves off the event's id and the version on purpose (the hash still covers them, and the terminal and the page show them); what it shows names the target unambiguously: for an event, the account, the calendar, the title and the time as read now; for a file to replace, the file, the **source** (the person's own label of the granted source, `CARD_FILE_SOURCE`, kept) and the version it replaces by its date (`PHONE_FILE_BASE`: "Substitui a versao de <modified>"; only the revision id is left off); for a reply, the message answered by its number in the person's list, its sender and its subject (`PHONE_MAIL_REPLY_TO` keeps `{ref}`; two replies from one sender with one subject read differently). **Open, said:** the received date and time of the message answered is not in the effect document of a reply (`send_document` `reply` has `ref`, `read_at`, `subject`, `address`, ids), so the phone cannot say it without adding it to the hashed document, which is not changed here; the number in the list is what names it. `test_phone_card_targets.py` builds, for each kind of card (calendar create, change and cancel; mail send, reply and draft; mailbox actions; file write, replace and trash), two cards that differ only in their target and asserts that their phone texts differ; it lists each dropped line with the reason it names no target, so a line added to `PHONE_DROPPED` fails until it has one.
- **Also in that round.** `_exact` no longer skips the nouns that are titles too (`TITLE_NOUNS`: reuniao, meeting, appointment, compromisso and their plurals); only the generic ones ("evento", "event") are skipped. `natural_write` returns `lead` to a messaging sender only (as `ops_say` does). When a line was sent before the card, the card is sent again whatever `RESEND_EVERY` says (`lead_first` returns whether it sent the line), so the card is still the last message.

## 2026-10-22: change or cancel an event by the closest name, never by its number (S16b, no model call, no lab run)

The maintainer's live test (17:02): right after the platform created "corte de cabelo" on Friday 23/10 at 14:00 on his Outlook calendar, "mude o corte de cabelo para as 10h" was answered with the hint of the typed numbered form ("Para mudar um evento, diga o número dele ..."). His rule: the person knows the name, not the number; the platform finds the event with the most similar name and asks whether it is the one.

- **The path that answered (reproduced with the stand-in model, 0 model calls).** The tier-1 grammar of the calendar family, which runs before the interpreter: `runtime/natural_writes_calendar.py` `_change` (the branch that returns `"say": "NW_CHANGE_BY_NUMBER"`): a change verb, no event noun and a time that NR-1 reads as a reminder; `ops_natural_writes.open_line` (`if reading.get("say")`) turned it into the sentence. The number hint now stays only when a calendar noun follows the verb ("remarca o evento 0 para as 10h": a typed number form whose number is not one); otherwise the reading is `by_name`, and `open_line` hands the line to `event_lookup.by_name`, which takes the operation from the line's leading verb, the time from the time engine when it reads one clear time with no doubt (`_code_time`), and finds the event as the interpreter's decision does.
- **When the interpreter's decision is invalid.** `ops_interpret.lenient` now falls back to the line itself for a refused decision about a line whose leading verb asks to change or cancel an event (`_by_verb`): the model's garbage, its other operation, or a question about another slot no longer ends in the ask-again sentence; the operation is the verb's and the event is found by the name. A model that named an operation of another kind (a request to cancel, say) and a line with no such verb ("Nao remova ...") keep the refusal. A negation is no verb.
- **The closest name (`event_lookup.ranked`).** Case and accents folded; the closed stop words left out (articles, polite and linking words, the verbs, the markers of a time, the closed words of LEADS: from the language tables; the calendar nouns and the names of accounts stay words, since a title may be "Reuniao" or "Reuniao do Google"); each title word is matched by a line word equal to it or within a small edit distance (none up to 3 letters, 1 up to 6, 2 above; a swap of two neighbours counts as one); a title is a candidate with at least half of its words matched and a share (2 x matched / (title words + line words)) of at least 0.5. The best is the one when it beats the next by 0.15; else the rows within 0.15 of the best, in the order of the calendar, are the several close ones. The window (today to 30 days, or the named day) and the account word work as before. A match is "exact" when every word of the title, the small ones too, is a word of the line as typed.
- **Replies.** One clear best: the change or cancel card opens, and when the name was not exact the reply starts with "Encontrei: <title>, <day and time>. É este?" (the card, which nothing executes before its approval, is the question; the line is in the reply, not in the card's stored body, so the hash of the card is unchanged). Several close: the numbered list, and "o 2" opens that entry. None: "no event with a similar name in the next 30 days, ask for your calendar" (no command), or, with no calendar source and no event created here, the S16 sentence. The time of a change that is a clock with no day ("para as 10h") is the clock of the found event's own day, keeping its length; with a list, the clock is kept as "@HH:MM" and takes the day of the entry picked.
- **Review round of #535 (three blockers, no card is approved or written by any of them, each opened a card nobody asked for).** (1) The verb fallback (`ops_interpret._by_verb`) applies only when all hold: the model's answer is garbage or a clarify of the slot `event`; the line names no other object (`event_lookup.names_other_object`: a closed list of mail, file, reminder, routine, task, folder and request nouns in the six languages, and any address), and is no question, no condition and has one verb that changes or cancels (`asks_again`); a verb leads. A clarify of another slot ("cancela a rotina do relatorio") and a line about an e-mail, a file, a reminder or a folder keep the refusal; the lookup itself (`open_event`) and the code reading (`by_name`) answer such a line with the plain "I did nothing" sentence. (2) A model event operation of the other kind than the line's verb is never swapped (S16's rule, restored); a line with two verbs that change or cancel, a trailing "?" or a condition after the verb ("se", "caso", "if", "unless", "si", "falls", "wenn", "qualora") asks again, whoever read it. (3) A clock moved onto the event's day is checked as any time is (`interpreter._instant_problem`, via `_past_problem`): past, too far, or an hour the clock skips or repeats is IX_ASK_PAST, IX_ASK_FAR or IX_ASK_TIME, on the code path, the model path and after a list; a time the check accepted is never replaced by an unchecked one.
- **Minors of the same round.** A clock change of an all-day event is refused in a plain sentence (EV_ALLDAY_CLOCK): no length is picked (a cancel is not refused). "Exact" now means the title's words, all of them and in order, are a run of the line's words and every other word of the line is a stop word or an account name (`_exact`): "cancela o almoco com a Ana" is not exact for "Ana", and "corte cabelo" is not for "corte de cabelo". Among close names, the exact ones win alone over the near ones.
- **Tests changed on purpose.** The S6 rule "a part of a title is not the title" and "a longer word is not the word" are the opposite of the rule above: `cancela o café` now finds "Café com Ana" (not exact), `cancela as consultas` finds "Consulta", and the not-found sentence of the lookup is the new one. Small words (up to 3 letters) are matched exactly, so "dia", "sim" and "ana" are not confused.
- **Not done.** No model call, no lab run. The line `mude o corte ...` is claimed by the tier-1 grammar only when it carries a time that NR-1 reads as a reminder; any other line reaches the lookup through the interpreter.

## 2026-10-22: cancel or change an event the platform created, with no calendar source, and a wrong event slot is no longer an ask-again (S16, no model call, no lab run)

The maintainer's project had Google and Microsoft accounts and no calendar source. After the platform created "corte de cabelo" through an approved card, "Remova o corte de cabelo de amanha da agenda" was answered with the ask-again sentence, and naming the Outlook agenda gave "Nenhuma agenda sua é fonte deste projeto". Later he added both calendar sources and the first sentence did not change, so the ask-again was never about the missing source.

- **The cause of the ask-again (verified with the stand-in model, not with the live one).** The check (`interpreter.check`) refuses a whole op decision for one slot it cannot believe. `event-cancel` has the slot `event` (a number written in the line); a model that fills it with the event's name or the day ("corte de cabelo", "amanha"), with a number the line does not hold, or with a slot the operation lacks (`title`, `time`) is refused as `E_INT` or `E_SLOT_UNKNOWN`, and a model that follows rule 3 of the prompt ("a needed slot cannot be read: clarify") answers `clarify` of the slot `event`; each of these ends in `IX_ASK_AGAIN` before `event_lookup` is reached. Which of them the live model wrote is not known (it is not logged); the test file lists eight plausible answers and all eight now reach the lookup.
- **The fix, after the check, not in it.** `ops_interpret.lenient` (called by `_ask_model` after `decide`, which is unchanged: the measured pipeline and its replay test are untouched) reads again a refused decision about `event-change` or `event-cancel`: the `event` slot that is not a number of the line is dropped (and, for a removal, any slot the operation lacks and its time), and the object is checked again; a `clarify` of the slot `event` becomes the operation the line's leading verb names (`event_lookup.verb_op`, the closed verbs of the language tables), checked against the sender's policy. A time or a title of a change that does not pass is still refused. The event itself is always found from the person's own words, by code, afterwards. Nothing the model wrote reaches a card.
- **One stderr line for each ask-again**, with a closed reason (`interpreter: ask-again (parse invalid)`, `(slot not a number written in the line)`, `(span not in the line)`, `(slot the op does not have)`, `(unknown op)`, `(model asked for a slot)`...), so the next live ask-again names its cause. It holds no line, span, value or model text (limit L63); a test checks that none of the words of the line or of the model's text appears.
- **Events the platform created.** `runtime/event_created.py` reads the store's approved `calendar-event` decisions (the result holds the provider's event id, the account, the calendar and its id) and their effect documents (the title and the times, which the decision's payload does not hold in words; read with the hash checked). A later approved change replaces the times, a later approved removal takes the event out. When no source holds the event (or there is none), `event_lookup.open_event` searches these rows by the same rules as the snapshots (the window or the named day, the title as whole words, the account word), reads each one that fits back from the provider (`CalendarReader.event_get`) and drops one that is gone or moved, and opens the existing card with the reference `created:<card>` (`ops_natural_calendar._reference`, slot `created`): the preparation of that card reads the event live and pins its version, as it does for the reference the calendar operations already accepted. Several: the numbered list, and "cancela o 2" after it opens that entry. None with no calendar source: "only events created here can be found until a calendar is added", with no command (L62). **Contracts:** limit L52 now says that reading back an event the platform wrote, by id, is allowed under the read right; the row is append-only.
- **Review round (the independent review of #530).** (1) `lenient` believes the model's operation only when the line's own leading verb names the same one (`event_lookup.verb_op`): "Nao remova o corte de cabelo" answered with a removal, and "Muda ... para as 18h" answered with a removal that dropped the time, stay refused. (2) The marker of a row of the ledger is `@created`, which no source id can be (`sources.SOURCE_ID`), so a calendar source called `created` is a source like any other. (3) A list of created events records the card number and a short hash of the title and the instants (no text); a number after it is asked again (EV_LIST_AGAIN) when the entry was moved or removed meanwhile. (4) A line reads at most `LIST_MAX` events from the provider, the rest are counted (a count makes the answer a list), and the ledger is read once for a line. (5) A read-back that fails is its own sentence (`EV_CHECK_FAILED`), never "no calendar added", and carries none of the provider's words. (6) Times are compared as absolute instants (`start_at`, `end_at`), and the day an event is searched on is computed from the instants in the machine's zone now, so a zone that changed since the approval moves neither the day nor the comparison.
- **Not done, by the brief.** No model call, no lab run; the catalog, the prompt, the enabling entry, the bridge, `mail_compose.py` and `ops_items.py` are untouched. An event the platform did not create is found only through a calendar source. A change of an event created elsewhere is not recorded in the ledger. A provider outage while reading an event back reads as "not found" (fail closed).

## 2026-10-22: a number after a list of events, the account a line names, and Google folders by path (S11, no model call, no lab run)

Three small things the maintainer met in real use before the release. **(1) "cancela o 2" after the list.** The S6 list ends "Diga, por exemplo: cancela o evento 2", and the answer "cancela o 2" (or "o 2", "2") went to the model and got nothing, because the numbered form needs the noun. **(2) "no outlook".** "Agende corte de cabelo amanha as 14h no outlook" with two calendar accounts was asked "Em qual conta?", because the interpreter's seed for a new event carried only the title and the time. **(3) A Google folder as a path.** `accounts.google.folders` refused a slash, so a folder could be named only by an id nobody remembers.

- **A number after a list.** `event_lookup.take`, called by `ops_natural.take` for a messaging sender (the place where the write forms, among them "cancela o evento 2", are already recognised; so it works with the interpreter off, and before any model call). The record is in memory, one for each (project, channel, sender), 10 minutes (`LIST_WINDOW_S`): the operation the list asked about, the new time a change was asked with (an ISO time, never a title), and (source, number, `read_at`) for each entry shown, by the S6 list and by the calendar's items list (`ops_natural._read` calls `remember_items`). It holds no line and no title. **The list is valid only right after it was said (review of #522):** `ops_say.say` counts the sender's lines (`event_lookup.begin_turn`), a line that does not take the list ends it, and so does a decision card made since the list was shown, so "o 2" after an unrelated reply, or a number meant for a list of cards, is not an event; only a question about the entry (`EV_LIST_WHAT`, `EV_CHANGE_WHAT`) keeps it. A plural article or "both" before the number ("cancela os 2") is a group, not the second, and is no form. An account word that stands in the title of an event that fits the line ("cancela a reuniao do google") names no account, and the model's own word counts only outside the title. `resolve-path` refuses a page that is not the whole list (`nextPageToken` or `incompleteSearch`). A form is a number alone ("2", "o 2", "#2", "the 2nd", with the closed articles, nouns and ordinals of the language tables), or a cancel or change verb and a number, rewritten as the numbered line ("muda evento 2 para as 15h") and read by the family itself (`ops_natural_writes._read`, a seed with `line` and `bind`: the source of the list), so the card, the questions and the rolled-time sentence are exactly those of the numbered form. A bare number follows the operation of the list; after the items list it asks what to do (`EV_LIST_WHAT`); after a change list asked by a rename it asks what to change (the title is not kept). **The number means the snapshot the list was made from:** before it is carried out the newest snapshot of that source is read again and its `read_at` must be the recorded one; a refreshed snapshot with other events, or a number two sources listed, is asked again (`EV_LIST_AGAIN`) and never guessed; a number the list did not show, another sender, a list older than the window and no list at all are today's behaviour. The answer to an open question of the family (a draft) is taken first, as before. *Known limit:* the messaging bridge answers a card word for the card list itself (S2) without calling the conversation, so only the rules above, not the bridge's last message, bound the list; while the sender has an open question of the planning agent, `ops_say` treats a line that code does not recognise as its answer, so a bare number then answers it; the list is not consulted.
- **The account word.** `event_lookup.account_named`: a closed list of brand words (google, gmail, gcal; outlook, microsoft, hotmail, office 365; language data, `requests.writes.calendar.brands_google` and `brands_microsoft` of the pt-BR and en tables, so no runtime module quotes a service name and the file and mail words of the same service, such as "drive", do not name a calendar) counts only beside an introducing word or a calendar word of pt, en, es, fr, de or it ("no", "na agenda do", "on my", "en", "sur", "im", "su", "calendar", "agenda", "kalender"...), and never inside the title the model read, so "lunch with Google" names none. The model's own `service` slot (the parameter `account`) counts only when it is one of those brands and stands in the line as a whole word; the check had already required it to be a piece of the line. Exactly one account is the account; both, or none, leave the question to the family, as today. The seed of `ops_interpret._carry_out` for a new event carries it, so the family skips the account question and the card shows the account. An account named but with no calendar configured is one sentence (`EV_ACCOUNT_NONE`), never a card for the other account. For an event to change or cancel the word narrows the search to that account's calendar sources (the same sentence when it has none).
- **A Google folder as a path.** The key accepts, in `accounts.google.folders`, a path in the form OneDrive's already took ("/Documentos", "/Clientes/2026"). **It is resolved when the person accepts the file**, not at first use: a write must never go to a folder the person has not seen as an id. `accept_config` calls `ops_google_folders.resolve` (the Drive provider's new read-only verb `resolve-path`: one GET of the files list for each name, folders only, the name escaped; no folder, or more than one, refuses the acceptance and names the path), builds the configuration with the ids in memory, makes the widening list from that (so a folder that now resolves to another id is a widening like any changed target and needs the hash), and only after every check passed writes `google-folders.json` in `data_dir` (mode 0600, replaced whole), which `project_config.load` reads to put the ids in place of the paths; `config` and the acceptance's answer show each path beside its id (`google_folders`). The first acceptance of a path asks for the hash twice, because the list `config` showed named the path and the list the acceptance makes names the id (the refusal prints the hash to type; nothing was kept by the refused try); the network is used once for each path in each try. A path that was never resolved is refused by `account_target` (the write kinds) and fails to list (the read of a file by its name). A folder renamed or moved later is not followed until the file is accepted again. The id form is unchanged and asks the provider nothing. *Known limit:* the file in `data_dir` is not covered by the file's hash; it sits with the store and the locks, and changing it changes where the platform writes without a new acceptance, which is the same trust the store has; a record that is not ours or whose id fails the pattern is ignored.
- **Not done, by the brief.** No model call and no lab run; the interpreter's catalog, prompt and enabling entry are untouched; the messaging bridge, the card words and the mail code are untouched. The comment at S6 ("cancela o 2 without the word evento is still not the numbered form") is answered only after a list.

## 2026-10-21: plain refusals on a phone, a card word from an e-mail reply's first line, and a typing indicator (S10, no model call, no lab run)

The maintainer's use showed a phone told `error: /file-read is not enabled for this channel; the project's configuration (channels.messaging) turns it on`, an e-mail reply "sim" under a phone's signature that decided nothing, and a Telegram chat that stayed silent while the planning agent worked.

- **One plain refusal.** `runtime/phone_refusal.py`: on the channel `messaging` a refusal of the policy is one sentence of the project's language (English: "You do not have permission for that here yet. It can be enabled on the platform's page."; pt-BR: `PHONE_NOT_ALLOWED`), with the page link when the project has one. `said(refused, channel, cfg)` is called at every phone path that words a refusal (`ops_interpret._refusal`, `ops_say`, `ops_natural`, `ops_natural_writes`); `plain(text, cfg)` is the net in `Bridge.reply` for the operations layer's own text (a `Refused` or an `OpsError` that reaches the chat), and turns "is done in the terminal or on the page" and "is not done from a messaging app" into the existing "that is not done from a message" sentence. The page, the terminal and the conversation keep the precise text. The action is not named in words: no phrase exists for each operation and the sentence is true without it (a later, smaller change if wanted).
- **The e-mail reply's first line.** `card_words.reply_line`: on a platform whose acts are replies the phrase is the first non-empty line, and everything after it must be a signature or a quoted history; a second line of words ("sim" and then "but change the time", on its own line) makes the text a request. This is stricter than reading the first line alone: the brief said the rest is ignored, and ignoring the second line would approve a card the person qualified, so the safer reading was chosen (principle 13). A reply that is "sim" and then a name signature with no delimiter ("Ana") is not a word, as before. The rest of L64 is unchanged.
- **The typing indicator.** The optional capability `typing`, the Telegram verb `typing --chat <id>` (`sendChatAction`, same host and token, no new secret), `Messenger.typing`, and `runtime/messaging_typing.py`. It starts after 0.5 s of a job, so that a reply code gives at once is not announced and not delayed, repeats every 4 s, stops before the reply is sent and after 60 s, is never shown for a line that starts with `/`, and a failure is ignored without a log.
- **Known limits.** The indicator is one subprocess call every 4 s on a polled provider (a listen-mode provider is not asked: its protocol has no such op); a plain line answered by code in more than 0.5 s shows the indicator for that time.

## 2026-10-22: the whole-suite run before the release, two failures of one cause (R1, no model call, no lab run)

The whole suite of the CI job `tests` (`WB_FULL_FUZZ=1`, 19,153 passed, 141 skipped, 2 failed, 13 minutes on 14 cores) and the `python39` job (11,707 passed, 8 skipped) were run on `feature/delegation-platform` at `5fa1d10`. Both failures are in `runtime/tests/test_interpreter_echo.py`, tests that are skipped without `WB_FULL_FUZZ=1`, and both come from TIME-GUARD (#505, 2026-10-21): the tree before #505 passes them. Neither is flaky (they fail alone, with the same ids).

- **`dev-es-00016`** (`el evento 9 se movió, ahora es el jueves a las 15:00`) is read as `time-ask`: the decision of #505 lists it among the three dev lines that change from `code` to `ask`. It is listed in `ALLOWED` with that reason.
- **Four lines of the relabelled file** (`dev-en-10259` "remind me at 12 to eat", `dev-pt-br-10207` "ao meio-dia e meia", `dev-pt-br-10209` "à meia-noite", `dev-pt-br-10223` "às 12") are answers to a reminder question with a bare whole hour and were asked back. This was a defect of #505, not an intended change: its rule keeps `at 3pm` and `às 15h` as code because a connector stands before the hour (`natural_time._extend` takes it into the phrase), but a bare hour carries its connector inside the phrase (`at 12`, `às 12`) and a noon or midnight phrase has it outside and unextended (`ao meio-dia`, `a meia-noite`), so `_hour_without_day_is_asked` saw no trigger word. #505 measured the 310 dev lines only and did not run the relabelled file, whose test is skipped by default. The supervisor's decision (the maintainer being away): the answer must keep working.
- **The fix** (`runtime/interpreter.py`, `_connector_beside_hour`, called beside `_hour_without_day_is_asked`, which is unchanged): the hour is not asked when the first word of its phrase is `at` or `as` (the connector the phrase holds), or when the word just before a noon or midnight phrase (`meio dia`, `meia noite`, `noon`, `midnight`) is `a` or `ao`. A connector before an hour written in digits (`a 15h`, `um 15 Uhr`, `alle 15`, `à 15h`) is not enough, and a day word beside the phrase is still asked by `_unread_day`: `test_interpreter_time_guard.py` and `test_natural_time.py` pass unchanged, and so does the replay gate. `remind Dana 15:00`, `remind Dana 3pm`, `remind Dana noon` (no connector) still ask. The echo test's `RELABELLED_ALLOWED` is now empty (the mechanism stays: the set is compared both ways) and a vacuity case shows that the four answers are correct and the same answer without a connector is off the mark. `dev-es-00016` stays in `ALLOWED`: an unread weekday word beside an hour is an intended #505 change.
- **Accepted, written down.** `remind Dana at 12 about the calendario` (an unknown word and a bare hour with `at`) is `code` on the clock's own day, as `remind Dana at 15:00` already was (the decision of #505, "names and other unknown words keep their behaviour").

## 2026-10-22: subject spelling and no dangling sign-off in AI-written e-mails, and a pending configuration that is not silent (S12, no model call, no lab run)

Four things the maintainer met in the live Telegram test. **(1) Spelling.** He typed the subject "Reuniao sobre o Openhora" and the card kept it; his decision: "a IA deve verificar a ortografia do assunto do email". **(2) A dangling sign-off.** A composed body ended with "Atenciosamente," and nothing after it, and a second card came out without accents in the body ("Ola", "voce", "reuniao", "amanha as 16hrs"). **(3) Silence.** After `runtime.json` changed and before `accept-config`, his "Ola" got no reply; only the service log said the configuration was not accepted. **(4) A cut log line.** That line ended "... Read the file; when it is what you want, ru".

- **The prompt.** `runtime/mail_compose_prompt.txt` asks for correct spelling, accents and punctuation in the subject and the body whatever the person typed; to copy a typed subject with only its spelling and accents corrected (same words, same order, nothing added, removed or replaced, no new number, capitals of a name kept); and to end with one courtesy sentence, never a bare closing formula or a placeholder name. A new dated entry of `interpreter_enable.json` (2026-10-22, basis `maintainer-decision`, the quoted words) pins its sha256; every earlier entry and every current hash stand, so the interpreter stays on and a changed prompt still turns only composing off.
- **Code does not trust the model's subject.** `mail_compose.spelled` takes the model's version of the typed subject only when ALL hold (an independent review found that an earlier, looser rule let the model change `-5` to `5`, `€` to `$`, `>` to `<`, `1.500` to `1,500`, `10%` to `10`, and use Cyrillic or full-width lookalikes): the text is NFKC and holds no format or control character; its letters are in the scripts the typed letters use; every non-letter character (digits, punctuation, symbols, spaces collapsed) is identical and in the same place, so only letters change; a word typed with a capital or a digit changes its accents only (the first letter of the first word may become a capital); a word typed in lower case with letters only may also have a small edit inside it (distance 0 up to 3 letters, 1 up to 6, 2 above; a letter added or removed at its start or end is refused, so "aprovado" never becomes "reprovado" nor "pago" "pagou"). Anything else keeps the typed subject, and the one-line, no-address, no-link checks of a model-written subject still apply. Accepted by design: a substitution or an inner edit in a lower-case word ("pagar" to "pegar"), which only the person who reads the card can catch. The body has no extra check beyond `validate`; `validate`'s signature is unchanged.
- **The closing.** `mail_compose.without_dangling_close` removes at most one closing formula that ends the body with nothing after it, with the bracketed placeholder name after it, never when the line before it ends with ":" (then it is the answer to that line: "Please reply with one word:\nThanks" stays) and never a list of bracketed items; the closed list holds the formulas of the six languages (folded for accents and case). A closing with a name after it, and a finished sentence ("Obrigado."), stay.
- **A pending configuration is told once.** `Bridge.config_pending` (called where `_handle` drops an update for want of a served project): a private line from a sender that the unaccepted file lists for the platform gets `CONFIG_PENDING` (pt-BR and en; other languages get English) once per chat and per hash of the file, with no hash, path or command (L62); the line is not acted on. **Decision to review:** whom to tell is read from the unaccepted file itself (nothing else says who the project's senders are); the sentence reveals nothing, and an unlisted sender or a group chat is told nothing.
- **The log line.** The cut was `service.dispatch_loop`'s `[:300]` (the refusal is about 520 characters: a path, two hashes and the command). The refusal is now a class of its own, `ops_core.NotAccepted` (an `OpsError`, code 3), and `service.fault_text` keeps that one whole up to 6,000 characters and every other error at 300. It carries `--widen <hash>` when the file widens what a channel or a sender may do (`ops_core._widening`, the hash `config` shows) and, in the same text and before the command, the widening lines the terminal prints (`policy_widening.lines`, with the retention of the daily route for `interpret`): the hash never travels without its list (S1).
- **Not done.** No lab run, no model call; the attached-card revision by words (S13) builds on `validate` and the enabling file after this merges.

## 2026-10-22: a waiting card is changed by words and replaced by a new card (S13, one extra model call for an e-mail, no lab run)

The maintainer's live case (Telegram, pt-BR): an AI-written send card ended with "Atenciosamente," and no name; the card itself says "reply with what to change, or reject". He wrote "Ajuste o corpo do email. Apos "Atenciosamente," coloque "Christopher Goncalves"" (accents dropped here) and the line went to the planning agent, which asked him to paste the e-mail. His words: "Caso eu queira ajustar alguma informacao antes de enviar, deve ser possível". The second live case: after the card of a new event he wrote `Mude o titulo do evento para "Evento Coders 26K"` (accent dropped here) and the lookup of an event of the calendar answered that no calendar is a source of the project; "Deveria sempre poder editar alguma informacao de algum card".

- **Which card.** A plain line that is not an approve or reject word (S2 goes first) is about the card that is the last message the bot sent the chat, or the card the line replies to, when it is an open, whole, unchanged decision of a write to the person's own accounts in a chat of its sender (`Bridge.revise_line`, after `word_line`, before every other branch; a platform whose acts are replies is not touched). The operations layer decides what the line is for that card (`ops.card_revise_check`): not about it (the line goes on as it always did), a new e-mail, an event, or a card that cannot be changed by words yet (one sentence: reject it and ask again). The change runs in a job and ends in the new card or one plain sentence.
- **An e-mail.** One call on the daily route with a fixed prompt (`runtime/mail_revise_prompt.txt`, pinned by `revise_prompt_sha256` in a new dated entry of `interpreter_enable.json` that keeps every current hash and the compose pin). The model is shown the language, the subject, the instruction and the body, never the recipients or the From. Code accepts the answer only whole: `mail_compose.validate` (strict JSON, no header, link or new address), a change from the card, every quoted text of the instruction in the new subject or body. The recipients and the account of the new card are copied from the old one and checked equal, so a revision can never change who receives an e-mail; an instruction that asks for it gets one sentence and no call.
- **An event.** No prompt: the interpreter reads the line as it reads any line (same entry, limits, checks of the spans against the line, TIME-GUARD and its questions), and only a decision `event-change` changes the card: the title span, the time on the card's day (or the day the span names) with the length kept, and the account word on a card that creates an event. The calendar is never searched and no event of it is touched. A decision that is anything else is kept in memory for 60 seconds so that the turn that follows does not read the line a second time.
- **The result.** The new card is opened by the operation of the typed command (`natural_write` with a seed for an e-mail, `event_create` or `event_change` for an event), checked equal to the old card in everything the line did not name, and then the old card is ended as rejected with a note that says it was replaced: its hash is never approvable again. Only the new card can be approved, by the same words and buttons. Nothing is sent or written by this path.
- **By recommendation, reversible.** A card of a cancel, a reply, a copy, a file, or a change whose event came from `created:` is "not revisable" and gets the sentence; the decision of a new event (`event-create`) right after an event card is read as a new request, not as a change (a created event names a title and a time, which is how a new request looks); a line only the interpreter reads ("lista os emails") typed right after an e-mail card costs one revise call, and when the model changes nothing and the line has no quoted text and no word that asks for a change it goes on to the conversation.
- **Review round (same day).** The model is called for an e-mail card only when the line holds a word that asks for a change, a quoted text or a word about the recipients (before, any unrecognised line was sent to it with the card's text, which swallowed requests and sent an "exact text:" body to the provider on a later line). A change of an event found in a snapshot is accepted only if the new card names the same event id on the same calendar, and keeps the parts the line did not name; a line that names another event than the card's ("muda o dentista para as 10h") leaves the card alone and goes on; the moment (the card's day and the new time) goes through the interpreter's own guard for a past or too distant time. Order: everything that can fail fails before the old card is touched (a sentence, and the old card is sent once more so a word decides it); then the old card is ended and the new one opened and checked, so the worst case is no open card, never two; a card decided meanwhile is told so; a reply to a replaced card is told it was replaced. The kept decision is keyed by project, channel and sender. Backlog: a diff of what changed on a revised card; the `pass` path runs inside a job labelled with a row that takes no model slot.
- **With S12.** `mail_compose.validate` now drops a closing formula that ends a body with nothing after it. A card whose own text ended with one (a text sent as written) keeps it in a revised card when the model kept it too (`mail_revise.accept` restores exactly the part the card ended with); a closing the model adds at the end is dropped as it is in a composed text, so an instruction to add a lone closing at the end is refused. The live case now needs a name after the closing, which is what the instruction asks for. The newest `interpreter_enable.json` entry carries S12's compose pin and the revise pin.
- **Where code cannot enforce it (limit L63).** The card's text and the instruction reach the model provider under the route's retention terms, as the interpreter's line does; a revision can still say something the person did not mean (the card the person reads before approving is the protection); a text the card already holds as a link cannot stay in a revised card.

## 2026-10-22: a phone in the project's language, and a question closed by rejecting it (S14, no model call, no lab run)

The maintainer's Portuguese Telegram showed the layer's English ("pending decision 14 is a question: reject takes a plan, an acceptance or an effect", "pending decision 17 is resolved, not open"), the router's own words in `/pending` (the route was not recognised, the router asks), a state digest glued above the planning agent's answer, titles stored in English in a status ("Mail: send from your account"), a delete-a-file line answered with a generic card sentence, and a `/help` padded into columns.

- **Rejecting a question closes it.** `ops.reject` on a pending decision of kind `question` calls `ops_question.dismiss`: the question is closed and its request cancelled by `ops_say._close_request` (the cancel `_close_by_code` uses, with the label of the person) when nothing else of the request is open; otherwise it is refused and nothing changes. The policy group is the same as for any reject (`reject-cancel`), so no sender gains a power. On a phone the question may be rejected without the card it was asked on (it has no hash and no effect); `/discard <n>` on a number that is no file does the same. The word of S2 is not extended: question cards are not decision cards (`NOT_DECISION`), and a "no" typed under a question may be its answer.
- **`/pending` and cards in plain words.** A question is `#<id> question from the assistant: <first line, cleaned as a phone shows it, cut at 80>`; a request the router could not read is `#<id> request not understood`; the list ends with how to dismiss. A project whose notices carry none of its text lists a question as before. The card of the router's question and of an unread request has a plain title and body. The stored titles and the card hash are unchanged.
- **The layer's errors in the language.** `phone_errors.said` under `Bridge.reply` and in `Bridge.oops`: a closed list of templates, then one plain sentence; a text already in the language is left alone; English keeps the precise text except for a decision already settled ("Request #17 was already resolved.").
- **Not reached:** `PQ_LABEL_ROUTE` (the label "Route") still labels a router reply that holds no question; the stuck-task notes of a status are the layer's English; a status of a project whose language has no table stays English; the page's own texts; and every `OpsError` the templates do not list is the generic sentence (the precise text stays on the page).

## 2026-10-22: fresh mail and calendar lists, and what to add when no source exists (S15, no model call, no lab run)

The live case (pt-BR, Telegram): "Liste o último email do meu Outlook" was answered "no numbered items yet: a mail or calendar source needs a snapshot first", which says nothing the person can do; the project had no mail source (sources are registered once, in the terminal or on the page). And a project that has a source lists a snapshot only as fresh as its last refresh. The calendar list on the phone also showed the source id, a UTC time and no day.

- **No source of the asked kind.** `ops_items.items` returns `missing` and `ops_natural._read` asks `ops_items.reply`. A phone gets one plain sentence (`NW_ITEMS_ADD_MAIL`, `_CAL`, and the `_ACCOUNT` forms that name Gmail or Outlook when the line named an account and only the other account has a source): the mailbox or calendar has not been added, and it is done once on the platform's page; never a command or a path (limit L62). The conversation and the terminal get the exact command (`operations.command_line`) with an example origin for each configured account (`accounts.google` gives `gmail:box=inbox;days=7;max=50` and `google:calendar=primary;past=0;ahead=14`; `accounts.microsoft` gives the `outlook:` forms); with no `accounts` key both are shown.
- **Fresh lists.** For a messaging line, after the read right is checked (`item_refs.may_read`, nothing new), each source of the asked kind (and account) whose newest snapshot is older than `FRESH_S` = 600 seconds, or has none, is read again once through `ops_sources.source_refresh`, the same call `event_lookup.refresh` makes for a calendar miss (S6); limit L52 is amended (append-only) to say that a line may do this. One read per source per line, one source after the other (they share the store). A failure is dropped (no provider text is kept or said) and the old snapshot is listed with one note, `(lista de HH:MM)`, in the machine's zone. The terminal and the page do not refresh: they have `source-refresh`. A listing unchanged since the last read makes no new version, so a list asked again after ten minutes reads the provider again; accepted, so that a list is never silently old.
- **The account in the line.** `ops_items._named_account` reads it with `event_lookup.account_named` (the closed brand words beside a lead word); `items` takes an `account` argument (not an argument of the typed command) and lists and refreshes only that account's sources.
- **A list for a person (phone only, the maintainer's addition).** Each source has a plain name ("Gmail inbox", "Google calendar", in Portuguese "Caixa do Gmail", "Agenda do Google"; two of one name are numbered) instead of its id; the time read is in the machine's zone ("lido às 10:31", with the day when it is not today); each event is written with its weekday and date ("qui 22/10 14:00–15:00 · corte de cabelo"); the organizer is left out when it is the person and said in words otherwise, and the snapshot's English words (all day, you: accepted, online meeting) are the project's language. The numbers are the snapshot's own, so the numbered forms and S11 are unchanged; the conversation and the terminal keep the source id and the line as the snapshot prints it (`items` rows gain `day`, entries gain `account`).
- **Deviations.** `event_lookup.refresh` is not imported: it drops every failure and reports none, and the list needs to know which source failed, so `ops_items._refresh_failed` makes the same call with the same rights. No file the brief put out of bounds was edited; `ops_natural.py` has two changed lines (the account in the args, and the reply for `items`), and two existing tests were updated for the new sentences (`test_ops_interpret_dispatch`, `test_natural_writes_items`).
- **Not done.** No model call and no lab run; the interpreter's catalog, prompt and enabling entry are untouched; the bridge, the card words and the mail compose code are untouched. Tests: `runtime/tests/test_items_fresh.py`.

## 2026-10-22: a file is found by part of its name anywhere under the granted folders, subfolders included (S17, no model call, no lab run)

The maintainer's first real use of `file-read` (S8) failed on a file in a subfolder of his granted Drive area: "bring me the content of the file <name> from my google drive" got the ask-again sentence, and the same line with "from the folder /<subfolder>" returned the text. His words, translated from Portuguese: "if the person already gave access to the folders, they may not remember the exact folder a file is in; that is why it is good that we try to search the files". S8 listed only the direct contents of each configured folder and matched the whole name.

- **The search.** `runtime/ops_file_read.py` walks the configured folders and their subfolders breadth first through the providers' existing `children` verbs (one call per folder, up to 200 items). Bounds in code: 5 levels below a configured folder, 2,000 items listed, 10 seconds in all; a search that was cut, or that could not list a folder, says so in the reply. A folder is listed only when it is a configured one or an item of kind folder found inside a folder that was listed, so a file outside the granted folders is never listed, named or read. **Not built, by choice:** the providers' own search (Drive `files.list` with `name contains`, Graph `search(q=...)`) plus an ancestry walk of every result; the walk reuses verbs that exist and are covered by the tests' stand-ins, no new provider verb, scope or secret was needed, and no real Drive or Graph call could be made to measure the search alternative. If a granted area is larger than 2,000 items the cut sentence says so and the search by the provider is the next step.
- **The match.** The name span is cut into folded words (`natural.normal`); a file fits when every word occurs in its name (a part of a word counts), the extension is optional and, when typed, must be the file's if the file has one. A file whose whole name is the span wins over files that only hold its words (S8's rule), so a name typed in full keeps reading one file.
- **The results.** One match: the text, with the folder path from the configured folder's name ("default › openhora"). Several: a numbered list with folder path and account, and a bare number on the sender's very next line ("o 2", "the 2nd", read by `event_lookup._number_form`, S11's closed forms) reads that file by code with no model call, the right to read files asked again; the list is kept in the process's memory for that one line (10 minutes at most, ended by any line, `begin_turn` in `ops_say.say`, or by a decision card made since), never written, logged or sent. None: one sentence naming the configured folders searched, and that their subfolders were included.
- **Narrowing.** An account word of the line ("do google drive", "no onedrive", "do drive") narrows the search; it is read from the line by code too (the language tables' `services`, with the introducing words S11 uses), so a model that wrote no `service` word still narrows, and a model word that contradicts the line asks again. A folder named in the line ("da pasta /openhora", "na pasta Igreja", a path with slashes must resolve as a whole) narrows the search to that folder and what is under it, decided against the folders the walk found; a folder that is not under a granted one is refused in one sentence naming the configured folders and nothing is read. The typed operation (page, terminal) searches subfolders too and names no folder (its row has no such argument; a new argument would edit `operations.py`).
- **The cause of the ask-again (verified, not guessed).** `natural.check_operation` reads a `word` slot as ONE whole word of the line (`WORD_RE` is `[^\s]{1,200}` and the value must be one of the line's whitespace tokens). The model wrote the `service` slot as "google drive"; the check refused it (`the argument 'service' is not a piece of the line`), `interpreter._check_slots` turned that into `E_SPAN`, the decision was `invalid`, and the reply was the ask-again sentence. "drive", "google" and "onedrive" passed, which is why the second live line worked. `natural.check_operation` is pinned by a hash (`test_natural_check_operation_is_untouched`), so it is unchanged; `interpreter._check_slots` now checks an `account` slot of two or three words in a row by the same rule for each word (`ACCOUNT_WORDS_MAX`), which also lets `event-create`'s `service` be "google calendar". The catalog, its hash and the interpreter's enabling entry are untouched.
- **What is kept.** As S8: the conversation stores a neutral word for the reply and the interpreter its own for the line; nothing logs a name, a folder or a text; the staging folder is removed. The line that picks ("o 2") is stored as typed, as any bare number after a list is.
- **Files outside the brief's list, and why.** `runtime/ops_natural.py` (3 lines: `take` calls `ops_file_read.take` after the events' `take`), `runtime/ops_say.py` (1 line: `begin_turn`, beside the events' one), `runtime/interpreter.py` (`_account_run`, `_check_slots`), `runtime/tests/test_operations_table.py` (three names in the set of public non-operations). Without the first two a number could not pick; the third is the verified cause.
- **Review round (independent review of #527).** (1) The pick re-checks the grant: each held item carries the configured folder it was found under, and "o 2" reads it only if that folder is still one of the configuration in force; else the plain no-folder sentence and nothing is read. (2) A found file, a list or an unreadable file is followed by a note ("I did not look through everything ...") when the walk was cut, a listing was in part or one failed, and so is the file a pick reads from such a list. (3) The items cap is counted item by item and the walk stops at the item that reaches it, inside a listing too. (4) Each listing is asked with a timeout of at most the time left, a listing that is not back when the time is spent is given up, and the walk returns without waiting for it. (5) The bare word "drive" names no service ("do meu drive" is searched in both accounts); a line that names both accounts searches both, and a model word with it asks again. (6) Outside a messaging app a list asks for the full name instead of a number.
- **Known limits.** A folder named with several words and no slash is matched by prefix against the walked folders (`da pasta default › Igreja` and `da pasta default` both read as the folder `default` unless the longer tail exists); the walk costs one provider call per folder, so a granted area of many small folders is cut at 2,000 items; the typed operation cannot narrow by folder; a Google Drive shortcut is not followed.

## 2026-10-22: a short acknowledgement on messaging when a reply takes time (S19, no model call, no lab run)

The maintainer's use showed that requests that take a few seconds (a read of the mail, a search of a folder, the planning agent) look like a chat that is stuck: the maintainer said that slow actions still send no automatic message, after asking earlier for a short confirmation, such as "let me see" or "searching", that fits the request.

- **When.** A plain line that is still unanswered after `ACK_DELAY` = 2 s gets ONE short text in the chat's language, then the real reply. A line answered sooner, a line that starts with `/` and an approve or reject word (`card_words.card_word`) get none; one handle sends at most once, and a write line that turns out to be a plain line is not acknowledged twice (`Bridge.ack_begin`).
- **What.** Chosen by code from a stage the job publishes (`runtime/line_stage.py`, a closed list of eight, set from the thread that runs the line at `ops_interpret._ask_model`, `ops_natural._read` (items), `ops_ask._ask`, `ops_file_read.reply`, `event_lookup.refresh`, `ops_natural_writes.open_line`, `mail_compose.write` and `ops_say._say_route`); the stage current at 2 s decides, none gives the generic sentence. The nine sentences are new phrase keys `ACK_*` in the English and pt-BR columns (let me see; let me check your e-mails; let me check your calendar; looking for the file; writing the e-mail; preparing the card; searching; I will work on that; and one moment, I am looking into it, for none); no model call and none of the person's words. A stage is published after every refusal of its operation, so that no sentence promises what is then refused.
- **Never in the way of the reply.** The send is made on the timer's own thread through `Bridge.deliver`; `stop` is called before anything of the reply is sent, sets a flag and does not wait for a send in flight, and every failure is ignored with no log. It runs for the lines of `Bridge.line` and of `Bridge.write_now` (the card of a write may wait for the model that words an e-mail).
- **Known limits.** A send that began just before the reply may reach the platform just after it (the reply never waits for a courtesy). The acknowledgement becomes the last message of the chat, so a card word typed meanwhile decides nothing (the safe side of rule 60). There is no setting to turn it off yet (`messaging.ack` would touch the configuration schema and its tests); the platform's `typing` indicator (S10) is independent of it. A language with no table gets the English sentences, as every phrase does.

## 2026-10-22: the found file itself on a messaging app, behind the group `send-files` (S18, no model call, no lab run)

The maintainer's words in chat, translated from Portuguese: "he should also bring me the source file". The supervisor's decision of the same day: S18 sends the found file as an attachment, behind a NEW group `send-files` that is off by default and accepted once with `accept-config --widen`, and the text of every readable file goes through the credential scan (L14) first. Round 1 is the Telegram provider.

- **The group.** `send-files` (`runtime/operations.py`): in no default policy, valid on a messaging app only, a capability that adds to `file-read` and never grants it (`KIND_ONLY`, as `interpret` is), absent from the MCP mode. `project_config._check_assigned` refuses a channel entry or a profile that lists it without `read-files` ("'send-files' needs 'read-files' beside it"), so the refusal is made when the file is accepted. Its widening line, printed by `config` and by the refusal of `accept-config` with the hash, is in `GROUP_TEXT`: it says the whole file leaves the drive for the messaging app's servers.
- **The path.** `file-read` finds the file as before. When the call names the app's limits (`can_send`, which only the bridge passes and only a messaging call keeps) and the sender's profile holds both groups, the reply carries `extras["attachment"]` = {name, kind `photo` or `document`, bytes}, returned by `say` as `result["attachment"]` and nowhere else. A photo is a png or jpeg image under the photo limit; every other file is a document; a Google Doc exported as Markdown gets the ending `.md`. A text file keeps today's preview as the message text; an image, or a document the converter gives no text for, is announced by one sentence. Without the group, or with a provider that cannot carry a file, the reply is today's text byte for byte (a test compares the whole result of `say` with and without the new argument).
- **Before a byte is sent, in code.** (1) The right and the grant are asked again at the send: the pick ("o 2") reads the configuration in force, so a profile that lost `send-files` or `read-files`, or a folder that was removed, sends nothing; and the bridge asks the policy once more when it hands the file to the provider (`Bridge.file_to_send`, `messaging_file.allowed`). (2) The size is checked against the app's limit and the type, read from the bytes, against the app's list; a file over the limit gets one sentence, plus the preview when it has text. (3) The text of every file the converter turns into text, a PDF or an Office file included, passes the same credential scan as a source's file (L14): a hit withholds the file and the preview with one plain sentence; a converter that fails withholds the file too (nothing unscanned is sent that could have been scanned). (4) Nothing is stored: the bytes are in memory for the call, then in one temporary file of a new 0700 folder for the length of the send, removed in a `finally` (`runtime/messaging_file.py`); the conversation keeps the neutral word of S8; no log names a file or a folder. Tests check it with caplog, capsys, the store, a scan of the data folder and the temporary folder itself.
- **A file whose text cannot be extracted is sent as it is** (an image; a PDF or an Office file the converter returns no text for), as the brief says. **Not as it is, on purpose:** a file whose type is not on the list (an executable, an archive, a video, an unidentified binary) is not sent, with one sentence. The brief says an image or a binary is sent as it is; a binary the code cannot even identify can be neither scanned nor told apart from a program, and what it saves (sending an archive) is less than the safety it gives up (principle 13). The list is the types the intake already identifies (`pdf`, `docx`, `xlsx`, `pptx`, `ooxml`, `html`, `csv`, `text`, `png`, `jpeg`, `webp`, `gif`) and is declared by the provider (`send_types`), so a platform may narrow it. **The gaps, stated (corrected after the independent review of #531):** a credential drawn inside a picture, one in a picture's metadata (a PNG text chunk), the images of an Office package and the image streams of a PDF are not seen, and a PDF stream compressed with an image codec is not read; a credential a PDF writer splits across text operators is seen only when the converter's text joins it. The first version of this slice scanned only the converter's text and called the picture "the one gap"; the review showed that text is not what is sent (HTML scripts, document properties, a Markdown data link, a PDF's /Title, hidden sheets and slides, embeddings were all sent unscanned).
- **What is sent is what is scanned (review fix).** `runtime/send_scan.py` (standard library only) scans the bytes that go: text, csv, html and Markdown raw; every XML or text part of an Office package (hidden sheets and slides, properties and comments included) and the zip comment; a PDF's raw bytes with its hex, octal and UTF-16 strings read and every FlateDecode stream inflated. Bounds: 32 MiB per part or stream, 128 MiB in all, 5,000 parts, 20,000 streams, read in overlapping windows so that the scan's own 5 MiB cap never lets a large file through unseen. The file is refused, with the same sentence as a hit, when it cannot be read to the end: an embedding, a macro project, an encrypted part, a name that leaves the package, a part that is neither text nor a picture (so a Word file with an embedded font or an Excel file with printer settings is not sent either, on purpose), an encrypted PDF, a Flate stream that does not inflate, a stream with a filter (ASCII85, LZW, run length, a chain) or a predictor it does not read, a cap, any error. **Not built:** ASCII85 / LZW / predictor decoding, text extraction from PDF content operators, and any reader of an image; a format that needs them is refused, not read.
- **Minors of the same review.** A policy that changed between the search and the send says that the right changed (`FS_RIGHT_CHANGED`), not that the file did not arrive; if the right to read files is gone the text found is withheld too. A send with a file waits 180 s in the bridge against the 30 s of each of Telegram's calls (it makes up to four), and a timeout after the text may have gone says only that the file did not arrive and never sends the text again. The Telegram file name keeps Unicode letters, digits and spaces ("Relatório do coral.pdf") and loses only path separators, quotes, control and format characters and leading dots. The bridge sweeps `messenger-file-*` folders older than an hour at its start (that exact prefix, a real folder, this user's, mode 0700).
- **Facts that did not hold.** (1) The brief says the bridge's message already has a `document` key to send; it is `{name, text}` only, a text turned into a `.txt` by the provider, with no path for bytes and no photo at all. S18 adds a `file` entry ({name, kind, path}) to the neutral message, because a listen-mode provider's line holds 1 MiB and a file must not travel as JSON; the bytes are in a temporary file the provider reads. (2) The platform's "type list" did not exist in the capabilities; the optional keys `send_files`, `max_send_bytes`, `max_photo_bytes`, `photo_types` and `send_types` are new (`messaging_provider.OPTIONAL_KEYS`, `providers/messenger/README.md`); the generated capability table shows `send_files` and `max_send_bytes`. (3) `say` took no limits; it takes `can_send` (not a row argument of the table of operations, so the service, the page and the terminal cannot pass it). (4) Only Telegram declares `send_files`; Discord, WhatsApp and e-mail carry no file out yet, so a sender of those apps with the group gets today's text (a follow-up per provider).
- **The record of the accepted file is nearly full (said here so that it is decided, not found).** Each place's groups are a hexadecimal mask in a cursor of 4,096 bytes; in the worst case the tests build (100 senders, every group) 45 groups were 3,684 bytes and the 46th makes 3,800, because the mask of every sender gains a hex digit at each fourth group.
