The test that kept "room for about 15 more groups" (3,700) could not hold with any new group, so S18 moved its margin to 3,900 (room for about 7 more) and says so in the test. The hard bound, 4,096, is unchanged and still met. **The next package that adds a group must first move the record to a denser mask** (base 64 gives a third less on the masks), a change of the cursor's format that is its own slice; it is not done here because it touches what every acceptance compares.
- **Telegram.** `telegram.py` reads the file (no link followed, a regular file, at most 20 MiB, or 10 MiB for a photo, a png or jpeg by its first bytes, the type on its list), refuses what does not fit before any call, sends the message and then `sendPhoto` or `sendDocument` as a reply to it, and reports a file the service refused as `document_failed` (the bridge then says the file did not arrive). **Unverified:** the Bot API limits (50 MB for a document, 10 MB for a photo) and the formats `sendPhoto` takes were written from the documented behaviour; this slice had no network, so the maintainer's live test confirms them. 20 MiB is below the documented document limit on purpose.
- **What the maintainer runs.** Nothing changes in his `runtime.json` by itself. To turn it on for one sender he adds `send-files` beside `read-files` to that sender's profile and runs `accept-config` (it stops, prints the list with the new line and the hash, and he repeats it with `--widen <hash>`).
- **Not built, by choice.** A sent-file log, a limit on how many files a day, sending to a chat other than the one that asked, a preview of the file before it goes (the right to read already is the person's), and the file for the page or the terminal (the page opens a link, the terminal reads the drive).

## 2026-10-22: removing an Outlook event is plain, with no live check first (S21, no model call, no lab run)

The maintainer's words in chat, answering how to turn on the removal of Outlook events: "Apenas apague" ("Just delete"). That reverses **Q12** of SPEC-SECRETARY-WRITES, recorded in the W1 entry above: "removing an Outlook event is off (exit 2 of the writer, refused at the start, in the document and in `preflight`) until a live check shows a removed test event in Deleted Items". The maintainer chose the plain removal: no local copy of the event, and no live check first. Q12 is reversed by the maintainer, not by evidence; nothing was run against a real Microsoft calendar.

- **What was built.** `providers/writer/outlook-calendar.py` has the verb `event-delete` (`--calendar`, `--event`, `--base-etag`, `--idempotency-key`, `--dry-run` or `--confirmed`), the same shape as Google's: the writer reads the event right before, compares its changeKey with the version in the approved document (`changed:`, so `stale`, otherwise), and makes one `DELETE /me/calendar/events/{event}` or `/me/calendars/{calendar}/events/{event}`. `--dry-run` makes no connection and reads no credential. The ledger and the key work as for a change: a key whose answer was lost is looked up first, and an event that is gone then counts as removed and is recorded done (`replayed`), as for Google. `ALLOWED` of the writer gained those two pairs, and the L57 allow-list of `runtime/tests/test_limits_account_writes.py` gained them for that file only.
- **Where the off switch lived, and is gone.** (1) The writer's module text and its `argparse` refusal ("removing an event from an Outlook calendar is not available yet"), exit 2. (2) `runtime/step_calendar_event.py`, the refusal at the start of `event-cancel` (`not_available`: "Microsoft does not say where a removed event goes"). (3) `runtime/calendar_document.py` `check`, the document refusal. (4) `runtime/effect_calendar.py` `preflight`. (5) The text of `runtime/ops_calendar.py`, `runtime/operations.py` (the help of `event_cancel`) and `runtime/cli.py` that said it is not available. (6) The tests that asserted it, in `runtime/tests/test_calendar_write.py` and `providers/writer/tests/test_writer_outlook_calendar.py`. (7) The documents: `providers/writer/README.md`, `providers/CONTRACT.md`, `contracts/runtime.md` (the W1 paragraph and L57), and the copies in `docs/architecture/platform/`.
- **The rules that stay (Q3, the same as Google).** A meeting with other attendees, a series (a master, an occurrence, an exception, or an event with a series master) and an event the person does not organize are refused: at the start by the runtime, and again in the writer on the event it reads right before the DELETE (exit 1, `not eligible:`), because the DELETE cannot be undone and a guest may have joined after the card. Nobody is told; no attendee is ever sent.
- **The card says the truth (principle 10).** The removal card of an Outlook event has its own line, in the project's language: "O Outlook pode não guardar uma cópia do evento apagado; depois de aprovado, pode não ser possível recuperá-lo." (English: "Outlook may not keep a copy of a removed event; once approved, it may not be possible to recover it."), after the line "O código remove este evento depois da sua aprovação." (phrase `CARD_CAL_CANCEL_OUTLOOK`). The Google card keeps its line about the trash. The card is approved by its hash as before: nothing is removed without the approval of its exact content, and a moved changeKey is `stale` with no DELETE. validate: allow english-only -- the card line and the live line are quoted in Portuguese, the language of the project
- **L57 is no longer "nothing is deleted permanently" without an exception.** The row now says that this removal is the one case where the destination is not a place the person can restore from by our knowledge: Microsoft's page for the call does not say where the event goes, and nobody has checked. **Recoverability is unverified.** It is listed as open under "To verify live" in `providers/writer/README.md`: delete a test event by hand through the writer and look in Deleted Items.
- **What is still unverified.** Whether a removed Outlook event lands in Deleted Items or is gone; that Graph answers 204 to the DELETE and 404 to the next read of the event, as the stand-in does; that `isOrganizer`, `attendees`, `type` and `seriesMasterId` read as the writer expects on a real calendar; and everything the W1 entry lists.
- **Cost of the safety.** The writer's eligibility check adds no request (it uses the read it makes anyway). The cost is that a meeting, a series and someone else's event cannot be removed here, as on Google, and that a removal the person approves may not be undone.
- **After the independent review (no blockers; minors folded in).** (1) The guard of the removal is tighter: every id segment of the writer's `ALLOWED` is the percent-encoded Graph id character set the builders enforce (letters, digits, `_`, `-`, `%3D`, and `%2B` for an event), so a dot segment (`..`), an encoded slash, a calendar group and any other tree are refused by `request()` itself; the L57 allow-list names the new regexes. (2) The pre-read of a removal and of a change goes through the route of the calendar the card names, and an event not found there is `changed:`; the stand-in now keeps each event in its own calendar and answers 404 through another's path (before, it served any calendar's path, which hid the check); that real Graph does the same is a new line of "To verify live". (3) L56 says the Outlook re-read is before the PATCH and before the DELETE and names the gap that remains: Graph has no `If-Match` on a DELETE. (4) Dead code of S14b removed: the refusal matching in `messaging_failure` and `phone_errors` and the phrase `FAIL_OUTLOOK_REMOVE`, since nothing raises that refusal any more. A lost answer followed by the event disappearing for another reason is recorded as done, by design; it is a line of "To verify live".
- **Tests.** Offline, on loopback stand-ins: the writer (`providers/writer/tests/test_writer_outlook_calendar.py`), the runtime path with the real cards (`runtime/tests/test_calendar_write.py`), the live line "Remova o corte de cabelo de amanhã da agenda" on an Outlook event (`runtime/tests/test_event_lookup_own_events.py`) and the allow-list scan (`runtime/tests/test_limits_account_writes.py`). validate: allow english-only -- the card line and the live line are quoted in Portuguese, the language of the project

## 2026-10-22: a question of general information answered by a web search through the research skill (R2-F1, no model call, no lab run, no skill change)

The maintainer's words in chat, translated from Portuguese: "As for R2-F, wouldn't it just be pointing to a web research skill?" and then "Yes: start R2-F1 (web research through core-research) now, in parallel." The live failures: "Como vai estar o tempo em SP capital amanha?" got "I did not understand the request and started nothing", and "Pesquise a previsao de tempo para Sao Paulo amanha" got a router question about a skill that was not installed (the planning agent's pack holds only the router and the brief skill).

- **What.** A new read `research` (class R, slot `question`, a span of the line) in the interpreter's catalog, picked for a question of general information that no file or source of the person's own answers. Code, not the router, runs ONE task of `core-research` (the name is a constant of `runtime/ops_research.py`), so no question is put to the person first and no plan is made. The skill is outside every area agent's pack and needs no runtime manifest (nothing it writes comes back); the code checks that it declares `search:web` and no side effect, else one sentence. **No skill file changed**: the skill's own contract (`requires: [search:web]`, `side_effects: []`) is what the code relies on, and a test reads the real file.
- **How the run gets the web, and why no adapter changes.** The lab facade already takes `web` (`lab.run_skill(web=True)` is the adapter's existing `--allow-web`, used for every task whose skill requires the web); `ops_contained.execute` hard-coded `web=False` and gains one keyword, `open_web`. A run with it is on the open network and has NO file of the project in its copy (not the state file, not an earlier brief), stricter than a web task, which gets the artifacts its skill declares. No fingerprinted file and no adapter was touched.
- **Proof decides the web.** The web is used only when the routed pair was measured with it (the proof's `web`, `web_measured` of the pair); a pair that was not runs nothing and the reply says there is no web. The model is the usual rule for a conversation run: the project's `default_model`, else `auto`, which is the cheapest pair on which the skill is `reliable`, else the reference model. Today `core-research` is `needs a test` on both models (its guard assertions have no run in the current set), so `auto` falls back to the reference model, `web_measured` is true for it, and the run is not proven: the reply says so in one sentence (`RS_UNPROVEN`) until a full test passes.
- **The group and its widening.** `research` in `operations.GROUPS`: in no default policy, valid on a messaging app and in the terminal (never narrowed), no row for the page or the MCP mode. Its widening line, printed by `config` and by the refusal of `accept-config` with the hash: "answers general questions (weather, prices, news, facts) from a web search: the question is sent to a web search service and to the model that runs the research, and the answer comes back to this chat". A sender without it gets the plain refusal of S10 and nothing runs.
- **Bounds.** The new key `research` of `runtime.json` (all optional): `max_cost_usd` 0.25 (the adapter's `--max-cost-usd`, always given), `timeout_seconds` 300, `max_per_day` 10. The daily count is read from the store (a research run is a row of `task_runs`, as an answer run is; a failed run counts; the terminal's and the phone's together) under the claim lock, with the route lock held for the run (one conversation model call per project). It is on top of the existing limits (the interpreter's per-sender and per-day limits, the sender limits of the bridge) and replaces none; the skill belongs to no agent, so no agent's daily cap counts it. **Not built, by choice:** a per-sender count, and a cost cap that stops the run in code (the adapter enforces `--max-cost-usd`, or says it cannot; a run whose reported cost reached the cap is named in the reply).
- **The reply.** Written by code from the brief in the run's copy (`runtime/research_reply.py`), not the skill's form: at most six claims in plain words, the sources they cite as short links (at most five; a fragment and tracking parameters dropped; only http or https, a host with a dot that is no address, local name or user name), cut to 1,800 characters on a phone with the sources never cut. No skill, flow or file name, no code fence, no link from a claim; the skill's section "Instructions found in external content" is never read. A failure is one plain sentence each: no web, timeout, cost cap, daily limit, nothing established, say the question again, any other. The stage `research` is published for the acknowledgement of S19 ("Pesquisando...").
- **What is kept (L63).** The question is the person's line: the interpreter stores it as a neutral word. **The answer is stored as a neutral word too** ("(answered by a web search)"), not as an agent's reply is stored: a question about health, money or a person is answered with the web, and the conversation is read by the MCP mode; the answer reaches the chat that asked and nothing else. The run is a row of the store (a request called "Question <n>" with the text "web research", and its run: skill, model, tokens, cost), so the daily count and `costs` see it; the store holds neither the question nor the answer. The run folder holds the prompt and the brief for `run_retention_days`, like every contained run, outside git. Nothing is logged or printed; an error prints the name of its type only.
- **The catalog changed.** The served catalog has 64 operations; its sha256 is `fd05b3d27bf994935dae860053652fdeeae10a90e23bb1bc75aa8c29081eedc7`. A new dated entry of `runtime/interpreter_enable.json` (basis maintainer-decision, quoting the two lines above) keeps every other pin (route, prompt, protocol, languages, the compose and revise prompts) and names the new catalog; a new epoch of `evals/interpreter/epochs.json` retires the one before. The replay gate keeps replaying the recorded answers against the committed copy of the catalog they were recorded with (`catalog-a43735d2.json`); its test now reads that the served catalog adds `file-read` and `research`. **Two more slices change the catalog (S20 and R2-A); whichever merges later carries the earlier entries and its own catalog hash.**
- **Not verified.** Whether the real interpreter picks `research` for a general question and for no line about the person's own data: the corpus has no item of the operation (`PENDING_CORPUS` in `test_interpreter_coverage.py` lists it beside `file-read`), so the choice is not measured; the group being off by default is the protection. No real web search, adapter or model ran: the run is a stand-in of `lab.run_skill`, and the brief the skill really writes (its reply and its `docs/workbench/research/*.md`) is read from the skill's template, not from a live run. How the adapter ends a run at its cost cap (the reply names the cap only when the reported cost reached it). The 4,096-byte record of the accepted file now has room for about 6 more groups (S18 said about 7).
- **What the maintainer runs.** To turn it on for one sender he adds `research` to that sender's profile and runs `accept-config` (it stops, prints the list with the new line and the hash, and he repeats it with `--widen <hash>`); the `research` key is optional.
- **Fix round after the independent review (one blocker, seven minors).** (1) **Privacy blocker:** the first version wrapped the prompt of the web run in the person's standing instructions (`ops_instructions.contained_prompt`, as every model run does), so a planted "My name is Ana Example; my bank is Banco Exemplo." was verbatim in the prompt of `lab.run_skill(web=True)`, and a page could have asked the run to send it out, while the widening line says only the question leaves. Now the prompt of an `open_web` run is the question and the fixed task text, nothing of the project; the old test that asserted the instructions reach the run is a refusal oracle with a vacuity case. (2) The help texts of the operation and of the terminal now say the truth: the conversation holds a neutral word, and the run folder keeps the prompt (the question) and the brief for `run_retention_days`, outside git; the widening line is unchanged. (3) A claim that is a slash line, a whole card word of `runtime/card_words.py`, or card-like (`#12`, a decision command anywhere, a 64-hex hash) is dropped from the reply. (4) A source link with a character outside printable ASCII (a bidi override, any Unicode format character, a no-break space) is refused. (5) The daily count counts only runs on a REQUEST of the skill (`task_id == request_id`), so a task of a flow or an agent that runs `core-research` is not counted. (6) A research line that meets a run in progress gets its own sentence ("Já estou pesquisando outra pergunta; tente de novo em instantes.", `RS_BUSY`). (7) The pt-BR plural: "Já fiz 1 pesquisa hoje" / "Já fiz N pesquisas hoje" (and the English one). (8) **A code guard against personal-data questions** (`runtime/research_guard.py`): a question or its line that holds a possessive form of a source of the project ("minha agenda", "meus e-mails", "my calendar", "mon dossier", "mein Kalender", "la mia cartella"...) does not run; the reply is the ask-again sentence and nothing is stored. The bare noun stays allowed ("agenda cultural", "pasta de dente"). It is a check of closed forms, not a reading of meaning: the Spanish and Italian "mi" counts only next to the noun or one word before it, the French "ma" and the Italian "ma" (but) are the same word, so an Italian "ma il file..." is refused and asked again; a question about the person's data worded without a possessive still depends on the interpreter's choice (still unmeasured). **Left as is, by the reviewer's word:** the route lock held across an account-limit pause (same as `ask`). The run row's `web` column is now truthful (`answer_run_start(web=True)`), but the count does not read it: a run on a request is exact without a new column in `runs_since`.

## 2026-10-22: a request of its own is served and the router's open question stays open (S4b, no model call, no lab run)

The maintainer's Telegram test of round 1: a question of the planning agent (#36) was open; five minutes later "mostra minha agenda" was answered with the sentence "I took that as the answer to question #36" (in Portuguese) and the question again, and the calendar was never listed.

- **The verified cause.** `ops_natural.recognises` (the list of lines that are a NEW request before an open question, LIVE-2 and S4) returned False for every tier-1 form of class R, on the thought that a read ("progress") may be the one-word answer. So a tier-1 list or read ("liste meus emails", "mostre meus eventos", "mostre os agentes", "what files do you have?") with a question bound to the sender reached `_say_route` as `answer_to` and was told with `ANSWER_TAKEN`. Reproduced with the real operations layer and the bridge on the chat tree before the change; the typed commands, the reminders and the card words were already read before the question and are tested here as they stay.
- **The rule.** `recognises` is `peek(...) or natural.candidate(...)`: any tier-1 form, a read included, a reminder, a write to the person's accounts and the answer to the sender's own draft are requests of their own. A line is the answer only when it is none of those (a typed command is read before any question), or when it replies (the reply gesture) to the question's own message, which the bridge answered first and still does. While such a request is served the question stays open, bound, and its request `requested`. For a sender the interpreter reads, a tier-1 form is claimed by code before the frame, so it costs no model call; a plain answer is decided by the one call of the frame and costs no second one.
- **The state questions are kept as answers.** "progress", "status" and "what is running" are not tier-1 forms (they are `ops_say.state_question`) and stay the one-word answer of an open question, as `asks_state` and the contract already said. Not in the list of the brief; reversible by one line if the maintainer wants them served too.
- **No note about the open question (decided).** A sentence such as "question #36 is still open" is not added, not even once per question. Reasons: it needs a mark per question to say it once, in memory that a restart loses; every reply path (a list, a file, a card, a refusal, a typing indicator's acknowledgement) would carry a second subject; and the question is still the person's to answer by a plain line, by a reply to it, or in the notice that the platform resends. Reversible: one phrase key and one mark in `pending_items`.
- **The calendar read in words is a tier-1 form (the coordinator's decision, same PR).** The live line, "mostra minha agenda", must not depend on the model. The `items` forms of the two shipped language tables gain the calendar nouns with their usual verbs (pt-BR: agenda, calendario, compromissos, with "minha agenda", "mostra/liste/veja ...", "o que tenho na agenda", "quais compromissos eu tenho", and an optional day word; en: calendar, agenda, schedule, with "my calendar", "show/list ...", "what is on my calendar", "what do I have on my calendar"). `writes.calendar.nouns_plural` of `en` gains agenda and schedule (the pt-BR list already had agenda, calendario and compromissos), so `natural_writes.items_kind` says `calendar`; that list is read by `items_kind` alone. The forms are whole lines, so "Agende ...", "remova ... da agenda", "muda ... na agenda" and "cancela ... da agenda" (and their English) are no tier-1 form and reach the paths they reached (tested, literal, with a vacuity case that plants a form too wide). A day word is accepted and read by nobody: the list is the snapshot's, as it is for "meus eventos". The tables for es, fr, de and it that the brief names do not exist in this repository (`natural_languages.json` lists en and pt-BR only): a line in those languages is still read by the interpreter, with the framed call; adding a table is a slice of its own.
- **Where code cannot enforce it.** A calendar read in a language with no table (es, fr, de, it) or in words no form carries is told from the answer by the interpreter's one call alone; a model that says `answer`, or a call that failed, takes it as the answer, and a sender the interpreter does not read has it taken as the answer.
- **Tests.** `runtime/tests/test_request_not_answer.py` (literal oracles, each with a vacuity case that plants the old rule): the reads, the typed commands, a reminder in words and the list of reminders, an approve word for the card that is the last message; a plain answer; a reply to the question's message; another sender; and the interpreter's rig (no call for a tier-1 form, one call for a line it maps to an operation or for a plain answer). `test_router_question_stale.py` was updated where it pinned the old rule.

## 2026-10-22: a mail list a person can use, and a freshness that tells the truth (S15b, no model call, no lab run)

The maintainer's live test of round 1 ("Liste o último email do meu Outlook" at 16:59): the reply said "lido às 10:31" and showed `no-reply-<random>@mail.<vendor>.com · <subject> | <date>`; the mailbox listing "brings me no useful information" (his words, translated). The store showed one version imported at 10:31 and nothing newer.

- **The cause of "10:31", reproduced with the stand-ins.** The refresh at 16:59 was made (one provider read) and found the listing unchanged (the same ids and change keys on the same local date), which by design makes no new version (`sources_mail.snapshot_refresh`), so the snapshot's `read_at` stayed 10:31 and neither the heading nor a note showed that it had been checked. It was not skipped and did not fail (a failure shows the note). Fix: the check is remembered apart (`ops_items._CHECKED`: a UTC time for each (project, source), no content, in memory; a restart loses it and the next list asks the provider once more), the heading says "verificado às 16:59" when the check is later than the reading, the freshness test reads the later of the two (so a list asked three minutes after a check reads nothing), and a failure writes one stderr line, `items: refresh of <source> failed (<word>)`, the word from the closed list of `sources.SourcesError` codes or `error`, never the provider's text.
- **One line for a person (phone only).** The snapshot's text, read after its hash was checked, gives for each message the `From:` line, the time (`## n. YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM`, the machine's zone) and the body. The line is the display name (else the address with the random tokens of its local part taken out, plus the domain: `no-reply-3f9a8c21@mail.vendor.example` is `no-reply@mail.vendor.example`), the subject as the e-mail has it, and "hoje 14:02" / "ontem 09:39" / "seg 20/10 18:10". The terminal, the page and the conversation keep `address · subject`.
- **The last e-mail.** `items_mail.count_of` reads the count from the line by code: "o último email" and "the last email" are 1, "os 3 últimos" / "the last three" are 3; digits and the numbers one to ten in pt, en, es, fr, de and it are a closed list in code; "os últimos emails" with no number is the plain list. With two mailboxes the newest by the printed time wins; each message keeps its own source and number, so the numbered forms still point at it. **The snapshot holds the body** (cleaned, links replaced, up to `BODY_CHARS`), so one message asked for also shows "Início do texto:" with about 300 characters of it, markup and link marks removed, cut on a word; no provider read was added. A count never exceeds 20.
- **Nothing of a mail reaches a log.** The only new write outside the reply is the stderr line above; a test plants a marker word in the sender, subject, body and the provider's failure and finds it only in the reply.
- **Not done.** No model call, no lab run; the interpreter, the bridge and the card code are untouched. The count applies to mail only.

## 2026-10-22: the English refusal that reached a Portuguese phone, and the file lines the delete check missed (S14b, no model call, no lab run)

Two live cases of the maintainer's Telegram (17:03 and 17:07). A haircut event removal asked in Portuguese ("remove the haircut of tomorrow from the calendar"), about an event on the Microsoft account, was answered with the writer's English sentence ("removing an event from an Outlook calendar is not available yet: Microsoft does not say where a removed event goes..."). a request to remove a named png file from the drive got the interpreter's generic "could not build the card" sentence, and the same line with "em /openhora" got the plan-stage acknowledgement and then "I did not understand".

- **The refusal itself is unchanged** (Q12 of SPEC-SECRETARY-WRITES: removing an event from Outlook stays off until a live check shows where a removed event goes). Only its words on a phone changed: `FAIL_OUTLOOK_REMOVE` (one plain sentence: it cannot delete events from an Outlook calendar from here yet, and the person can delete it in Outlook itself), used on the preparation path (`messaging_failure.from_step`), for the writer's exit reasons (`from_error`) and by the text net (`phone_errors.SEARCHED`). The page and the terminal keep the precise text.
- **The cause.** `step_calendar_event._prepare` raises `StepFailure("not_available", ..., next=<English>)`; `account_start.start` puts `messaging_failure.from_step(..., said=next)` on the `OpsError` as `.phone`; `from_step` had no sentence for that code and returned `phrases.localize(said)`, which knows only the families of reminders and files and returns the English unchanged; `ops_natural._error` sent `.phone` as a plain reply with no `error: ` mark, so the net of S14 (`phone_errors.said`, which looks at an `error: ` text or an `OpsError` the bridge reports) never saw it.
- **The net now covers the shapes.** `phone_errors.safe` (a sentence some code wrote for a phone: unchanged when it is in the language, a known refusal as its sentence, any other English as `PHONE_FAILED`) is applied to the `.phone` of an error at `ops_natural._error`, `Bridge.failed` and `messaging_attach.phone`, to the step's own sentence in `from_step`, and to the result fields `error`, `refused`, `reason` and `why` that `ops_natural._render` shows a phone. An English project keeps the precise text (only the Outlook refusal and an already settled decision are told plainly).
- **The sweep** (`test_phone_plain_words.py`): every message of an `OpsError`, `_usage` or `EffectError`, every `next` of a `StepFailure` and every text of an effect's `return <code>, "..."` in the calendar, mail, files and sources modules is run through the real net as a Portuguese phone gets it, and must come out as a sentence of the phrase table.
- **The delete check** (`phone_delete.asks`) ran only after the open question of the conversation was looked at and after the busy/queue check of `ops.say`, and the bridge queued the line before `ops.say` saw it. It now runs first: in `Bridge.say` (before the queue), in `ops_say.say` (it waits for no run) and in `_turn` before the open question, the lists of code and the interpreter. It reads a verb of deleting or throwing away in six languages (a verb of moving only with a word of the trash), then the first thing named within four words: a file decides yes, an event, a reminder or a message decides no; a name that ends as a file ("logo.png") counts without the word "file".

## 2026-10-22: the folder is the one the person sees, and every reply names folders by their real names (S17b, no model call, no lab run)

The maintainer's live Telegram test: "Me traga o arquivo Copy of the-first-sin-activities da pasta /openhora" and "Me traga o arquivo openhora-mark da pasta /openhora" both got the refusal sentence ("I did not find that folder inside the folders configured for your accounts, and I do not look outside them: default (Google Drive), default (OneDrive)"), where before S17 the same request found and read the file. His words, translated from Portuguese: "file search does not work; before, it at least brought the file in writing when it was text".

- **The cause (verified).** S17 matched a folder named in the line only as the tail of a path that began at the configuration's alias (`_named_dirs` in `runtime/ops_file_read.py`: the first name of every path was the alias, "default"), and a folder it did not find was a refusal (`no_folder`, `_find_and_read`). The person never sees "default": it is a name in `runtime.json`. A configured Google folder that is itself the Drive folder called openhora (the live case: `accounts.google.folders.default`) was matched by no word the person could type, and a subfolder was matched only when the path was written from the alias. The refusal also came before the "could not list" sentence, so a walk whose listings all failed read as "that folder is not here".
- **The rule.** A folder named with or without slashes is matched from any folder of the walk and never through the alias: "/openhora" and "openhora" mean a folder openhora at any depth, "/openhora/x" the chain openhora then x at any depth, case and accents folded; a configured folder is also meant by its real name and still by its alias. Only folders the walk reached are looked at, so the grant boundary is the one of S17.
- **A folder that is not there.** The search goes on in all the listed folders and the reply adds one note (`FR_FOLDER_MISSING`: "I did not find the folder x; I looked in all your folders"); with nothing found, the none-found sentence (the real folder names). The refusal sentence (it says nothing outside the listed folders is searched) is kept for a path that clearly points outside: a `..` or `.` step, the root of a drive (`/`, `~`) or an item id (a long run of letters and digits); it lists nothing and reads nothing. **Decided by recommendation:** an absolute path of several names that is not found (`/a/b`) is a name that is not there, not a refusal, because on a Drive it cannot be told from a typo; the probes of the S17 review (`..`, ids, roots) stay refused.
- **Real names in every reply.** The head of a found file, the list rows, the none-found sentence and the refusal name a folder by its real name: a Google folder's through one `stat` of its id per search (the existing provider verb, same host and scope, bounded to a quarter of the search time), a OneDrive path's from its last name, a Drive root as "Meu Drive"; a name that could not be read is the service's own name. The alias appears in no reply unless the person typed it. After a pick the refusal for a removed folder names the accounts only (the names are not read again).
- **The file itself.** The text of a plain text file comes as it did (the preview), and with `send-files` it also comes as a file (S18); both are tested for a file in a subfolder named as the person sees it.
- **Known limits.** The sort of a list is by the folder names shown, so the order of a list can change when a folder is renamed; a folder of several words typed without slashes is matched by prefix; the reply repeats the folder as the person typed it in the note.


## 2026-10-22: move a file to the trash by its name, on a messaging app (R2-A, no model call, no lab run)

The maintainer's live Telegram test (translated from Portuguese): "Removing files does not work either", then "Yes: start R2-A (move a file to the trash) as soon as S17b is in." S14 had made the platform answer a line that asks to delete a file with one honest sentence; this slice replaces that answer with the real path for a sender who may do it, and keeps the sentence for one who may not.

- **A trash, never a delete.** The one removal is a move to the service's trash, where the person restores the file: Drive `PATCH /drive/v3/files/{id}` with `{"trashed": true}` (a module constant), OneDrive `DELETE /drives/{drive}/items/{item}` with `If-Match` (Graph documents it as a move to the recycle bin). The calls that delete for good (Drive's `files.delete`, Graph's `permanentDelete`) and any call on the trash are in neither writer's allow-list; the L57 scan (`test_limits_account_writes.py`) gained the two exact exceptions (the constant of the Drive file, the pair of the OneDrive file) and still finds every other way. What the documents support, and what is not verified live, is in `providers/writer/README.md` ("The trash"): the card says "recoverable for about 30 days" for Google Drive (Drive's help page; the API pages do not say it) and only "recoverable while it is in the trash" for OneDrive.
- **The same effect kind, a fourth operation.** `trash` is an operation of the effect `files` (document `content` null; base = version/eTag, cTag, folder, granted folder, folder path by real names, size, exactness of the name), so the approval, the hash, the dry run, the ledger key and the stale rule are those of every file write. The file is found by the search of `file-read` (the same walk, ranking, account and folder words, list and "o 2"); its id and the granted folder come from the search, never from the person's words or the model's text.
- **The grant is asked three times:** when the card is prepared (`FileGrant.locate`: a walk from the granted folder must reach the file's id), when it is approved (`effect_files.preflight`: the folder listed under its name is still the one the file was found under, and the walk still reaches the file) and by the writer (the file's folder is the card's). A walk that was cut proves nothing and refuses.
- **Rights (corrected after the independent review of #541): a group of its own, `files-trash`.** The first version put `file-trash` in `files-write`. The review found two blockers. (1) *Privacy:* a sender with `files-write` and no `read-files` could list file names: the "several" list and the card lines are not `CARD_PRIVATE` (a sender without `ask-mail` has to be able to approve a card that names the file) and `_fits` matches any part of a word, so "apague o arquivo e" listed every file with an "e". (2) *Acceptance:* the accept-config record keeps group names as masks, so a project that had accepted `files-write` gained the trash with no new acceptance. The fix: `files-trash` is a group of its own (operation `file-trash` only), in no default policy, valid where the row is listed (messaging, page, terminal), with its own widening line ("moves a file you name to the trash of your Google Drive or OneDrive, after you approve its card; recoverable while it is in the trash (adds to read-files)"); `files-write` is as it was. Adding it to a project that accepted `files-write` is a widening, shown by `config` with a hash that `accept-config --widen` takes. `project_config` refuses a policy that lists `files-trash` without `read-files` beside it (as S18 does for `send-files`), and `ops_file_trash` asks `read-files` again at every use (the typed operation, the interpreter's reply, a pick, and `permitted` for the line of code). A sender without both gets S14b's plain sentence (`PHONE_NO_FILE_DELETE`) and no provider is called. *The approval stays `approve-effect-files`.* `account_start.GROUP_OF_WORD` maps one effect word to one group (`files` to `files-write`), and a test pins it; rather than change it, `account_start.OPERATION_GROUP = {"file-trash": "files-trash"}` names the group that gates the operation, separately from the approval grant (the grant of the effect word is unchanged). The card lines still name the file to the sender who asked: that sender now necessarily holds `read-files`, so they see no name a read would not show.
- **The line.** `phone_delete.name` reads the file's name from a clear line with no model; a sender who holds `files-trash` and `read-files` gets the card (or a list, or one sentence), a sender without it gets `PHONE_NO_FILE_DELETE` as before, and a line whose name code cannot read goes on to the interpreter, which gets `file-trash` (catalog 65 operations with `research`: the new epoch, a new dated entry of `interpreter_enable.json` that carries every other pin, the replay gate unchanged against the recorded catalog).
- **Not done.** No model call, no lab run, no real provider call; the live behaviour of the trash on both services is unverified (the list is in the writers' README). Answering a question about a file stays R2-A2.
- **Minors of the review.** (1) The DELETE pair of the OneDrive writer now has id segments that refuse `..`, `%2E%2E`, `root:` and a query string at the guard (`(?!(?:\.|%2[eE])+(?:/|$))[A-Za-z0-9_%.~=-]{1,600}` for each id; the scan's allow-list names that exact regex). (2) The Drive PATCH pair is bound at `send()` (`guard_trash`): the one PATCH of a file carries exactly `TRASH_BODY` and the fields-only query, so it cannot rename, add or remove a parent, set a flag or reach `/files/trash`. (3) Said in the README of the writers: the Drive trash PATCH, like `update`, has no precondition (Drive v3 has no etag on `files.update`), so a few hundred milliseconds pass between the read and the write; the OneDrive delete has `If-Match`.
## 2026-10-22: every command can be asked for in words (S20, no model call, no lab run)

The live test of round 1: the maintainer sent "rejeite a pergunta 25" and was told "A decision is made on its card: use the buttons of the card." (translated from Portuguese), while `/reject 25` worked. His words: "Devemos ser capaz de acionar QUALQUER comando utilizando linguagem natural". The line was not in the closed lists (the noun "pergunta" was missing), so the interpreter named `reject`, and the interpreter answered every decision, a rejection included, with that sentence.

- **The rule.** On a messaging app the words of a command are the typed command: the same policy check, the same refusals, the same effect. The operations layer reads the line (a closed form of the tier-1 tables, or the interpreter's decision) and runs the typed line (`ops_natural.hand_over`): `/cancel`, `/stop-task`, `/retry`, `/attachments` and `/progress` are called as `ops_say._say_command` calls them; `/pending`, `/reject`, `/release`, `/add` and `/discard` are the bridge's own, so the layer hands it the typed line (`result["typed"]`) and `Bridge.line` handles it as it handles the typed one, so the card the sender was sent, its hash and the grant are the typed command's. A routine's pause, for which a phone has no typed command, is called after the same `operations.refusal` (review round: `routine-cancel` is final and is not done from words). `/project` has no operation: its words are `runtime/command_words.py` (six languages; the project is named explicitly, and a lead that may begin another request counts only for a name that is a project of the sender).
- **Approve is the exception, on the side of the effect.** "aprova o 31" sends card #31 again and approves nothing; the sentence says the card is approved with its button or with the word that follows, and the card is left the last message (`keep_cards`), so the word that follows (S2) does decide it. Before this change the card was sent and then a sentence, which made the sentence the last message and "the word right after" untrue. `answer` and `go-ahead` keep their words (a plain line answers the open question; S4). The sentence "A decision is made on its card: use the buttons of the card." (`IX_USE_CARD`, in both phrase columns) is removed: an approval now says the card was sent again (`NAT_DECISION_CARD`), a line with no number asks which decision (`NAT_WHICH_DECISION`), and an answer named by the model says a question is answered by replying to it (`IX_ANSWER_ON_QUESTION`).
- **The number is read by code.** The model names `reject`, `release` or `approve` and never gives the number (those operations are in the catalog's `refused` list, without slots); code takes the one whole number written in the line (`natural.decision_numbers`: "25", "#25"; none in a line that says a date or a time, none glued to a letter or a mark: "25h", "12:30"). None or two asks "Which decision? Say its number in the same line." A number that is not an open decision gets the one sentence of the closed lists, "There is no open decision #N."; a decision with no card in the chat is the typed command's "Act on the card sent to you".
- **The catalog did not change.** No operation was added, so S20 changes neither the catalog nor its hash (before the merge of R2-F1 it was `90c93343...`; now it is R2-F1's `fd05b3d2...` and its enabling entry stands), the prompt, the protocol, the route and the enabling entries are as they were (`runtime/interpreter_enable.json` has no new entry, and the replay gate has nothing to explain). The brief allowed a new entry if the catalog changed; it did not need one, because a refused operation already carries the model's choice and the number is code's.
- **Closed lists are in the languages that have tables.** The tier-1 lists are language data of the pt-BR and en tables (the only two the runtime has), and `command_words.py` carries `/project` in six languages. The other four languages (es, fr, de, it) reach every command through the interpreter, as they reach every other command; a test runs a line in each.
- **Not in the brief, decided here.** (1) `retry`, `add`, `attachments` and `progress` were not in the brief's table; their typed lines are as safe as the table's (a retry makes a task ready, `/add` is the button of a file's card the sender already holds, the other two are reads), so they run by their typed lines as well. `add` is the one with a doubt: it makes a file a source, but only through the card bound in the ledger to the sender and its hash, and the "adiciona ao projeto" line already did it for the latest file (ATT-3). (2) A closed form of a decision or an act (`reject`, `release`, `cancel`, `stop-task`, `retry`, `add`, `discard`) takes no model slot, so a brake said in words is not kept behind the planning agent: `Bridge.say` starts it as a job of its own operation. (3) A rejection by words carries no note (`/reject 12 note` has one): the words of a note are an open tail that no closed list may have. (4) `routine-pause` runs by number without a typed command to be equal to, because the brief lists it; the policy group is `routine-brakes` (see the review round for the actor and for the cancel).
- **Limits amended.** L54 (a write and a decision are run from a line on a messaging app only as this entry says; an approval, an effect, a gated act and the creation or change of a routine never) and L64 (the card stays the last message after the sentence about it). `test_limit_54_...` keeps the walk of the table and now lists, by its own literal, the four acts that call their operation, and asserts that no approval and no answer is ever called.
- **Not verified.** The words were run against a stand-in model and a stand-in messenger; no live model, Telegram or WhatsApp call was made. The real model's choice of `reject` for a line such as "rejeite a pergunta 25" rests on the prompt's rule 7 (name a refused operation when the line asks for one) and on the measured behaviour of S1; a model that names another operation gets that operation's reply.
- **Review round (the independent review of #538: two blockers and the minors).** The review found that a decision or an act the interpreter or the router chose ran on the model's reading of the verb and the number, and that the line was never asked: "no need to cancel request 1", "should I cancel request 2?", "if it fails, stop task 3", "don't stop task 3" (each in Portuguese too) and "cancel 2" with no list acted, and "stop task 1 of 2" would stop task 1. The tier-1 forms were safe by their shape (a closed phrase, the whole line); the model's readings were not. (1) **A reading by a model is believed only when code finds the same in the line** (`command_words.vet`, called by `ops_natural.run` for every `routed` call of `approve`, `reject`, `release`, `cancel`, `stop-task`, `retry`, `attachment-add`, `attachment-discard` and `routine-pause`): the line holds exactly one number (`natural.decision_numbers`) and it is the model's; its leading verb, after the polite words, is one of the closed verbs of that operation in the six languages; the object it names is the operation's own (a request, a task, a file, a routine; a decision needs only its number, and a line that names a task, an event, a file, a routine, a reminder or a mail for a decision is refused); and there is no negation, no condition or time word (se, caso, if, unless, wenn, si, quando, when, depois, after...), no "?" and no more than 14 words. Anything else is "I was not sure what you meant, so I did nothing" (or "which number" when the number is the problem). The lists are this module's own and closed; S16/S16b's helpers read events through the calendar tables and name a different set of verbs, so only their CONDITIONAL words were kept in mind, not imported. A line the person wrote in a looser way ("I want to cancel request 3") is asked again: it costs a second line, and an act that cannot be undone never rests on a guess. (2) **Relative times and quantities are no decision number**: "3 days ago", "2 hours ago", "in 2 weeks" (and their Portuguese forms), "25 euros", "25%", "$25" (`natural.MEASURES`, `natural.plain_number`; the tier-1 forms no longer read "reject 25%" as #25). (3) **`routine-cancel` is not done from words** (it is final): the reply is one sentence that offers the pause and names the page; `routine-pause` calls `ops_actor.guard`, takes `channel` and `sender` (`channel_arg`) and records `pause:<actor label>` beside the routine in the cursor `routine-moved-by:<id>` (no column holds it; migrating the store for it was not worth a change of schema in a fix round). (4) **The typed line is dispatched, not read again** (`Bridge.typed_line`): for the line's own project and chat, with no second `project_of`, no reminder question and no `/project` that moves the act; a platform whose acts are replies gets "reply to the card". (5) **A sentence that says the card was sent again is said only when it was**: `resend_forced`, as S13 does; the flood rule stays for every other flow. (6) After a numbered list, "approve 2" still chooses position 2 (S2/S11) and the S20 sentence is not said (a test keeps it). **Not fixed on purpose:** "no" is a negation even when it abbreviates "number" ("task no 3") - such a line is asked again; the verb list has one verb form per person that the six languages commonly use, not every conjugation.


## 2026-10-22: the gate of the change of a card by words reads the verbs that write, a text block after a colon and the card's own object; and an event card is read as the card it is (S13b, no model call in the tests, no lab run)

The maintainer's live Telegram test after round 1. **Case 1.** An open e-mail card already ended with a closing formula and a name after three changes had been asked and done. He wrote "Escreva ao final do email:" and, after a blank line, the closing and the name again; the bot said it was working on it and the planning agent asked him to paste the e-mail. **Verified cause:** "escreva" was no word of `mail_revise.CHANGE_WORDS`, the text had no quotation marks (`literals` found nothing), and no word named a recipient, so the gate of `ops_card_revise._taken` was closed, the bridge's `revise_line` took no job and the line went to the conversation; reproduced on the real operations layer with the stand-in model (`check` returned None). Nothing about "three changes" matters: the cache holds only the interpreter's decision of an event line, there is no count limit, and after each change or sentence the card is sent again and is the last message (tests after three earlier changes). **Case 2.** An open event-create card (Microsoft, "corte de cabelo", Fri 23/10 14:00-15:00) and "muda para as 10h": IX_ASK_AGAIN, no revision.

- **Closed list.** `CHANGE_WORDS` gains the verbs that write, end, sign or put a text in the six languages (escreva, ponha, deixe, termine, finalize, encerre, assine, reescreva, acrescentar, retire...; write, sign, end, finish, append, rewrite; escribe, pon, firma; écris, mets, signe; schreibe, setze, beende, unterschreibe; scrivi, metti). "pôr" is left out on purpose: folded it is the preposition "por", which would open the gate for "obrigado por tudo".
- **A text block.** A text that follows a line ending with a colon ("...:\n\n<text>") is a text the instruction asks for, as a quoted text is (`mail_revise.literals`, read from the instruction with its line breaks, `mail_revise.instruction`): the gate opens, the model is shown the line with its line breaks, and the answer must hold the block (white space compared as one space). The revise prompt says so and says to return the e-mail as it is when it already says what is asked: a new dated entry of `interpreter_enable.json` re-pins it (the interpreter stays on).
- **Already so.** When the model changes nothing and the card already holds every text the line asks for (or the model writes again, at the end, the very text the card ends with), the sentence is RV_ALREADY ("the e-mail already says that"; the pt-BR text is in `runtime/phrases_pt_br.py`) and the card is sent again (`resend(force=True)`), so a word still approves it.
- **The card's object (superseded by the review round below).** The first choice was one sentence for a line that names the card's object and asks nothing the gate reads; it swallowed real requests ("lista o email do Joao", "encaminha o email"), so it was removed.
- **The event card is the event.** The card itself names the event, so the model's slot `event` (a title, a day, a number the line does not hold), an answer `event-create` (only when the line's own leading verb changes an event), extra slots (`calendar`, `service`) and a clarify of `event` no longer make the line a request (`ops_event_revise.card_decision`): the title and time slots are kept and checked as for any line. When the answer cannot be read at all (invalid JSON, `{}`) but the line's verb changes an event and the natural time engine reads its time by itself, the revision is built from that time (the card's day, the length kept); the interpreter's rule for who may decide a time decides (an hour that may be morning or evening is asked, a moment in the past is asked). It asks again only when the line has no time code can read and no title span that passed the check. The names-another rule, TIME-GUARD and the verb rule still apply. The stderr reasons an ask-again can have (`ASK_AGAIN_WHY` in `ops_interpret`) are: slot not a number written in the line, span not in the line, slot the op does not have, unknown op, parse invalid, time and weekday disagree, inherit refused, unknown frame item, policy refuses the op, model asked for a slot or between ops, private line sent to the route.
- **The subject finding (investigation only).** The card's subject read "Reuniao sobre o Openhora" even after S12. Which path built it cannot be told from the code alone (the maintainer's line and the model's answer are not recorded, by design), but every path that leaves a typed subject unaccented is a rule working as written: (1) the text was sent as written ("texto exato:"), where no model is called; (2) composing was off or limited for that line (prompt pin, rate, day or breaker limit, a description over 1,000 characters); (3) the model's answer was invalid or failed; (4) the model's subject is not "the same subject with its spelling fixed": `mail_compose.spelled` refuses a version that changes a capital inside a word typed with a capital, so "Openhora" answered as "OpenHora" (checked: `spelled("Reuniao sobre o Openhora", "Reuni[a-tilde]o sobre o OpenHora")` returns the typed subject; "Reuni[a-tilde]o sobre o Openhora" is taken). The revise path does not skip the rule it should apply: the subject is not the part the instruction names, the prompt says to change nothing else, and a revised card keeps the subject of the card it replaces, so a subject typed unaccented stays so through any number of changes. No code change.
- **The subject, word by word (same PR, after the finding above).** `mail_compose.spelled` took the model's version only when EVERY word passed the rule, so one word that failed ("OpenHora" for "Openhora") kept the whole typed subject unaccented. Now the checks of the whole subject come first and are unchanged (NFKC-stable, no format or control character, the typed scripts, the same non-letters in the same places, the same number of words in the same order, and the checks of a model-written subject); then each word is the model's when it passes the rule for a word (`_word_ok`: accents and the capital of the first letter of the first word for a word typed with a capital or a digit; the bounded inner edits for a word typed in lower case with letters only) and the typed word when it does not. "Reuniao sobre o Openhora" answered as "Reuni[a-tilde]o sobre o OpenHora" becomes "Reuni[a-tilde]o sobre o Openhora". A subject whose every word fails stays as typed byte for byte. The compose prompt is unchanged, so its pin is. Seven rows of the S12 tests that expected the whole typed subject to stay (one word failing, the others fine) now expect the merge; every other probe of the S12 lists, and the probes of the whole-subject checks, still keep the typed subject.
- **Review round of S13b.** (1) The verbs S13b added (write, end, sign, put, finish and their six-language forms, `WIDE_VERBS`) open the gate only with a locator ("ao final", "no fim", "at the end", "no inicio"...) or an object of the card ("o email", "the subject") in the same line; the words S13 had (`ORIGINAL_WORDS`) keep their rule. A block after a colon counts as a text asked for only when the line before it holds a verb (either list) and a locator or an object; "Lista de compras:\n..." and "Pensando aqui:\n..." open nothing. Thirteen ordinary lines of the review ("escreva para a Ana amanha", "termina a tarefa 3", "deixa", "write to Bob", "sign me up", "end", "pon una alarma a las 7"...) are tests: no revise call, they go on to their path; the live line still works. (2) RV_NOT_UNDERSTOOD_CARD is removed: a route or none decision is never answered for the card ("lista o email do Joao", "encaminha o email", "resume o texto do contrato", "o card de ontem foi aprovado?" go on). (3) The fallback of an event card does not take a distance from now ("daqui 2 horas", "em 30 minutos", "in 2 hours", "dentro de uma hora") onto the card's day, by the model or by code: it asks; it refuses a line that ends in a question mark; it is not used when the model answered route or none. (4) Minors: the model's words in a subject are taken only when every word the rule refuses was refused for its case alone ("OpenHora"), a word refused for being another word keeps the whole typed subject as S12 did; RV_ALREADY only when the text is at the place asked (end, start); quoted texts are looked for as written, only a block is compared with its white space flattened; `names_another` reads the original change words, so an event called "Sign Day" is still another event's name.

## 2026-10-23: a source with nothing in its window is empty, not missing; a list that shows what was just changed; a day that filters the agenda (S15c, no model call, no lab run)

The maintainer's retest (23/10 09:55, "Liste o ultimo email do meu Outlook"): the reply said the Outlook mailbox had not been added, but the source was registered and its newest snapshot (refreshed into version 2) read "0 messages, inbox, received 2026-10-16 09:49 to 2026-10-23 09:49". At 09:58 "mostra minha agenda de amanha" listed a haircut already moved and removed through approved cards, with a bare heading for the empty Google calendar.

- **Cause of the first (read in `ops_items.items`).** With a count ("o ultimo"), `_newest` dropped every mail source left with no items; with no message in any source `found` was empty, `refused` was empty, and `items` set `missing`, which `reply` says as "not added". The two meanings (never registered, registered but nothing in the window) were told apart by whether `found` was empty.
- **Fix.** `missing` is set only when no catalog row of the asked kind (and account, and source) matched; a registered source is never missing. `_newest` keeps a source that had no message to begin with and still drops one that the selection of the `count` newest left with none (a Gmail source that lost to a newer Outlook message is not called empty). An empty entry carries its `window` (`days` of the origin for a mailbox; the snapshot's own range, else the origin's past and ahead, for a calendar).
- **The phone's sentence.** One plain sentence in place of a bare heading: "Nenhum e-mail na caixa do Outlook nos ultimos 7 dias (verificado as 16:59)." and "Nenhum evento na Agenda do Google entre 17/10 e 31/10 (lido as 07:12).", "(lido ...)" when no check is later than the reading, "no ultimo dia" for a one-day window. With two sources the one with items is listed and the empty one gets its sentence. Keys appended in both tables (`NW_ITEMS_EMPTY_*`, `NW_ITEMS_STATE_*`, `NW_ITEMS_NAMEIN_*`). The terminal and the page keep an empty source as its heading; `items` gains `window` on an empty entry only.
- **A list that shows what the person just changed.** The 09:51 snapshot was younger than `FRESH_S`, so it was not read again. `ops_step_effects.approve`, the one place an approved effect is recorded as done, now calls `ops_items.mark_changed` (it reads no provider and cannot fail the approval): for a calendar effect (create, change, cancel; Google or Outlook) or a mailbox effect that changes a listing (archive, move, trash) the check times of that account's mail or calendar sources are forgotten and the sources are marked (`_DIRTY`); the next list on a phone reads them again whatever the age, and a successful read clears the mark. A draft, a send and the other account change nothing.
- **The day asked for.** `ops_items._range_args` reads "de amanha", "de hoje", "de sexta", "dia 20" with the time engine (`event_lookup._day_named`) and "desta semana" / "semana que vem" from a closed list of week words in the six languages; `items` takes `first` and `last` and keeps the events listed under those days (the numbers stay the snapshot's own); a phone's list opens with "Agenda de amanha, dom 18/10:". **The time engine reads Portuguese and English only today**, so a day word in another language names no day (the whole list is the answer) while its week words work. A day with nothing is the empty sentence for that day ("Nenhum evento na Agenda do Google amanha (lido as 09:51).").
- **Tests.** `runtime/tests/test_items_empty.py` and `runtime/tests/test_items_changed.py`; the stand-in provider's events carry an etag that moves with a change, as a real provider's does (a listing with the same ids and etags is unchanged by design). Restoring the old drop in `_newest` and the old test for `missing` fails five of the ten empty-source cases, the live one first.
- **Not done.** No model call, no lab run; the S15b freshness and line forms are untouched.

## 2026-10-23: the file alone, with one caption (S18b, no model call, no lab run)

The maintainer's retest of S18, his words translated from Portuguese: "we can stop sending the text of the files, the file itself is enough". The live line ("bring me the file Copy of the-first-sin-activities from the folder /openhora", with `send-files` on) gave a long preview of the Markdown file AND the file.

- **The reply.** A sender who holds `send-files`, on an app that carries the file, gets the file with one caption, "Here is the file <name> (<account>, folder <folder>).", and never the text (`_read_match` returns `sent`; the text is read only to be scanned). Every case in which the file is withheld gets its own sentence and no preview: a credential (as before), a file over the limit (it used to get the preview and the sentence), a type the app does not take, a provider that refuses or cannot carry it, a right that changed. In the bridge a dropped file now replaces the caption by the sentence alone ("the right changed" / "the file did not arrive") instead of adding it after a caption that promised a file; a file that Telegram refuses after the message went out keeps the caption and gets the sentence after it (the message cannot be taken back). A sender with `read-files` alone gets the text, as before. New keys, append-only: `FS_FILE`, `FS_FILE_ACCOUNT`, `FR_HEAD_ACCOUNT`, `FR_ITEM_ACCOUNT` (`FS_SENT` is no longer used and stays).
- **The folder label, and its cause.** The live reply said "pasta Google Drive". The file sat at the configured folder itself, whose real name is read by one `stat` of its id (S17b); `_walk` gave that read `min(ROOT_NAME_SECONDS, MAX_SECONDS / 4)` = 2 seconds, taken from the walk's own ten, from the start of the provider process (token refresh, HTTPS) to its answer. A name that was late or failed fell back to the service's own name, which `_render` printed as if it were the folder. **Cause, found in the code and reproduced with a slow `stat`; not reproduced live (no network here):** the 2-second budget. Fixes: the names are read together and with a time of their own, 6 seconds (never more than four fifths of the walk's, so a silent provider is still given up), and the walk's ten seconds start after them; and a name that still is not back is LEFT OUT, never replaced: a file at the root is "(Google Drive)" with no folder, and a file in a subfolder keeps its own name ("pasta openhora"). The Drive root stays "Meu Drive" / "My Drive". The same label rule holds for the text of a reader and for a numbered list (`FR_HEAD_ACCOUNT`, `FR_ITEM_ACCOUNT`). The S17b test that pinned "pasta Google Drive › openhora" was changed to the new rule.
- **Cost.** A search whose provider is silent now waits up to 6 seconds for the names and then up to 10 for the walk (it waited 10 in all). Not built: reading the names while the walk runs and filling them in late.


## 2026-10-24: the whole suite runs in three shards behind the one required check `tests` (T3, no model call, no lab run)

The maintainer (PLAN-BRAIN-2026-10-23, section 7): the whole-suite runs take 19 to 32 minutes (19:12 on the newest read, 29:06 and 31:59 before it), and the tests are to be made faster without losing coverage. T3 shards them. Nothing else of CI changes.

- **Design.** A matrix job `tests-shard` (`shard: [1, 2, 3]`, check runs `tests shard <k> of 3`, `fail-fast: false`, 30 minutes) runs the whole-suite step of before (`WB_FULL_FUZZ=1`, the same `$(python3 scripts/test_dirs.py)` targets, `-n auto`) through `scripts/shard_tests.py`, which loads the pytest plugin `scripts/pytest_plugins/wb_shard.py`. After every other collection hook (`trylast`) the plugin keeps the collected items whose 0-based position `i` has `i % 3 == k - 1` and reports the rest as deselected. The job `tests` keeps its key and its name, gains `needs: tests-shard` and `if: always()`, runs its two selected steps unchanged, and in whole mode runs a gate step that fails unless `needs.tests-shard.result` is exactly `success`.
- **Why `always()`.** Without it a failed shard makes GitHub skip the dependent job, and a skipped required check counts as passing. A matrix job's result is `success` only when every leg succeeded; a failed, cancelled, timed-out or skipped shard fails `tests`. `scripts/tests/test_checks_wiring.py` reads the workflow, evaluates the conditions for eight events, and plants four broken gates (no `always()`, a shard left out of the matrix, a gate that does not compare, shards that run on fewer events than the gate waits for) that it must catch.
- **Why round-robin by item, with no stored durations.** Every collected item is in exactly one shard by construction (proved offline for every N up to 5 and every length up to 40, and under xdist and without it, on real runs of the launcher), and a new test file is collected as before, so it is never left out. No durations file has to be kept current; interleaving by position spreads every file and every parametrized family over the shards (the 36 full-fuzz cases go 12, 12, 12). The cost: a module- or session-scoped fixture runs once per shard and worker instead of once per worker. Every shard collects the same items (same commit, image, environment and targets), and pytest-xdist already refuses workers that collect differently, so a non-deterministic collection cannot pass silently.
- **Why a launcher.** The root `conftest.py` and `pytest.ini` are not touched (a change to either makes the selector run every test folder in the hook), and `PYTHONPATH` would be a new variable in every process the tests start. The launcher puts the one folder `scripts/pytest_plugins/` at the front of the controller's `sys.path`; pytest-xdist carries it to its workers.
- **The single point of the required name.** In the workflow, the one line `name: tests` of the aggregator; in the tests, the constant `REQUIRED_CHECKS = ("validate", "tests")` of `scripts/tests/test_checks_wiring.py`, which mirrors the `required_status_checks` of the `main` ruleset. A rename changes those two lines, `AGENTS.md`, the docs that name the job, and the ruleset (the maintainer's action). Shard check runs are named `tests shard <k> of 3`, which can never equal a required name, and a wiring test asserts it. The maintainer has still to confirm the required-check name before this reaches `main`.
- **python39 stays unsharded.** It runs its fixed list on every pull request and in every whole-suite run (12 to 19 minutes), so after T3 the wall time of a whole-suite run is bounded by python39 (about 17 to 19 minutes), not by the shards: the release run goes from about 30 to about 19 minutes, not to 10 to 12, until python39 is addressed. T4 (pending a maintainer decision) changes what python39 runs on pull requests, so a shard now would be redone; and python39 is not a required check (`docs/backlog.md`, N10 proposes making it one, which would need its own aggregator).
- **Principle 13.** Saved: the whole-suite `tests` job (19 to 32 minutes) becomes three jobs of about a third of the pytest time each, plus about 1.5 minutes of setup; the measured shard times and the run's wall time are recorded in the pull request. Costs: two more runners per whole-suite run (setup and collection, a few runner-minutes), and fixtures that run once per shard and worker. Safety given up: none. The same tests run, with the same full fuzz, under the same required name, and the gate fails closed on a shard that failed, was cancelled, timed out or was skipped.
- **Principle 14.** `pytest-split` balances by a stored per-test durations file (about 21,000 node ids that change with every renamed test) and adds a pinned dependency to the CI environment; `pytest-shard` does the same round-robin as here but adds a third-party plugin to the release gate for about 30 lines. The saving of adopting either is smaller than the new supply-chain surface and the upkeep, so this is built in-house, in a form that a later adoption can replace without touching the workflow's semantics.
- **Not done.** No sharding of python39; no change of the ruleset or branch protection; no stored durations.


## 2026-10-24: "Shall I go ahead?" before a read or an act asked in words, and the limits it touches (BL2a, no model call, no lab run)

The maintainer (his words translated from Portuguese: PLAN-BRAIN-2026-10-23, section 3, "go ahead along this line"; DECISIONS.md of 2026-10-24, "I accept all your recommendations for the open decisions"): a read or an act asked in words on a messaging app first gets a sentence code wrote from the typed call and runs only after the person's yes. This is the operations side. It ships switched off (`plan_confirm.ACTIVE` False): the bridge cannot take a yes to the question until the package that owns it (BL2b) lands, so nothing asks and the S20 verb check stays; BL2b sets it True, deletes the verb lists and updates the tests that then meet a question.

- **The five decisions.** (1) The policy is the group `confirm-reads-off`, because a group is a grant and a profile that lists its groups would lose a confirmation that the absence of a group meant: the question is on for every sender, and turning it off for the reads is a widening typed with `accept-config --widen` (in the documents, "the policy confirm-reads"). (2) Research, `ask` and the acts asked in words (cancel, stop-task, retry, routine-pause, attachment-add, attachment-discard, reject, release) always ask, whatever the group says. (3) A yes is read by code alone (`natural.confirm_kind`, under L64's conditions, which the bridge checks); a model's `confirm yes` on the plan item gets one sentence asking the person to reply to the question, a model's `no` drops the plan, a model's operation states a new plan. (4) Notices keep their own confirmation. (5) Messaging only.
- **The plan.** `runtime/plan_confirm.py`: the sentence from the phrase keys `UNDERSTOOD_*` (en and pt-BR; the other languages get en with the values quoted), kept in memory for 600 seconds as a `plan-confirm` item of `pending_items` and bound to the sha256 of the operation, its arguments, the sender, the project and the line; `take` pops it only for its id AND the hash recomputed from its own state. `ops_say.say(plan_ok=...)` runs a confirmed plan once through `ops_natural.run(confirmed=True)`, which asks the policy again. The typed frame gains the `plan-confirm` item (rule R8: id, kind, need `confirm`, op, age; the prompt text is unchanged, its sha256 stays `5bee55ce`), and `last` holds the plan's own words so that "no, for tomorrow" is a new interpreter turn that revises the plan.
- **The P0 amendments of contracts/runtime.md, in the maintainer's words of 2026-10-24.** L63 (1), "messaging only": a page or terminal line reaches the model route only by a new dated decision, because the daily route's retention is not confirmed. L63 (2): the whole served catalog is pinned, no per-operation pins, a change is a new dated entry. L63 (3): "tier-1 precedence is replaced explicitly as the tables shrink, never silently dropped": the precedence is listed in order and a claimer is removed only with its named replacement. L54: the S20 verb lists (`command_words.vet`) are replaced by the plan's OK; the number written in the line stays the rule. L52 and L53: the two existing exceptions are named (`event_lookup.refresh`, the live Drive and OneDrive walk of `ops_file_read._search`). L49 and L61 are drafted in a "Drafted, not in force" subsection and take effect only at Phase 2 by a new decision. L65 is new.
- **The research brake (operations side).** A running search is recorded in memory for its sender and stopped by that sender's stop word (`ops_research.stop`, a closed list in six languages in `plan_confirm.STOP_WORDS`), through the cancel path that stops what runs; its reply is `RS_CANCELLED`. The word's text in the acknowledgement and the bridge's reading of the word are BL2b's; the 0.60 default cap and its message are RL1's.
- **Where the code differs from the spec.** (a) `ops_research.stop` cancels without naming the messaging channel to `cancel_request`: that path asks the group `cancel` of the sender, which a sender who may search need not hold, and the stop word answers the sender's own search; the policy it asks instead is `research`, now, and the stop label is written by hand with the sender's profile as R18's is ("user from messaging, profile <name>"). `RS_CANCELLED` is the reply only to a stop that came through `stop`; a search cancelled from the page, the terminal or the board keeps `ASK_CANCELLED`. (b) A plan's line is the person's line followed by the earlier words of theirs that a correction inherited and the line does not hold, because the search and the file read check their span against the line they run with; the hash binds that line. (c) A week asked for a list of items is written as a range ("Mon 26/10/2026" to "Sun 01/11/2026"), because the read narrows to the week. (d) `_complete`, which answers a draft, calls no `run`, so it needed no `confirmed` argument.
- **Principle 13.** Saved: no verb list to keep in six languages once BL2b deletes them, and the meaning of a model's reading of an act is checked by the person on a sentence code wrote, not by words. Given up: one more message before every read for a sender who has not turned the question off, at most 600 seconds of a plan in memory, and a yes the person meant for something else while the question is the last message (the question says what it will do). The stricter rule stays: the group is a widening, never a default.
- **Not done / not verified.** No model call and no lab run; the bridge's dispatcher, the Yes and No buttons, the two-question guard and the stop word's reading are BL2b's; whether `ops_research.stop` ends a contained run within seconds is the existing R18 path, tested here with a stand-in run.
## 2026-10-24: the default research cap is 0.60 USD, and the cap's message says what to do next (RL1)

No model call, no lab run, no skill file changed.

- **Authority.** The maintainer, 2026-10-24: "Aceito todas as suas recomendações para as decisões em aberto." Adopted with it: the default per-run research cap goes from 0.25 to 0.60 USD, the project override in `runtime.json` (the key `research`) stays, and the cap message says what to do next. (`validate: allow english-only -- the maintainer's words, quoted`)
- **Live evidence (2026-10-23).** A weather question ("Como vai estar a previsao do tempo de amanha?") on `claude-sonnet-5-5` with the web on stopped at the cap: 0.254 USD against 0.25, 86k tokens, 34 s, and the phone got a sentence with no next step. The same question for another city succeeded at 0.227 USD (162k tokens, 42 s), and a third weather question stopped at the cap again. So one lookup sits at the edge of 0.25; 0.60 is about 2.4 times that.
- **What changed.** `RESEARCH_DEFAULTS["max_cost_usd"]` is 0.6 (`runtime/project_config.py`; the bounds 0.01 to 5 are unchanged). `RS_CAP` in both phrase tables now adds: try again, or raise `research.max_cost_usd` in the project's `runtime.json` and accept the configuration again. The sentence carries no number and no field. Tests: the sentence in both languages, a vacuity case for the old sentence, and the default with a project value still winning.
- **A project with no `research.max_cost_usd`.** It gets the new default without a new acceptance, because its file's hash does not change. The per-day count still bounds it: 10 runs of 0.60 USD is 6.00 USD a day at most on a metered credential, instead of 2.50. A subscription run has no billed cost. The research skill belongs to no agent, so no agent's daily cap counts it.
- **Not a confirmation or policy path.** The bound is a read operation's; the group `research` (in no default policy), `accept_config` and the adapter's `--max-cost-usd` enforcement are unchanged. Raising the key changes the file's hash, so the runtime asks for `accept-config` again; that is not a widening.
- **The R2-F1 entry** keeps 0.25 as the record of the decision made then.
- **Deferred.** Cancel by words ("cancela", "pare") ships with the D5 frame dispatcher, in another spec.
- **Principle 13.** Saved: a research question that needs a little more than 0.25 USD is answered at the first try instead of failing and being asked again (a retry costs another full run; two of three live weather questions hit the cap), and a stop on the cap tells the person the two ways forward. Safety given up: the default per-run ceiling rises from 0.25 to 0.60 USD for a project that has not set its own, without a new acceptance; the worst metered day goes from 2.50 to 6.00 USD. The group stays off until the person widens it, the adapter still enforces the cap, and the project can set any value from 0.01 to 5.

## 2026-10-24: the card and the phone name the message a reply answers by its number, its sender and the time it came (BL4, S16C-REPLY-DATE; no model call, no lab run, fingerprint unchanged)

The maintainer (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-24): "S16C-REPLY-DATE: yes, inside B''-lite (mail part); a hashed-document change, reviewed by Opus." The phone's reply line named the message answered as `inbox#3`, with no date to tell it from another message of the same sender.

- **An optional key, old hashes kept.** The reply of an own-account e-mail has the seven keys of `REPLY_KEYS` and, only when the index recorded the time, `received` (`REPLY_OPTIONAL`; UTC, `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ`). `_own_of` copies it only when it is there, so a document made without it has exactly its old keys and its old hash (the literal `send-37567b7e2315990c0a9875f4c714a57f` of the test fixture was computed in a copy of 5b684f5 and at the base of the change; an open card made before approves unchanged), and a document with it has it inside `key_of`'s hash: one changed character is another key and `check` refuses the document. `check` accepts the seven keys or the seven and `received`, and a present `received` that is not null, the closed shape and a date that exists is refused with a usage error (never a crash on the card). `reply_of(facts)` builds the `reply` and leaves a null time out.
