- **The index and its read-back.** `sources_mail.mail_index_entry(n, message, scan, moment)` writes `received` from the moment the snapshot already shows for the message: the provider's received time (Outlook's `receivedDateTime`, Gmail's `internalDate`) and, when Gmail gives no `internalDate` (a raw answer without it, or an .eml), the sender's own `Date` header (`providers/mailbox/gmail.py` `received_at`): a well-shaped time the sender chose, shown as the snapshot already shows it, which injects no text and chooses no recipient (null when it has none, and null with the other words of an entry the credential scan hits); `item_refs._entry` reads it back through the shape and a date that exists (anything else, `2026-02-30T10:00:00Z` included, is null; an entry with no key is null, and the index's digest is over the stored bytes, so an old snapshot stays valid). `mail_refs.reply_facts` carries it. `SIGNATURE_MARK` is not raised: an old snapshot keeps its index until the next refresh makes a version, and its card shows the line it always showed.
- **The card.** With a time, the line is `CARD_MAIL_REPLY_TO_AT` (the sender, the number, the time the message came in the machine's zone as `Thu 22/10/2026 14:05`, the read time, the subject), in `CARD_PRIVATE` like the line it replaces; without one it is `CARD_MAIL_REPLY_TO`. The shown time is a function of the hashed one, so the card shows what the hash covers and nothing the hash does not.
- **The phone keeps the number.** `PHONE_LINE` maps the new line to `PHONE_MAIL_REPLY_TO_AT`: the number stays (S16c: a phone never drops a line that names the target), the read time goes, the received time is new, and `localize_dates` says the weekday in the project's language.
- **Limit L60.** The time is shown and chooses nothing; the recipient checks are unchanged.
- **Not done.** The draft of a reply in the mailbox family (`CARD_BOX_DRAFT_REPLY`, `mailbox_document.py`) is a follow-up. `step_send_email.py` is assigned to BL4 by the supervisor (no other package touches it): its one line is now `reply = send_document.reply_of(facts)`, and no other place builds a reply document from `REPLY_KEYS` (grep).
- **Principle 13.** Saved: nothing is measured or re-run; the change is code and offline tests. Cost: a new optional key in a hashed document, bound by the same hash and guarded by the literal hash of an old document; the safety given up is none that the tests do not hold (an old hash, a bound key, a closed shape, a private line).

## 2026-10-24: slow meta-tests move to a nightly run; the Python 3.9 import runs on Python 3.9 only (T2b, no model call, no lab run)

**Authority.** The maintainer (DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-24) accepted all recommendations for the open decisions, among them: "T2 (rest): the select_tests oracle, checks_wiring and the security-scan test move from always-run to nightly, plus whenever the CI wiring changes. Cost: a selector regression is seen within 24 h; the scan still blocks every commit (hook step 2, validate job)." "T3: the required check keeps the name `tests` (the aggregator)." "T4: accept 24 h detection of a 3.9 break in unselected code; python39 full at nightly and release." T2b is the rest of row T2 of the plan; the map refresh belongs to T5 and is not here.

- **What moved, and what still guards each one.**
  - The import oracle (`real_import_oracle` and its test, 18 s locally and 49 s in CI) moved to `scripts/tests/test_select_tests_oracle.py`. `test_select_tests.py` stays a guard and keeps the three planted-selector tests on a fictional tree, so a change to the selector's rules is still caught on every commit; what waits for the night is a miss that only the real tree shows (a new import form).
  - `test_checks_wiring.py` (10 s for its slowest test, 20 s the file in CI) left `GUARDS`. It reads the workflows, the hook and the test-folder list; rule 5 selects all of `scripts/tests` for a change to `.github/**`, `.githooks/**`, the selector, the slot helper, and now also `scripts/test_dirs.py`, `scripts/shard_tests.py` and `scripts/pytest_plugins/**` (`WIRING_FILES`, `WIRING_DIRS`). `test_hook_parallel.py` stays a guard.
  - The whole-tree security scan (9.7 s locally, 17.8 s in CI) moved to `scripts/tests/test_security_scan_repo.py`. It repeated two checks that run on every commit and every pull request: hook step 2 (`scripts/security_scan.py --strict`, before the selector is called) and a step of the `validate` job (a required check, with no condition). `test_security_scan.py` stays a guard and is stricter: it asserts that the hook step is a failing step run under `set -euo pipefail` and before the selection, that the `validate` job runs the scan with `--strict`, and that neither an `if:` nor `continue-on-error` is on that job; two planted workflows (a skipping condition, a tolerated failure) must be seen. The scan gives up nothing.
  - `test_imports_on_the_interpreter_running_the_tests` of `test_runtime_python39.py` (27 s in CI on Python 3.11) is skipped on any interpreter other than 3.9, where each module's own tests import it; on 3.9 it is the real check, and the `python39` job runs it on every pull request and at night. The file stays in `GUARDS`; its name and `ON_SYSTEM_PYTHON` do not change.
- **The nightly run** (`.github/workflows/nightly.yml`). Daily at 06:17 UTC; by hand (`workflow_dispatch`, optional `ref`); and on a pull request that gets the label `nightly`, which is how a change to the file is tried. `nightly-shard` runs the whole list (`$(python3 scripts/test_dirs.py)`) with the full fuzz in three shards through `scripts/shard_tests.py`; `nightly-python39` runs the python39 list of `checks.yml` in full (a wiring test holds the two path lists equal). A failing test is run again up to three times (`--reruns 3`); a test that then passes shows as `RERUN`, one that fails every time fails the job. The report is the `FAILED`, `ERROR` and `RERUN` lines in each job's summary, and GitHub mails a failed scheduled run to whoever last edited the cron line; nothing is written to the repository, no issue is opened, no artifact is kept. The workflow is read-only (`contents: read`), uses no secret, and no job is named like a required check. No `${{ }}` appears in a `run:` line (they are under `with:` and `env:` only), and a wiring test enforces it. The docker, converter and Go jobs are not nightly: nothing in the decision asks for it, and they run in the whole-suite runs of `checks.yml`. A scheduled workflow is disabled by GitHub after 60 days without activity in a public repository.
- **The schedule limit.** GitHub starts `schedule` (and `workflow_dispatch`) only from a workflow file on the default branch, `main`, and the release merges there only after v3. The repository variable `NIGHTLY_REF` names the branch the scheduled run tests (unset: `main`, which lacks `scripts/shard_tests.py` until the release, so the run would fail every night). A `pull_request` event uses the workflow file of the pull request's merge commit, so the label `nightly` tries this file before it reaches `main`.
- **Decided by the maintainer, 2026-10-24: option A.** The same `nightly.yml`, byte for byte, goes to `main` now in a one-file pull request that the maintainer merges (merges into `main` are his); the supervisor sets `NIGHTLY_REF` to `feature/delegation-platform`; the variable is cleared when the release merges, and the identical file arrives with the release. Risk to `main`: none to its code; the file is read-only, uses no secret and runs only the checked-out branch's own scripts.
- **Pins (principle 14).** `pytest-rerunfailures`, an existing and maintained plugin, adopted rather than built, nightly only (never in the hook or in the required `tests` check, where a flaky test still fails): `16.7` (released 2026-09-17) with pytest 9.1.1 on Python 3.11, and `16.0.1` (released 2025-09-02) with pytest 8.4.2 on Python 3.9, because every release from 16.1 on requires Python 3.10 or newer; both import on their interpreter (checked with `uv`). The probe of the rerun and its report (a test that fails once, a test that always fails, run through the shard launcher with `WB_FULL_FUZZ=1`) printed `RERUN` and `FAILED` lines, 1 failed, 1 passed, 4 reruns.
- **A limit found.** The selector follows references, so a change to a runtime module can still select the oracle and the scan test file by name through the import graph (the oracle names `runtime/natural_time.py` in a string); what is gone is their selection on every commit and for a document or a skill text. The vacuity case of the selector tests therefore uses documents (none of the three runs) and asserts only that a runtime module does not select the whole folder `scripts/tests`.
- **Principle 13.** Saved: about 113 CPU-seconds on every hook run and every selected CI run that selects only the guards (48.7 + 20 + 17.8 + 27 s, the analysis' CI figures) and about 49 s of critical path (the oracle). Measured locally on the guard list (`README.md` changed, `-n auto`, warm `uv`): 57 s at the base, 15 s here. The nightly adds four runners once a day on a public repository. Safety given up: a selector miss that only the real tree shows is found at the next nightly, within 24 h, instead of at the commit (the planted-selector tests still run on every commit, and a change to the selector or the CI wiring still runs all three meta-tests); a Python 3.9 import break is seen in the `python39` job, which runs on every pull request, instead of also on 3.11, where it was not a 3.9 check. The scan gives up nothing. The 24 h holds only once the schedule fires (option A above); until then the gap is covered by the whole-suite runs of `checks.yml` (the label `full-suite`, a manual run, the release).

## 2026-10-24: an item named by its words, grounded by code; the number slots stay (B''-lite, BL1, no model call, no lab run)

PLAN-BRAIN v3 section 1 (approved by the maintainer on 2026-10-23, his words translated: "You may go ahead along these lines.") says that the operations that act on an existing item take a target span plus an optional day instead of a number, and that code grounds the span with the existing search. The live failure it fixes (2026-10-23): "Remova o corte de cabelo de amanha da agenda" was asked again, while "Remova o evento corte de cabelo da agenda" opened the card. The maintainer accepted every recommendation of the open decisions on 2026-10-24 (translated: "I accept all your recommendations for the open decisions."), among them that a catalog change is a new dated entry and that no row is pinned alone.

- **The slots are added, the numbers stay.** `event-change` and `event-cancel` take `target` and `day`; `mail-reply`, `mail-archive`, `mail-move`, `mail-mark` and `mail-trash` take `target` and `day`; `mail-draft` takes `target`. `event`, `message` and `messages` stay. Removing them would break the frozen corpora (72 `event-change`, 44 `event-cancel`, 53 `mail-reply`, 57 `mail-draft`, 37 `mail-archive`, 35 `mail-trash`, 34 `mail-move` and 56 `mail-mark` items of the dev file have golds with those slots, and the replay gate scores against them), and a number written in the line is the exact form that also keeps the rule that a decision number must be written in the line. The model is told so in the `what` text of each row.
- **Grounded by code.** `event_lookup._by_target` searches the closest name over the snapshot rows and the events created here with the verified target (the new time and the new title are not cut from it) and the day; `natural_writes_mail.target_words` turns a mail target into the words that must all be whole words of the index's from-address or subject (`match_words`, unchanged), and `ops_natural_mail._on_day` keeps the messages whose `received` falls on the day; files already search by their `file` span. One match is the card, several are a numbered list, none is one sentence. A day code cannot read, and an index with no `received`, are the sentence that a date is not guessed.
- **A day the engine cannot read.** `event_lookup._day_named` reads no relative day before today ("ontem", "yesterday" are `none`) and reads a date written with no year as the next such day; the day of a mail is a past day, so those lines get the sentence that a message is not found by its date, as they did before. A reader of past days is a later change.
- **The entry.** `runtime/interpreter_enable.json` gains one dated entry that carries every pin of R2-A and names the new catalog hash (`interpreter_catalog_blite` of `scripts/tests/pinned_hashes.json`); the epoch file retires the earlier catalog and serves the new one. The entry says it is served before its measurement: the release to main waits for the measurement (BL3) and the maintainer's decision. `check_operation` is unchanged (its source hash pin stands).
- **One card or a list.** `plural` is set on a mail seed only when the target or the line holds a noun of the tables' `plural` list (`natural_writes_mail.plural_mentioned`, the words the closed form reads as several messages): then every match (up to 20) is one card; otherwise one match is the card and several are a numbered list of five that a number picks, as PLAN-BRAIN section 1 says (an independent review found that marking every archive, trash, move and mark target plural put up to 20 unrelated matches on one card).
- **Principle 13.** Saved: no new operation, no per-row pin, no change to the check, the prompt, the protocol or the route; the corpora and the replay gate stay valid. Given up: a line that names an item by its words is read by the interpreter's model, not by the closed forms, and a model that gives a span the line does not hold is a clarify as before.
- **Not done.** No model call, no lab run, no measurement; no reader of past days for a mail.

## 2026-10-24: the long-line dev file of B''-lite and its measurement plan, written down and waiting for "pode" (BL3, no model call, no lab run, fingerprint unchanged)

BL1 added the `target` and `day` slots and BL2a the `plan-confirm` frame; neither has been measured. This package adds `evals/interpreter/dev-long-v1.jsonl`, 46 long, fictional lines (pt-BR 24, en 10, es, fr, de and it 3 each; event-cancel 10, event-change 6, mail-reply 4, mail-archive 2, mail-trash 2, mail-mark 1, mail-move 1, cancel 5, stop-task 1, items 6, file-read 2, research 3, answer 1, none 1, route 1; five lines with a `plan-confirm` frame), and `runtime/tests/test_interpreter_dev_long.py`, which holds its form offline.

- **What it is and is not.** It is dev evidence, authored by the specification and not labelled blind; it is a new file because `dev.jsonl` is frozen and the replay gate draws its samples from it (adding lines there would move the draw). It is never the held-out split and its result never replaces the frozen gate's. No threshold, no evidence kind and no measurement constant changed.
- **The measurement plan** (in `evals/interpreter/README.md`, "The long-line dev file and its measurement plan"): M1 the 46 lines, 2 runs, on `or-deepseek-v4.1-flash` (92 calls, about US$ 0.04) and on `acct-claude-sonnet-5.5` (92 calls, the maintainer's account); M2 the regression draw of 50 items of `dev.jsonl` (seed 20261025, 1 run) on both routes, against the recorded events (DeepSeek 46 correct, Sonnet 48); M3, optional, the spent test split on the daily route (about US$ 0.70). Metered cost about US$ 0.06 without M3, about US$ 0.76 with it.
- **The pass rule, against the frozen `thresholds.json`:** 0 unconfirmed effects in every event; pt-BR and en `correct_direct` at least 0.85 and `clarify_rate` at most 0.15; the `wrong_card` point rate at most 0.03; the Wilson bounds reported as "not decidable at this n" (0 of 92 is about 4.0%); M2 `correct` not below the recorded count minus 2 and no `wrong_*` above its recorded count; the daily route's p95 at most 4,000 ms and median cost at most US$ 0.002. A rule not met is published as it is and the maintainer decides; the interpreter stays on only through BL1's dated entry ("served before its measurement"); never silently on, never a threshold edited.
- **Waiting for "pode".** Nothing was run: the runs need the maintainer's explicit go-ahead, and the supervisor makes them from its own clone.
- **Principle 13.** Saved: no new evidence kind, no change to the harness or the gate, 92 plus 50 calls instead of the full battery. Given up: at 46 lines the bounds that need hundreds of runs stay undecidable, and the golds were not labelled blind.

## 2026-10-24: D5 on the messaging bridge: the OK of a "Posso?", the two-question guard, the research brake that names its word (B''-lite, BL2b, no model call, no lab run)

**Authority.** PLAN-BRAIN v3 section 3 (approved by the maintainer on 2026-10-23): "One dispatcher decides a "sim" or a correction across all pending kinds, under L64's conditions: last message; not forwarded; private chat, or reply_to; e-mail needs a reply." The maintainer, 2026-10-24: the cancel by words ships with the D5 frame dispatcher, and, on the research acknowledgement, "vamos informar que caso a pessoa queira cancelar, qual palavra ela deve enviar, algo como 'Pesquisando... Digite 'Pare' para cancelar'".

- **The order of the chain** (`Bridge._handle`): the drafts of the sender, then the OK of a plan (`plan_line`), then the card words (`word_line`), then the research brake (`research_stop_line`), then the change of a card (`revise_line`), then the project, the help, a file, a reminder, a write form, `say`. The OK of a plan is before the card words because a plan's question is no decision card (`NOT_DECISION`); the brake is after them so that "no" with a decision card as the last message still rejects the card (L64 first).
- **What an OK is.** A yes or no word code reads (`natural.confirm_kind`) on a private line of a listed sender, not forwarded, when the question is the LAST message the platform sent to the chat (`last_sent`, None while a send is in flight and after a restart) or the line replies to it; on e-mail only a reply (its first line). The question's Yes and No buttons name their message and need no such rule. The confirmed call is a job labelled with the plan's operation, so a read takes no model slot and research and `ask` take it (a busy project answers `STILL_WORKING` and the plan is not spent); the operations layer takes the plan once (id AND hash) and asks the policy again. A correction ("no, for tomorrow") is no word: it reaches `say`, where the interpreter reads it with the plan as a frame item.
- **The two-question guard.** A decision card is not decided by a bare word while ANY question of a plan in this chat still waits (live: inside its 600 s, neither taken nor dropped): one sentence (`PLAN_TWO_OPEN`), the person replies to the message they mean or uses the card's buttons; a reply to the card still decides the card. There is no "the question came first" condition: `last_sent` follows the order sends start, a ledger record is added when its send returns, so two threads can record the card before the question (and `sent_at` is taken at the same moment); a question sent after the card is the last message and `plan_line` takes the word first.
- **The brake.** A stop word (`command_words.stop_form`, re-exported by `plan_confirm`: the layer map lets the bridge import `command_words` and not `plan_confirm`, so the closed lists and the function moved there) or a no word stops the sender's own running research; the bridge asks `ops.research_stop` for each project that lists the sender, the operations layer checks the key and the policy, and the run answers `RS_CANCELLED` itself. On e-mail the line must reply to a message this bridge sent to the chat (a bounded list of ids, in memory).
- **The acknowledgement.** `ACK_RESEARCH` now says the word: "Searching... Send 'Stop' to cancel." and, in pt-BR, "Pesquisando... Digite 'Pare' para cancelar." (es, fr, de and it get the English text); a test holds the quoted word, folded, in the stop list of each language. A confirmed plan's line is a bare "sim", a card word, which the indicator and the acknowledgement never serve: the confirmed call is shown them under a neutral word so that the research acknowledgement is still sent.
- **The switch.** `plan_confirm.ACTIVE` is True; the S20 verb lists (`names`, `VERBS`, `OBJECT`, `NOUNS`, `NEGATION`, `CONTRACTION`, `CONDITION`, `LINE_WORDS_MAX`) and the `again` branch of the vet are deleted, replaced by the person's OK; the number rule (`decision_numbers`) stays. Existing tests that now meet a question were given `confirm-reads-off` (reads) or the "sim" step (acts, research, ask); none was deleted or weakened, and the lines the lists refused ("nao cancela o pedido 3") now assert the question and that nothing ran before the OK.
- **Not enforced by code (stated, L65).** A yes meant for something else while the question is the last message is taken as the OK; a plan is not dropped when the line after its question raises or finds the route busy (`ops_say.say` drops it only when `_turn` returns), so only L64's last-message condition protects it: the bridge's `RUN_BUSY`, `QUEUED` or error sentence follows the question and ends the "last message", and a reply to the question can still confirm it within 600 s.
- **Principle 13.** Saved: no new operation, no new store, no change to the prompt, the catalog, the replay gate or the measurement (fingerprint unchanged). Cost: every read in words on a messaging app now costs one round trip unless the person turns it off with `confirm-reads-off`; a phone's sentences gain one question. The safety given up is none that the tests do not hold.
- **Not done.** No model call, no lab run, no measurement; the page and the terminal run what they ran.

## 2026-10-25: the first B''-lite measurement published as it was, a re-score under widened span golds labelled "not blind", and the recorded answers committed (BL5A, no model call, no lab run, fingerprint unchanged)

**Authority.** The maintainer, 2026-10-25 (said in Portuguese, here in English): yes, make adjustment A (the expected answers, and a replay at no cost), B (teach the correction after "Posso?") and C (investigate the double date), and ask for the go-ahead before measuring again. This entry is adjustment A. Principle 10 ("a result is published whether it passed or not") and `evals/README.md` ("a case changes for a stated reason, never to raise a score").

**The first result, as published (run on `81c1274`, catalog `a77f8a8f...`, prompt `5bee55ce...`, frozen `thresholds.json`; events M1 DeepSeek `20261025T033351Z-4d5c4ce4`, M2 DeepSeek `20261025T033519Z-4ffcb9b2`, M1 Sonnet `20261025T033607Z-72f2f30b`, M2 Sonnet `20261025T034023Z-0b0cc69e`; 0 errors, 0 parse failures).** It stays as it was scored, on `dev-long-v1.jsonl`:

| Rule (SPEC-BLITE-3-MEASURE section 5) | DeepSeek | Sonnet | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1. `wrong_effect_unconfirmed` = 0 (hard) | 0 (M1, M2) | 0 (M1, M2) | pass |
| 2. M1 `correct_direct` >= 0.85, pt-BR / en | 0.667 / 0.50 | 0.667 / 0.80 | fail |
| 3. M1 `clarify_rate` <= 0.15 | 0.283 | 0.196 | fail |
| 3. M1 `wrong_card` point rate <= 0.03, pt-BR / en | 0.125 / 0.10 | 0.125 / 0.00 | fail |
| 4. Wilson upper bounds | not decidable at this n | not decidable at this n | n/a |
| 5. M2 `correct` >= recorded - 2, no `wrong_*` above recorded | 45 >= 44, none | 46 >= 46, `wrong_read` 1 > 0 | DeepSeek pass, Sonnet fail |
| 6. Daily route p95 <= 4,000 ms, median cost <= US$ 0.002 | 1,534 ms, US$ 0.00052 | n/a | pass |

M1 (46 items, worst of 2 runs): DeepSeek correct 27, clarify 13, wrong_card 6; Sonnet correct 31, clarify 9, wrong_card 6. M2 (50 items): DeepSeek correct 45, clarify 4, missed 1; Sonnet correct 46, clarify 3, wrong_read 1. The interpreter stays on only through BL1's dated entry ("served before its measurement"); nothing was edited.

**Why the golds are widened.** Every `wrong_card` of M1 is a correct operation with a correct span (a value with or without its article, its preposition, the family noun or a body's lead-in) that stands outside the gold list; the list was an incomplete enumeration of one implicit rule (BL3's own table lists "da Carla Menezes sobre ..." and not "Javier sobre ..."), and a card built from any of those spans shows the same message or event, because grounding drops those words. The stated reason is a defect of the golds, not a wish for a higher score.

**Rule W1** (`python3 scripts/interpreter_corpus.py widen-golds`, literals in that script, applied once to all 46 items): for every gold of outcome `op` and every span slot that is not a time slot, the accepted list becomes the union, over each listed alternative found in the line as whole words, of the alternative, every piece obtained by removing whole words at its left and/or right edge when each removed word is in the language's EDGE list (at least one word that is not an edge word must remain), and each of those with one article of the language added at the left when the line has it right before the piece; the v1 alternatives are always kept, every piece is a piece of the line, no content word is added or removed, no op, number, time, frame or line changes. The result is `evals/interpreter/dev-long-v2.jsonl`; `dev-long-v1.jsonl` stays frozen.

**The re-score, labelled beside the first result: re-score under golds v2 (rule W1), NOT BLIND, not a measurement, no evidence.** W1 was written after the v1 answers were read; it is uniform but not blind. It is `--replay` with the new `--golds FILE` (the recorded answers scored with that file's gold and also, same ids and every other key equal; `rescore: true`, the file and its sha256 in the summary; nothing written), 0 calls:

| M1, worst of 2 runs, 46 items | correct | clarify | wrong_card | `correct_direct` all / pt-BR / en | `clarify_rate` | `wrong_card` point pt-BR / en |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DeepSeek, golds v1 (published) | 27 | 13 | 6 | 0.587 / 0.667 / 0.50 | 0.283 | 0.125 / 0.10 |
| DeepSeek, golds v2 (re-score, W1, not blind) | 32 | 13 | 1 | 0.696 / 0.750 / 0.60 | 0.283 | 0.042 / 0.00 |
| Sonnet, golds v1 (published) | 31 | 9 | 6 | 0.674 / 0.667 / 0.80 | 0.196 | 0.125 / 0.00 |
| Sonnet, golds v2 (re-score, W1, not blind) | 37 | 9 | 0 | 0.804 / 0.792 / 0.80 | 0.196 | 0.00 / 0.00 |

M2 is unchanged by this package. Under golds v2 alone rules 2 and 3 still fail on both routes (`correct_direct` under 0.85, `clarify_rate` over 0.15, DeepSeek pt-BR `wrong_card` 0.042 over 0.03): this package fixes the measure of the span-boundary cases only; the clarify items belong to the correction after "Posso?" and to the double date. The one wrong card left (DeepSeek, `dev-pt-br-50007`, target "Carla Menezes") is a real loss of the subject words and W1 keeps it wrong.

**Why not a scorer change.** Scoring target spans by the words grounding uses would change `runtime/interpreter_score.py` and the `matching` text of `taxonomy.json`, so the taxonomy hash would change for every corpus, the held-out split included, and every earlier `ix-*` line and the "versus current" comparison of `thresholds.json` would be read by another rule: a measurement decision with a re-freeze, and a measure tied to one grounding implementation. A data file and a labelled re-score mode change nothing already measured.

**Committed.** The four recorded answers files under `evals/interpreter/replay/<route>/blite-20261025/M1|M2/` (an exact-path `english-only` line each), the four evidence files `evals/interpreter/evidence/ix-20261025T03*.jsonl` byte for byte, the catalog they were recorded with (`evals/interpreter/replay/catalogs/catalog-a77f8a8f.json`), and `runtime/tests/test_interpreter_blite_replay.py`, which keeps the published counts (read from the evidence, never re-scored) apart from the counts the current code gives the recorded answers, so later code changes can be replayed with no call; the pin `interpreter_prompt_5bee` names the prompt of that run for the test, so a later change of the prompt moves `interpreter_prompt` and not this test.

**Next.** The next M1 runs on `dev-long-v2.jsonl` (the first measurement whose golds precede its answers) and needs the maintainer's "pode"; it is reported beside the same answers re-scored with `--golds evals/interpreter/dev-long-v1.jsonl`.

**Principle 13.** Saved: no change to the scorer, the taxonomy, the thresholds or any hash a measurement reads; the re-score costs no call. Given up: the re-score is not blind, and the golds of v2 were written knowing the answers; both are said beside every number it produces.

## 2026-10-25: the double date of a change, the invented time of a removal, the `KeyError: 'spec'` crash and the written date read (B''-lite, BL5C, no model call, no lab run, fingerprint unchanged)

- **Five root causes** (SPEC-BLITE-5C; each reproduced offline by a test that failed first). C1a: `interpreter._classify` asked an `event-change` whose line held the event's current day ("tomorrow", "amanha") as well as the new time, because `_cover` wants the time span to hold every time phrase of the line; the old day is the other slot (`day`), not a doubt about the new time. C1b: `_cover` also wanted the trigger word the engine puts before a phrase ("para sexta", "for tmrw") inside the span, while the prompt tells the model to keep the words around a value out of it (also hit remind and event-create). C2: `_check_op` refused any `time_iso` on an `event-cancel` (E_SHAPE) and the runtime's rescue (`ops_interpret._event_slots_fixed`) then dropped the verified `target` and `day` too. C4: `event_lookup._day_named` indexed `named[0]["spec"]` for any single day hit, but a `slash` hit has no `spec`, and a date with a time range makes the engine answer with several readings: `KeyError`, no reply at all, from `open_event`, `_by_target`, `_clock_only`, `ops_items._range_args` (and so `plan_confirm`), `ops_interpret._day_read` and the card change. C5: `_by_target` read the day of a target from the model's `day` span alone, so a title word that looks like a day ("amanhas") lost the date written in the line ("dia 25/10").
- **The fix.** C1a: for `event-change`, a `day` span that is a date and nothing else, ends before a time span that holds a day of its own, does not lie inside the target, and is not doubted, is blanked in the line the time is classified against (`_without_old_day`); anything else leaves the line as it was, so the time is asked. C1b: a piece also covers when it holds the core of every hit and nothing outside the hits (`_cover`). C2: an `event-cancel`'s unasked `time_iso` and `weekday` are dropped, never read or kept (`TIME_DROPPED_OPS`); `_event_slots_fixed` keeps `target` and `day`. C3 (found on the way): a change or removal with a `day` and no `target`, `event` or inherited target is `clarify no-target`, so the measure agrees with the runtime's `IX_ASK_AGAIN`. C4: `_day_named` answers every shape (one reading, several readings on one date, a weekday, one slash date, anything else unread) and never raises. C5: with a `day` span the day of a target is read from the span and from the rest of the line (the line with the target, day, time and title spans cut out); a conflict is asked by the new `EV_ASK_WHICH_DAY`, which names both days (one form, the weekday's word and the date) and asks for the event and the day again, and opens nothing; a span that names no day takes only a date WRITTEN in the rest, never a day word; with no `day` span the rest is never read (the review of #562, F1: a line that excludes tomorrow's event, "cancela o dentista, mas nao o de amanha", opened the excluded event); a rest that names another object is ignored.
- **Kept.** L61: no write is opened with a guessed slot; two readings of a slot are asked back. The de and fr change items stay asked (the time engine has tables for pt-BR and en only): the refusal is correct, the golds still say `op`, the miss is reported. RETEST-V3-CRASH observation 2 (a day span code cannot read, no date written) stays a question.
- **Expected numbers (SPEC-BLITE-5C section 5, "replay of recorded answers, dev, not blind").** M1 with golds v2: DeepSeek 32/13/1 to 36/9/1 and Sonnet 37/9/0 to 40/6/0 (correct/clarify/wrong_card). Re-run after BL5A merged (the golds re-score of the recorded answers, 0 calls): the numbers above hold, M2 is unchanged, and this PR updates the replay literals of `test_interpreter_blite_replay.py`. `test_interpreter_replay_gate.py` passes unchanged.
- **Principle 13.** Saved: five items asked again and the invented-time refusals of a removal, with no new operation, no new store, no change to the prompt, the catalog or the measurement (fingerprint unchanged). Cost: a `day` span that follows the time span, a change whose time is a clock only ("para as 10h"), or a new day put in the `day` slot ("para amanha, as 10h") is still asked, a line with two different days gets a question where it used to get a card of the first one, and a removal's invented time is dropped instead of refused. The safety given up is none that the guard tests do not hold: the hidden-second-time probes (a clock in the day span, an overlap, a range, a third day word, a repeated span) are asserted asked.
- **Not done.** No model call, no lab run, no measurement.

## 2026-10-25: a "no" with a new detail after "Posso?" is the corrected request, a new question, never a run (B''-lite follow-up, BL5B, no model call, no lab run; the prompt is served before its measurement)

**Authority.** The maintainer, 2026-10-25 (translated): "Yes: make adjustments A (...), B (teach the correction after \"Posso?\") and C (...), and ask me for the go to measure again." The live retest of 2026-10-25: "mostra minha agenda de hoje" -> "Entendi: mostrar os eventos da sua agenda de dom 25/10/2026. Posso?" -> "Nao, a de amanha" -> the sentence that says the line was not understood (IX_ASK_AGAIN), and the plan was dropped.

- **Root cause.** (1) The prompt never named the plan-confirm item and its answer row invited `confirm` with a slot, so a line that starts with "no" read as an answer to the question. (2) `interpreter._check_answer` believes a `confirm` only when the line minus the answer's span is a bare yes or no, so both shapes the protocol allows ("no" alone; "no" with a slot and a span) ended in `clarify`. (3) Even a right op would have shown the wrong list: the kind of a list was read from the correction line alone ("Nao, a de amanha" has no calendar noun, so "mail").
- **The change.** Prompt: rule 9 and three worked examples (a cancel with another number, a list for another day, a search with another question); the new pin `interpreter_prompt` and a new dated enabling entry whose status line says "served before its measurement"; `interpreter_prompt_5bee` keeps the previous hash for the recorded answers. Code: `interpreter._plan_correction` turns a model's `confirm: no` on a `plan-confirm` item of the operation `frame.last` holds into a revised op (a slot of the plan's operation with a span: that slot, the other words inherited except a number or a time; or, for a list, a line that holds a day the time engine reads) checked like any op, marked `corrects`; `ops_interpret.turn` carries it out like any op, so the dispatcher asks a NEW "Posso?" (the old plan is gone), and answers PLAN_DROPPED when the revised call equals the plan's. A list call now carries its kind, account, range and count (`ops_items.items_args`, `natural_writes.items_noun`), so the plan's hash binds them, the question says what will be read and a correction keeps what the old line said and the new one does not (`extras["plan_previous"]`: the sender's own live plan).
- **A rule of BL2a changes.** Until now "a model's no to the plan item drops the plan" (`ops_interpret` module text, `test_plan_confirm_frame.py`). Now a bare no, or a no that changes nothing, drops it; a no that carries a new detail revises it into a NEW question. A model's yes never runs a plan and is never converted; nothing runs from a correction (only the person's yes, read by code, or the Yes button, runs a plan); a number is the one the correcting line writes, never carried from the old line.
- **Replay (0 calls).** The recorded B''-lite answers (`blite-20261025T003348`, dev-long-v1 golds, 92 runs per model on M1, 50 on M2) replayed through this code, against the same code with the conversion switched off: DeepSeek M1 clarify 23 -> 15 and correct 57 -> 65, Sonnet M1 clarify 17 -> 10 and correct 64 -> 71 (the four plan-confirm items, dev-pt-br-50016, dev-en-50008, dev-pt-br-50017, dev-pt-br-50023, in both runs; wrong_card unchanged at 12 and 11 under the v1 golds); M2 unchanged. These are replays of answers given to the OLD prompt: they show what the code does with those answers, not what the new prompt makes the models say. The effect of rule 9 is not measured.
- **Principle 13.** Saved: no new operation, no new store, no new group, no model call. Cost: one more question when a person corrects a plan (the same question they would have had after a clean request), a prompt of about 800 characters longer on every call, and a prompt not yet measured. The safety given up is none that the tests do not hold (nothing runs from a correction; a yes is never converted; no number is carried).
- **Review round (2026-10-25).** A no with a courtesy ("nao, obrigado", "no thanks": closed lists in `interpreter.py`) is a bare no and drops the plan, whatever slot and span the model gave the courtesy; a line inherits from the open plan (`plan_previous`) only when it opens with a no or is a `corrects` decision, so a new request of the same operation gets no invisible account or range.
- **Not enforced by code (stated, L65).** A no with extra words that is none of the two shapes (a plan whose operation has slots and a model that names none; a list correction with no day the engine reads, such as "a da semana que vem") is asked again and drops the plan.
- **Not done.** No model call, no measurement: M1 and M2 on both routes wait for the maintainer's "pode". The reads-off sender has no plan for a read to correct: a corrected read of theirs runs at once, as any new read line of theirs does.

## 2026-10-25: a phone names a mail or calendar source by its plain name, never by its id (BL5D, no model call, no lab run, fingerprint unchanged)

**Authority.** The maintainer, live retest on Telegram, 2026-10-25: "apague o e-mail do github" was answered "De qual caixa? 1. caixa-gmail 2. caixa-outlook"; he said "Nome das caixas devem ser algo como Gmail / Outlook", and then asked for the plain names to be built now, together with the other three fixes of the night (A, B and C).
- **Names are derived at drawing time, values do not change.** `runtime/source_names.py` names every mail and calendar source over the whole catalog, in the registry's order and the project's language: the provider's plain name ("Gmail", "Outlook", "Agenda do Google"); sources with the same name get their box, "principal" or the person's description (24 characters); what is still equal is numbered. The names of a project are unique by construction (a test enumerates every multiset of up to four sources). The option VALUE of a question stays the source id (or `<id>#<n>`), so a number, the id, the name or the provider's word ("google", "hotmail") selects exactly what it selected; a word that fits two options is asked again and opens nothing.
- **Where.** The questions and sentences that name a source (`ops_natural_writes.py`: `_ask`, the `say` branch, `_option`; the family modules are untouched), the phone list and the line for a source it could not read (`ops_items.py`; the English reason with ids and "refresh" no longer reaches a phone), the list of events of two calendars (`event_lookup.py`), and the phone form of a reply card ("3 do Gmail", from `cfg["source_names"]`, which the bridge hands to every card).
- **A mailbox name is the provider's brand, not a phrase.** The spec had `NW_ITEMS_NAME_MAIL` become `{service}`; a sentence that is only a field matches every text in `phone_errors.spoken`, so a pt-BR phone was handed English errors unchanged (14 tests failed). The key is gone; a mailbox is named by its brand in `source_names.plain`, a calendar keeps `NW_ITEMS_NAME_CAL`, and a test holds that no sentence of the table is only fields.
- **The account address is not used to tell sources apart.** There is one account per implementation on a machine (`sources_mail.py`), so two sources of one provider have the same address.
- **The stored card is untouched.** The phone line is drawn from a copy of the line's fields; the card, its hash and the effect document are the approval's (L55, L56). S16c still holds: the number differs and the names are unique, so two targets never read the same.
- **Left out, named.** The `/ask` answer over a snapshot ("Lido: caixa-gmail (version 3)" and the checked quote marker, a format only code may write); the typed operations' refusals (`ACCT_MBOX_REF_*`, which reach a phone only through `phone_errors.py`); model text that names a source; the folder keys of the configuration; the file source of a file card (the person's own label); the wording of `NW_MAIL_NO_FOLDERS`.
- **Principle 13.** Saved: a person is never asked to choose between ids and never reads an English sentence with an id in a pt-BR chat. Cost: one catalog read for each question or phone list on a messaging app (a registry read, no provider), and "Caixa do Gmail" / "Gmail inbox" become "Gmail" (one inbox per provider is the common case; twins are told apart by their box).
- **Not done.** No model call, no provider call, no lab run; the terminal, the page and the conversation show what they showed.

## 2026-10-25: the platform refreshes a stale mail snapshot for the line that needs it (BL5E, no model call, no provider call in the tests, no lab run, fingerprint unchanged)

**Authority.** The maintainer, live retest on Telegram, 2026-10-25: "apague o e-mail do github" ended in "refresh it and ask again", and he said the platform must refresh the mailbox by itself and not wait for the person to do it in the terminal; the design (mirror the calendar's exception) was approved the same day.

- **The triggers.** `item_refs.read_index` refuses with `stale` (older than 26 hours, or a time that cannot be trusted), `no_version` or `no_index`; those three, and only those, are cured by a read. `integrity`, `not_readable` and every other refusal keep today's sentence.
- **Where.** `runtime/mail_refresh.py` `refresh_once`, called by `ops_natural_mail._read_snapshot` for a line that found its message by words or asked for the newest; the read is `ops_sources.source_refresh` (the origin's own box, days and max), through a new `timeout` keyword that only this caller passes.
- **Why not on a number or a written reference.** A refresh renumbers the snapshot: the number the person saw would then name another message. Such a line keeps the sentence that says to refresh.
- **Why messaging only.** The terminal, the page and the conversation have the refresh command and the button, and show the id and the age; nothing changes for them (`refresh_once` answers `not-allowed` for any channel but messaging). The sender needs `ask-mail`, the group the lookup already needs: no new group, nothing widened.
- **Why the budget lives in `resolve`.** The bridge reaches the mail family through `ops.natural_write` and not through `ops_say.say`, so a per-line hook in `say` would miss it. Every mail-family line resolves once (`ops_natural_writes.advance`), so one turn budget opened in `resolve` is one provider read for a line; with no turn open, no read (fail closed).
- **The numbers.** 60 seconds for the provider and for the wait on a source's lock, 120 seconds of rest after a failure for that source: the supervisor's reversible choices (constants `TURN_TIMEOUT_S`, `FAIL_COOLDOWN_S`). One lock for each source: a second line waits, then finds the snapshot fresh; a line that waited for a source that then failed does not try again inside the rest.
- **A failure.** One sentence (`NW_MAIL_REFRESH_FAILED`), no card, no list; stderr gets one line with a closed word (`mail: refresh of <id> failed (<word>)`), never the provider's text.
- **Limits.** L52 and L53 name the new exception (`mail_refresh.refresh_once`), dated 2026-10-25; the L52 static test is unchanged.
- **Review round (2026-10-25).** (1) A number chosen from a list: the draft keeps the snapshot version the list was drawn from (`_listed`, through a `keep` the family asks `ops_natural_writes._ask` to store: one line there), and the answer is refused with `NW_MAIL_LIST_CHANGED` when the newest version differs (another line's refresh, a list on a phone, a typed refresh or a routine); the same hole existed before, the refresh widened it. (2) A read that works but leaves the snapshot still refused rests the source like a failure (`(unchanged)` on stderr). (3) The limit text says "by its words, or asked for as the newest". (4) The worst case of one line is about 120 seconds: 60 for the lock of a source another line reads, 60 for its own read.
- **Left to the maintainer.** OPEN-E1: one refresh on a miss too (a snapshot younger than 26 hours that lacks this morning's mail; the calendar does it). OPEN-E2: the page's conversation. Until he decides, a fresh-but-old snapshot is searched as it is, and the page keeps its sentence.
- **Principle 13.** Saved: no new operation, group, store or phrase beyond one sentence; no change to the interpreter, its frame or the measurement. Given up: the refresh costs one provider read of up to 60 seconds in the line that triggers it, for a sender who asked for it.
- **Not done.** No model call, no real provider call, no lab run.

## 2026-10-25: the second B''-lite measurement published as it was, passed or not, and its answers and evidence committed (BL5F, no model call, no lab run, fingerprint unchanged)

M1: the 46 long lines of `dev-long-v2.jsonl`, 2 runs per item. M2: the 50-item draw of `dev.jsonl`, seed 20261025, 1 run. 0 errors, 0 parse failures, 0 private-route leaks. Metered cost US$ 0.080 for DeepSeek; Sonnet ran on the maintainer's account.
- **Not blind.** M1's golds (v2, rule W1) were written after the first answers were seen, and fixes B and C were made looking at these 46 lines. The M1 numbers below say how the fixes did on lines they were shaped on, not how a stranger's lines would do. A new blind long set is recommended before the release.
- **The pass rules (SPEC-BLITE-3 section 5), worst of the runs of each item.**
  | Rule | DeepSeek | Sonnet | Verdict | First measurement |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 1. no wrong effect unconfirmed (hard) | 0 in M1 and M2 | 0 in M1 and M2 | pass | pass |
  | 2. M1 correct_direct at least 0.85, pt-BR and en | 0.875 and 1.00 | 0.958 and 1.00 | pass | 0.667 and 0.50 (DeepSeek); 0.667 and 0.80 (Sonnet) |
  | 3. M1 clarify rate at most 0.15 | 0.087 | 0.043 | pass | 0.28 and 0.20 |
  | 3. M1 wrong_card point rate at most 0.03, pt-BR and en | 0.042 and 0.00 | 0.042 and 0.00 | fail in pt-BR on both routes (1 of 24 items each) | 0.125 and 0.10; 0.125 and 0.00 |
  | 4. Wilson upper bounds | not decidable at this n | not decidable | none | none |
  | 5. M2 correct at least the recorded value minus 2, no wrong_* above the recorded | 46, none | 46, wrong_read 1 against 0 | DeepSeek pass, Sonnet fail (same item as before) | same |
  | 6. daily route p95 at most 4,000 ms, median cost at most US$ 0.002 | 1,516 ms and US$ 0.00058 | not applicable | pass | pass |

M2 (50 items): DeepSeek 46 correct, 4 clarify; Sonnet 46 correct, 3 clarify, 1 wrong_read. The replay of the current code on the recorded answers, at 0 calls, gives the same counts.
- **What still fails.** DeepSeek, `dev-pt-br-50006` (both runs): "renomeia o evento treino funcional de quarta..." read with the day kept inside the target ("treino funcional de quarta") and no day slot; W1 does not move a day out of a target. Sonnet, `dev-pt-br-50007` (one run of two): target "Carla Menezes" for a line about an e-mail of hers on a contract, a real loss of the subject words. Sonnet M2, `dev-de-00003`: "recherchier mal..." read as `research` (with D5 on, research asks first, so not an unconfirmed effect; the same item as the first measurement). Clarify left, in the safe direction: `de-50001` and `fr-50001` (no time table for German and French, a correct refusal) and two DeepSeek Portuguese items in one run.
- **Verdict as recorded.** The hard rule and four of the five numeric rules pass on both routes; two single items stay under the gate: the pt-BR wrong-card point rate (1 of 24 items, 4.2% against 3%) on both routes, and Sonnet's M2 wrong_read. The interpreter stays on by the dated entries ("served before its measurement"). Nothing here edits a threshold or a gold.
- **What is committed.** Four answers files under `evals/interpreter/replay/<route>/blite2-20261025/M1|M2/`, four evidence files under `evals/interpreter/evidence/` (named by UTC date, `ix-20261026T...`), exact-path `english-only` lines for the answers, the pin `interpreter_prompt_1423`, and `runtime/tests/test_interpreter_blite2_replay.py`, which reads the published counts from the evidence and replays the answers against the recorded catalog (published and replay counts kept apart).

## 2026-10-26: the blind long-set measurement published as it was, passed or not, with its set, answers and evidence (BL7-0, no model call, no lab run, fingerprint unchanged)

- **Accepted.** The maintainer decided on 2026-10-26 that the spent blind set and its answers get published; principle 10 says a result is published whether it passed or not. This entry commits the set, its README and its checker's findings, the recorded answers and the evidence of the run, as BL5A and BL5F did for the earlier long-set measurements. It changes no code, no threshold and no catalog, and moves no band.
- **The run.** `440b4d2`, catalog `a77f8a8f...`, prompt `1423ba89...`, frozen `thresholds.json`. The set is `blite-blind-v2.jsonl` (sha256 `984356db...`), 60 fictional long lines (pt-BR 30, en 15, es 4, fr 4, de 4, it 3) written by an isolated author session that had not seen any earlier long set or result, with golds checked by a second isolated reviewer; v2 is v1 with three alternatives added to its golds before any model read it. 2 runs per route, 120 calls each, DeepSeek and Sonnet (on the maintainer's account). 0 errors, 0 parse failures, 0 private-route leaks.
- **The pass rules (SPEC-BLITE-3 section 5), per item, worst of the 2 runs.**
  | Rule | DeepSeek | Sonnet |
  |---|---|---|
  | 1. no wrong effect unconfirmed (hard) | pass (0) | pass (0) |
  | 2. correct_direct at least 0.85, pt-BR and en | 0.857 pass and 0.786 fail | 0.893 pass and 0.929 pass |
  | 3. clarify rate at most 0.15 | 0.123 pass | 0.035 pass |
  | 3. wrong_card point rate at most 0.03, pt-BR and en | 0.033 fail (1 of 30) and 0.133 fail (2 of 15) | 0.067 fail (2 of 30) and 0.000 pass |

- **This is the blind number.** The set is now spent. Every later replay of these answers, by code written after reading them, is a dev re-score and is labelled so; the published counts stay as they are. A new blind set is needed to say anything about code or a prompt changed after this run.

Three path edits were made for principle 8 (a builder's scratch path, the measuring commands' paths, the checker's path to v1) and are listed in the README's last section; no item, gold or line was changed. `evals/interpreter_status.py` lists the two events under `heldout`, not in a band.
- **Not done.** No model call, no lab run, no re-score of the answers under other golds. The derived summaries, the stderr notes and the builder scripts stay outside the repository.

## 2026-10-26: the check keeps the subject of a message, drops an inherit the line fills, and keeps a corrected read a read (BL7-1, no model call, no lab run, fingerprint unchanged)

- **Accepted.** The maintainer, 2026-10-26: a package for the product failures of the blind measurement (the subject of the e-mail, the correction with the old value, the correction that became another act), in parallel with the Section 4.1 packages. Code only: `runtime/interpreter.py`; the prompt is untouched (its pinned hash is unchanged).
- **Three root causes, each found by reading the blind answers item by item.**
  1. A reply's `target` that the model gave as a person only ("Gideon Farrow") lost the subject the same line gave ("about the warehouse lease"): the check believed any span of the line, so grounding chose by sender alone (a list, or the wrong one of the sender's messages). DeepSeek `test-en-60005` and `test-pt-br-60006` (both runs), and the older `dev-pt-br-50007` on both routes.
  2. A model that put the new value in `slots` and the old one in `inherit` had the whole decision refused (`E_INHERIT`, asked again): DeepSeek `test-pt-br-60019` (both runs) and `test-pt-br-60020` run 2.
  3. Nothing tied a correction of a READ plan to that read: DeepSeek `test-en-60013` ("nope, I meant my unread emails from this morning, not the calendar") became a `mail-mark` card.
- **The rules.** (1) `interpreter._target_with_subject`: for the mail operations that name a message by `target`, a target that stands once in the line and is followed after white space by a subject marker (a closed list, six languages) is widened to the end of the subject: the tail stops at a stop word, a date written as numbers, a word that ends in punctuation (taken without it), the start of another slot's span or of a time phrase, loses the function words at its end, holds 1 to 6 words, is at most `SPAN_MAX`, passes the same span check as any slot, and must add a content word that names a message (`natural_writes_mail.target_words`). The result is always the target or a piece of the line that starts with it: no word comes from a message, an index or the model. `_check_op` checks the replaced slots again, so a helper that returned a word the line lacks is `E_SPAN`. The reply still goes to the index's From (L60); a subject word the index lacks makes grounding answer `NW_MAIL_NONE`, and several matches are still a list. (2) An `inherit` of a slot `slots` also holds is dropped before anything reads it (an unknown slot is still `E_SLOT_UNKNOWN`, first; an inherited time slot, or one the line does not fill, is checked as before). (3) `interpreter._read_plan_kept`: a line that opens with a no, under a live `plan-confirm` item of a class R operation that is the sender's last request, for which the model named a class E card of another operation, and which holds no head of a write family (`natural_writes_<family>.HEADS`, read, not copied), is the plan's own read with the model's slots that the read has (none for a list), marked `corrects`: asked as a NEW plan, nothing runs (L65). A W, N or D operation, a plan that is not a read, a line without the no, a line with a write head, and a slot the read lacks keep today's path.
- **Replay of the recorded blind answers, labelled "dev re-score of the spent blind answers, code written after reading them: not a blind number".** The published blind result stays as it was. Worst of 2 runs per item, 60 items:
  | Route | Before (published) | After BL7-1 |
  |---|---|---|
  | DeepSeek | correct 48, clarify 7, wrong_card 3, wrong_read 1, wrong_route 1; correct_direct 0.807 (pt-BR 0.857, en 0.786); clarify_rate 0.123 | correct 53, clarify 5, wrong_card 0, wrong_read 1, wrong_route 1; correct_direct 0.895 (pt-BR 0.964, en 0.929); clarify_rate 0.088 |
  | Sonnet | correct 54, clarify 2, wrong_card 2, wrong_read 2; 0.895 (0.893, 0.929) | unchanged (no item moves) |
  By gap, DeepSeek: gap 1 alone, correct 50 and wrong_card 1; plus gap 3, correct 52; plus gap 4, correct 53. Regression replays of the committed answers: only `dev-pt-br-50007` moves (blite M1 DeepSeek run 1 and blite2 M1 Sonnet run 2, wrong_card to correct); blite M2, blite2 M1 and M2 otherwise, and `test-full` (both routes) are unchanged item by item. The replay literals of `test_interpreter_blite_replay.py` and `test_interpreter_blite2_replay.py` moved by that one item, with the reason in a comment; the published literals did not.
- **Product truth the measure does not show.** (a) `test-pt-br-60019` ("a 8 deixa rodar...; para a 9"): the decision becomes `stop-task` 9, but the line writes two numbers, so the vet of decision numbers (`command_words.vet`, L54) asks "which number?" (`NAT_ASK_ID`) instead of `IX_ASK_AGAIN`. Kept on purpose: a decision number is only ever the one the line writes, exactly one. (b) The two blind lines of gap 4 name both the e-mails and the calendar ("not the calendar"); `natural_writes.items_noun` reads a line that names both as "calendar", so in the product the corrected read equals the plan and is dropped (`PLAN_DROPPED`): nothing runs and no card of another act opens, which is safe, but it is not the new question a line that names only the e-mails gets (both are guarded). Left for the owner of `items_noun`: a noun after a negation could be ignored.
- **Not done.** No model call, no lab run, no new measurement: the blind set is spent, and only a new blind set can say whether these numbers hold. The whole suite was not run (the touched tests only).
